PART 303-308 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PBR MATERIAL PRESETS
================================================================================

Image-driven factories choose PBR presets by mapping the image's
visual cues to the 14 preset archetypes. PART 303-308 here is the
image-driven form of the 14 presets, with a "image → preset"
mapping table.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 303 — 5 BASELINE PRESETS (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 303 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes. The
  image-driven additions:

    - Matte plastic:        if image shows rough, non-reflective surfaces
    - Glossy plastic:       if image shows shiny plastic (e.g. vehicle fairings)
    - Brushed metal:        if image shows metallic grooves / directional grain
    - Chrome:               if image shows mirror-finish reflections
    - Anodized metal:       if image shows premium colored metal

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 304 — 3 CHARACTER PRESETS (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 304 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 305 — 2 SPECIALTY PRESETS (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 305 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

  - Glass quality:        if image shows transparent surfaces
                         (windows, glass jars, screens)
  - Glow quality:        if image shows emissive parts
                         (headlights, screens, lava, magic)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 306 — 2 CREATURE PRESETS (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 306 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 307 — 2 ENVIRONMENT PRESETS (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 307 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

  Image-driven specific:
    - Stone/concrete: if image shows masonry / concrete / brick
    - Wood:           if image shows wood grain (auto-detect
                      via Sobel direction analysis)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 308 — IMAGE → PRESET MAPPING TABLE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image cue → Preset mapping (use this to auto-pick the preset):

    Image visual cue                          Preset
    ────────────────────────                  ─────
    Saturated primary color, no grain         matte plastic
    High gloss + saturated color              glossy plastic
    Visible metal grain (parallel lines)      brushed metal
    Mirror reflections, no diffuse            chrome
    Saturated metal, premium look             anodized metal
    Black with white highlights (eyes)       eyes (3 mats)
    Long parallel strands (fur, hair)        hair/fur
    Soft surface, subsurface hint             skin
    Transparent with refraction              glass
    Bright glow, internal light source        glow
    Iridescent surface (rainbow shift)        chitin/elytra
    Soft pile, slight sheen                  velvet
    Stone / brick / masonry                  stone/concrete
    Wood grain (linear striations)           wood

  Auto-detect:
       from js import lblSpec
       preset = lblSpec.helpers.detectPreset(ref_img)
       # Returns the most likely preset name + confidence.

================================================================================
PART 200-219 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON CONTRACT (FULL MIRROR)
================================================================================

This section is the IMAGE-DRIVEN MIRROR of PART 200-219 in
Prompt_To_Py.txt. The original v1.36 / v8.27 release put the
Python-specific contract in Prompt_To_Py.txt and had Image_To_Py.txt
cross-reference it. v1.36 / v8.27 closes that gap — image-driven
developers should be able to read THIS file alone and know everything
they need about Python factories.

Every PART in this section is functionally identical to PART
200-219 in Prompt_To_Py.txt, with image-driven emphasis on the
sections that differ (Style A vs. Style B for image work,
PART 221-225 image reading patterns, etc.).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 200 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON OUTPUT CONTRACT
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The image-driven Python factory has the same output contract as a
  prompt-driven Python factory (PART 200 in Prompt_To_Py.txt) — the
  factory returns either:

    (a) A blueprint dict (Style A): `lbl.blueprint()`
    (b) A THREE.Group (Style B): `THREE.Group.new()` with children

  The DIFFERENCE for image-driven factories is that the factory
  MUST ALSO accept a `ref_img` argument when the reference image is
  available. The renderer wires this in automatically:

       def create_model(ref_img=None, options=None):
           """Image-driven Python factory.

           ref_img: the reference image (HTMLImageElement) when
                    image-driven mode is active; None for prompt-only.
           options: future per-load options (currently always None).
           """
           if ref_img is not None:
               # Image-driven mode
               palette = sample_image_palette(ref_img)
               dimensions = measure_dimensions(ref_img)
           else:
               # Prompt-only mode
               palette = DEFAULT_PALETTE
               dimensions = DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS
           # ... build the model using palette + dimensions ...
           return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 201 — IMAGE-DRIVEN TWO AUTHORING STYLES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Style A (Blueprint-Return) and Style B (Three.js Direct) for
  image-driven work. Style A is recommended for ~80% of images
  (most reference images map cleanly to the primitive vocabulary).
  Style B is needed when the image shows non-primitive shapes.

  Style A (recommended for simple images):
       def create_model(ref_img=None):
           lbl.name("My Model")
           lbl.define_palette(sample_image_palette(ref_img))
           # ... lbl.attach_* calls ...
           return lbl.blueprint()

  Style B (recommended for complex images):
       def create_model(ref_img=None):
           from js import THREE, MT_core
           g = THREE.Group.new()
           # ... THREE.* + MT_core.* calls ...
           return g

  Decision rule (image-driven):
      Image shows only primitives → Style A
      Image shows any of: curved fairings, sculpted organic,
        articulated joints, non-primitive characters, skeletal
        subjects, transmission/clearcoat/iridescence
        → Style B

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 202 — IMAGE-DRIVEN lbl HELPER MODULE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The `lbl` Python helper module, with image-driven emphasis on
  the parts that differ. See PART 202 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the
  full reference.

  Image-driven specific:
    - lbl.attach_box() — pass image-derived footprint_size
    - lbl.define_palette() — pass image-derived palette
    - lbl.create_anchor() — pass imageToWorld coordinates
    - lbl.blueprint() — same as prompt-driven

  Apply via:
       lbl.name("My Model")
       lbl.define_palette(sample_image_palette(ref_img))
       lbl.attach_box("body", footprint_size=(1.0, 0.8), length=1.0,
                      position=(0, 0.5, 0), material="shell")
       return lbl.blueprint()

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 203 — IMAGE-DRIVEN from js import THREE BRIDGE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Same as PART 203 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven factories
  reach for `from js import THREE` more often than prompt-driven
  ones (Style B is more common in image-driven work).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 204-208 — IMAGE-DRIVEN BASIC PYTHON ADAPTATIONS
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 204 (geometry choices), PART 205 (PBR materials vocabulary),
  PART 206 (palette discipline), PART 207 (sculptRuntime contract),
  PART 208 (tick function) — all functionally identical to the
  prompt-driven versions. See Prompt_To_Py.txt PART 204-208 for the
  full reference.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 209 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PROCEDURAL CANVAS TEXTURES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Same as PART 209 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven factories
  often use procedural textures to add detail that the reference
  image doesn't show explicitly. See PART 296-302 below for the
  full image-driven procedural texture forms.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 210-214 — IMAGE-DRIVEN VALIDATION + WORKED EXAMPLE + COMMON MISTAKES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 210 (validation checklist) — same as prompt-driven.

  PART 211 (minimal worked example — Style A) — see PART 226
  below for the image-driven Style A worked example.

  PART 212 (advanced worked example — Style B) — see PART 276
  in this file for the image-driven Style B worked example.

  PART 213 (common mistakes) — see PART 309-315 in this file for
  the image-driven specific mistakes.

  PART 214 (pre-output checklist) — same as prompt-driven.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 215-219 — IMAGE-DRIVEN CROSS-REFERENCES + DIFFERENCES + WHEN TO USE
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 215 (cross-references to other spec files) — image-driven
  factories cross-reference:
    - Prompt_To_Py.txt for the basic Python contract
    - Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the canonical methodology
    - Image_To_Ts.txt for the image-driven TS contract
    - public/modeling/ for the MT bundle implementation

  PART 216 (differences from TS path) — image-driven Python
  factories differ from TS in:
    - Pyodide load cost (10 MB compressed, 2-5s first time)
    - 1.5-2x slower than TS for image-driven procedural models
    - Single-threaded (no parallelism for the 3-variant ranking)

  PART 217 (when to reach for Python vs. JS blueprint vs. TS factory
  vs. image-driven) — image-driven factories:
    - Image-driven + simple primitives → Python Style A
    - Image-driven + complex shapes → Python Style B
    - Image-driven + skeletal subjects → TS (preferred) or Python B
    - Image-driven + huge photogrammetry meshes → TS or Python with
      MT_highpoly.importExternalMesh

  PART 218 (runtime error reference) — same as prompt-driven.
  Image-driven factories face the same Pyodide + MT bundle errors.

  PART 219 (file metadata + companion files) — image-driven
  factories use:
    - public/models/Model_10.py — the shipped worked example
    - public/modeling/lbl_py.py — the standalone lbl helper
    - public/modeling/mt-texture-extensions.js — for procedural
      textures (PART 230-234)
    - public/modeling/mt-highpoly.js — for high-poly meshes
      (PART 235-242)

PART 230-234 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PROCEDURAL TEXTURE EXTENSIONS
================================================================================

The 5 procedural texture helpers (PART 230-234 in Prompt_To_Py.txt)
are particularly useful for image-driven work. PART 230-234 here is
the IMAGE-DRIVEN form, with image-derived seed/parameter examples.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 230 — lbl.style_field (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  `lbl.style_field()` for image-driven work — derive the style
  hint from the image:

       from js import lblSpec

       def style_field_from_image(ref_img):
           """Derive a style hint from the reference image."""
           sf = lblSpec.helpers.imageToStyle(ref_img)
           # sf = {
           #   "mood": "metallic" / "matte" / "glossy" / "iridescent",
           #   "brightness": 0..1,
           #   "saturation": 0..1,
           #   "keyColors": ["#cc4444", "#4a6fa5", "#ffaa00"],
           #   "matchedKeywords": ["metal", "rubber", "glass"],
           # }
           return sf

       # Use to auto-set meta.style:
       sf = style_field_from_image(ref_img)
       bp["meta"]["style"] = (
           "pbr-realistic" if sf["mood"] == "metallic" else
           "chunky-mascot" if sf["mood"] == "matte" else
           "smooth-low-poly"
       )

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 231 — lbl.multi_scale_pattern (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-aligned multi-scale pattern. The seed and warp direction
  come from the image:

       from js import lbl

       def multi_scale_pattern_from_image(ref_img):
           # Sample the image's gradient direction
           from js import lblSpec
           grad = lblSpec.helpers.imageGradient(ref_img)
           seed = lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img)
           return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
               {"type": "noise", "seed": seed, "octaves": 4,
                "warpDirection": grad["dominant_direction"]},
               size=512,
           )

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 232 — lbl.cavity_aware_pattern (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-derived cavity-aware pattern. The curvature comes from the
  image's edge map:

       def cavity_aware_from_image(ref_img, geom):
           from js import lblSpec
           edges = lblSpec.helpers.imageEdges(ref_img)
           curvature_strength = edges["density"]  # 0..1
           seed = lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img)
           return lbl.cavity_aware_pattern(
               {"type": "noise", "seed": seed},
               geom, size=256,
               curvatureMode="both", curvatureStrength=curvature_strength,
           )

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 233 — lbl.geodesic_field (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-aligned geodesic field. The anchor points come from
  prominent features in the image:

       def geodesic_field_from_image(ref_img, geom):
           from js import lblSpec
           anchors_2d = lblSpec.helpers.detectFeatures(ref_img)
           # anchors_2d = [{"x": 0.5, "y": 0.2, "strength": 0.8}, ...]
           # Convert to 3D world coordinates
           anchors_3d = [
               lblSpec.helpers.imageToWorld([a["x"], a["y"]],
                                            ref_img, [0, 0, 0], [0, 0, 1])
               for a in anchors_2d
           ]
           return lbl.geodesic_field(geom, anchors_3d)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 234 — lbl.progressive_uv (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-aligned progressive UV. The face order is determined by
  which faces are most visible in the reference image:

       def progressive_uv_from_image(ref_img, geom):
           from js import lblSpec
           face_order = lblSpec.helpers.imageFaceOrder(ref_img, geom)
           # face_order is a list of face indices sorted by visibility
           return lbl.progressive_uv(
               geom, cameraDir=[0, 0, 1], method="planar",
               axis="y", atlasSize=1024,
               customFaceOrder=face_order,
           )

PART 235-242 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN HIGH-POLY / STYLE-AGNOSTIC SURFACE
================================================================================

The 8 high-poly helpers (PART 235-242 in Prompt_To_Py.txt) are
essential for image-driven work because the reference image is
often a high-poly photograph or scan. PART 235-242 here is the
IMAGE-DRIVEN form.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 235 — lbl.import_external_mesh (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven import — load the source mesh that produced the
  reference image (when available):

       from js import lbl
       import asyncio

       async def import_source_mesh(ref_img):
           """Try to import the source mesh from a sibling file."""
           # Check if the reference image has a sibling .glb/.obj
           url = ref_img.src.replace(/\.(png|jpg)$/, ".glb")
           if exists(url):
               return await asyncio.ensure_future(
                   lbl.import_external_mesh(url, format="glb")
               )
           return None  # No source mesh available

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 236 — lbl.make_atlas_uv (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven atlas UV — pack UVs based on the image's texture
  density requirements:

       def make_atlas_uv_from_image(ref_img, geom):
           from js import lblSpec
           density_map = lblSpec.helpers.imageTextureDensity(ref_img)
           # Use the density map to inform atlasSize
           atlas_size = 2048 if density_map["max"] > 0.7 else 1024
           return lbl.make_atlas_uv(geom, atlasSize=atlas_size, padding=4)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 237 — lbl.fast_curvature (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven fast curvature — derive curvature from the image's
  edge map (no need for BVH on the mesh):

       def fast_curvature_from_image(ref_img, geom):
           from js import lblSpec
           # For image-driven work, you can derive curvature
           # from the image's edge map (faster than mesh-based)
           return lblSpec.helpers.edgeMapToCurvature(ref_img, geom)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 238-239 — lbl.highpoly_pbr / lbl.photogrammetry_pbr
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven high-poly PBR materials. Use these when the
  reference image is a high-poly photograph:

       from js import lbl

       # High-poly PBR for photogrammetry-style images
       mat = lbl.highpoly_pbr(color=0x885533, roughness=0.4)
       # MeshPhysicalMaterial: roughness 0.45, metalness 0.05,
       # clearcoat 0.3, envMapIntensity 1.0

       # Photogrammetry PBR for scanned-mesh images
       mat = lbl.photogrammetry_pbr()
       # MeshStandardMaterial: roughness 0.85, metalness 0.0,
       # envMapIntensity 0.5 — matte + strong normal-map

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 240-241 — lbl.subdivide_for_highpoly / lbl.decimate_for_lowpoly
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven round-trip — convert the high-poly source to
  chunky-mascot output:

       async def image_to_chunky(ref_img, target_tris=3000):
           """Convert a high-poly reference image to a chunky model."""
           source_mesh = await import_source_mesh(ref_img)
           if source_mesh is None:
               return None
           # Subdivide if too low-poly
           if source_mesh.attributes["polygons"] < target_tris:
               source_mesh = lbl.subdivide_for_highpoly(
                   source_mesh, {"levels": 2},
               )
           # Decimate to the target tri count
           chunky = lbl.decimate_for_lowpoly(
               source_mesh, {"ratio": target_tris / source_mesh.attributes["polygons"]},
           )
           return chunky

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 242 — lbl.make_image_texture (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven texture loading — wrap the reference image itself as
  a UV texture:

       from js import lbl
       import asyncio

       async def use_image_as_texture(ref_img):
           tex = await asyncio.ensure_future(
               lbl.make_image_texture(ref_img.src)
           )
           # Apply to a material
           mat = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
               "color": 0xffffff,
               "map": tex,
           })
           return mat

================================================================================
PART 243-274 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN FULL METHODOLOGY LAYERS
================================================================================

This section is the IMAGE-DRIVEN MIRROR of PART 243-274 in
Prompt_To_Py.txt. Every PART in this section covers the same
technique but with image-driven specific notes, examples, and
emphasis. Image-driven factories should be able to use this file
alone without needing to cross-reference the prompt-driven mirror.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 243 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 8-STEP PIPELINE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 8-step pipeline from PART 145, with image-driven emphasis:

    Step 1. ANALYZE        Read the reference image. List the
                            major volumes. Sample the palette.
                            Measure the proportions.
    Step 2. SKELETON-FIRST Build a Group with placeholder meshes
                            at the major volume positions.
    Step 3. PROFILES       Define cross-section profiles from
                            the image's tapered surfaces.
    Step 4. LOFT           Pass each profile to MT_core.loft.
    Step 5. ACCESSORIES    Add small detail parts (rivets, decals).
    Step 6. UNIFORM BEVEL  Apply RoundedBoxGeometry consistently.
    Step 7. RIG + VALIDATE Build sculptRuntime, run IoU check.
    Step 8. ANIMATE        Add tick if the model has motion.

  Image-driven factories differ from prompt-driven in step 1:
  the image is the design source, not the text. Use the image-
  reading patterns from PART 221-225.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 244 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 8 SHAPE-CREATION TECHNIQUES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 8 shape-creation techniques from PART 146, with image-driven
  emphasis:

    1. PROFILE LOFTING   - MT_core.loft (most common for image-
                           driven limbs/tapers)
    2. LATHE             - THREE.LatheGeometry (image-aligned rims)
    3. EXTRUDE            - THREE.ExtrudeGeometry (most common for
                           image-driven fairings/panels)
    4. CSG               - MT_meshes.csg_union (use sparingly)
    5. SDF + MC          - MT_core.sdfUnion + sdfMarch (image-
                           driven organic merging)
    6. SUBDIVISION       - MT_meshes.subdivide (for high-poly sources)
    7. NURBS             - via MT_hardsurface.createBezierExtrude
    8. PROCEDURAL DEF.   - MT_meshes.bend/taper/twist (use sparingly)

  Image-driven factories use ExtrudeGeometry MORE than prompt-
  driven ones (because most reference images show panel-style
  shapes that map cleanly to ExtrudeGeometry).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 245 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 5 MATERIAL & SURFACE TECHNIQUES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 245 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - Sample the image's color palette for `color` / `baseColor`
    - Use the image's specular highlights to drive `roughness`
    - Sample the image's metallic cues for `metalness`

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 246 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 6 RIGGING TECHNIQUES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 246 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - Pivot positions derive from the image's geometry (NOT
      from eyeballed magic numbers)
    - Hierarchy follows the image's anatomy
    - Naming follows the image's visible parts

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 247 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 6 ANIMATION TECHNIQUES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 247 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven specific:
    - The reference image is a STILL frame. Animation must be
      inferred from context (a walking pose, a flying pose, etc.)
    - Phase-offset for walk cycles: assume the canonical pattern
      unless the image shows otherwise

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 248 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 6 VALIDATION & QA TECHNIQUES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 248 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - Silhouette test is MANDATORY (compared against reference)
    - IoU check is mandatory (PART 254)
    - Color match check is mandatory (sample image vs model)
    - Triangle budget can be guided by reference complexity

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 249 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 10 NAMED PITFALLS
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 249 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - P1 STACKED CYLINDERS — visible in image as segmented look
    - P2 FLOATING JOINTS — visible in image as gaps
    - P3 SMOOTH-PLASTIC LOOK — visible in image as flat shading
    - P4 FLOATING PRIMITIVES — visible in image as visible gaps
    - P5 MISSING TICKS — image doesn't show motion
    - P6 WRONG SCALE — IoU drops
    - P7 BAD PALETTE NAMING — not picked up by IoU but by validator
    - P8-P10 — same as prompt-driven

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 250-254 — IMAGE-DRIVEN FLOATING-PARTS FIX (MIRROR)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 250-254 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - PART 254 IoU check is MANDATORY for image-driven mode
    - PART 254 retry loop is the canonical response to IoU < 0.80
    - PART 250-253 apply with same priority

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 255-259 — IMAGE-DRIVEN VEHICLE PIPELINE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 255-259 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - Use the image's wheelbase / track / length for dimensions
      (lblSpec.helpers.imageToWorld converts pixel → world)
    - Lathed tires (PART 256.3) for ALL wheels — flat cylinders
      fail E41
    - Curved fairings via ExtrudeGeometry (PART 256.5)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 260-264 — IMAGE-DRIVEN ARTIST-FIRST GEOMETRY
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 260-264 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - Hierarchy of Fidelity applies with image-specific priority:
      the reference image is the GROUND TRUTH
    - Rule 167.1.1 Form Preservation Mandate: when porting an
      image-driven TS factory to Python, the form must be
      preserved exactly. Don't replace ExtrudeGeometry with
      BoxGeometry just because BoxGeometry is shorter — the
      reference image is the source of truth

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 265-269 — IMAGE-DRIVEN SPORT MOTORCYCLE REFERENCE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 265-269 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - Image-driven bike factories should match the reference
      image's proportions exactly (imageToWorld for dimensions)
    - 5-field style pair (PART 269) should be derived from
      the image (sport-motorcycle / vehicle / etc.)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 270-274 — IMAGE-DRIVEN ARCHITECTURAL PIPELINE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 270-274 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - Wall apertures derived from the image's door/window
      positions (lblSpec.helpers.detectApertures)
    - Roof pitch derived from the image's roof angle
    - STACK_INDEX from image's depth cues

================================================================================
PART 281-285 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN MT BUNDLE COVERAGE
================================================================================

The 44-helper MT bundle (PART 281-285 in Prompt_To_Py.txt) is the
workhorse for image-driven Python factories. This section is the
IMAGE-DRIVEN MIRROR, with image-driven focus.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 281 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MT.core (12 CORE HELPERS)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 12 MT.core helpers, in image-driven priority order (most
  used first):

    1. PRIMITIVE          — most common, use for boxes/cylinders
                             matching the reference image
    2. LOFT               — for image-aligned tapered limbs
    3. SDF + MARCH        — for image-aligned organic merging
    4. CSG               — for image-aligned boolean shapes
    5. EXTRUDE (via makePrimitive) — for image-aligned panels
    6. LATHE (via makePrimitive) — for image-aligned rotational shapes
    7. PARAMETRIC        — for image-aligned mathematical surfaces
    8. BMESH             — for image-aligned editing ops
    9. NURBS             — for image-aligned smooth curves
    10. PROCEDURAL       — for image-aligned L-system grammar
    11. GENERATIVE       — for image-aligned branching networks
    12. VOXEL            — for image-aligned blocky shapes

  See PART 281 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 282 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MT.mesh (9 MESH QUALITY HELPERS)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven priority:
    1. QEM DECIMATION    — most common, decimate source mesh to target
    2. ATLAS UV          — for image-aligned UV packing
    3. SUBDIVISION       — for image-aligned low→high boost
    4. ISOTROPIC REMESH  — for image-aligned even triangles
    5. AUTO RETOPOLOGY   — for image-aligned quad mesh
    6. FAST CURVATURE    — for image-aligned curvature (BVH)
    7. TRIANGULATE       — for image-aligned triangulation
    8. QUADIFY           — for image-aligned quad conversion
    9. LAPLACIAN SMOOTH  — for image-aligned smoothing
    10. SCULPT BRUSH     — rarely used (intent unclear from image)
    11. DISPLACE SURFACE — for image-aligned organic detail

  See PART 282 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 283 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MT.organic (13 ORGANIC HELPERS)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories rarely use MT.organic — the reference
  image doesn't carry skeleton / blend-shape / muscle information.
  Most image-driven factories skip skinning and use static meshes.

  Use MT.organic only when:
    - The image clearly shows a skeletal subject (a character
      with visible joints)
    - The user has supplied a skeleton file alongside the image
    - The user wants animation (image-driven motion)

  See PART 283 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 284 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MT.materials (6 MATERIAL/UV HELPERS)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven priority:
    1. PROCEDURAL TEXTURE — derive seed from image (PART 299)
    2. BAKE MAP          — for normal/AO from source mesh
    3. UV UNWRAP         — for image-aligned UV
    4. PBR MATERIAL NODES — for image-aligned material composition
    5. PAINT TEXTURE     — for image-aligned texture painting
    6. ATLAS PACKING     — for image-aligned atlas packing

  See PART 284 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 285 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MT.advanced (4 ADVANCED HELPERS)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven priority:
    1. INSTANCED         — for repeated details in the image
    2. LOD GENERATION    — for high-poly source meshes
    3. PHYSICS SIM       — rarely used (no physics in image)
    4. GEO NODES         — rarely used (no node graph in image)

  See PART 285 in Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full recipes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 286-293 — IMAGE-DRIVEN TECHNIQUE COVERAGE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  See PART 286-293 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    - PART 286 (PBR creature recipes): use iridescence for
      iridescent creatures (beetles, butterflies)
    - PART 287 (cross-primitive): use sparingly — most image-
      driven primitives map to makePrimitive
    - PART 288 (standalone primitives): rarely used in image-
      driven work (most reference images show organic shapes)
    - PART 289 (CS2 PBR): use for image-driven weapon / blade
      subjects
    - PART 290 (advanced shaders): use for image-driven VFX /
      particle systems
    - PART 291 (declarative constraints): use to enforce image-
      derived rules (the image shows no overlap, so set
      noOverlap constraints)
    - PART 292 (rigging): only when image shows skeletal subject
    - PART 293 (sculpt enrichment): use for image-driven hero
      models

================================================================================
PART 316-322 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN WORKED EXAMPLES (ALL 8 ARCHETYPES)
================================================================================

The original v1.36 / v8.27 release shipped only 2 image-driven
worked examples (scooter Style A in PART 226, scooter Style B
in PART 276). PART 316-322 here adds worked examples for ALL
8 archetypes: character, creature, vehicle, architecture, weapon,
plant, furniture, prop. Each example follows the image-driven
recipe: sample the palette → measure the dimensions → build with
SDF + loft + lathed parts → run IoU check.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 316 — CHARACTER (CHUNKY ROBOT — STYLE B IMAGE-DRIVEN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_robot_image_b.py — image-driven Style B factory.
  Analysis: chunky 5-piece robot, red body, blue head, glowing
  antenna tip, matte plastic. Style B for SDF body merging.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_materials, THREE, lblSpec
  import math

  def create_model(ref_img=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Robot (image-driven)"

      # Sample the image's color (PART 297)
      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=4) if ref_img
          else [{"name": "shell", "color": "#cc4444",
                 "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05},
                {"name": "accent", "color": "#4a6fa5",
                 "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1},
                {"name": "glow", "color": "#ffaa00",
                 "emissive": "#ff7700", "emissiveIntensity": 2.0,
                 "roughness": 0.3}]
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      shell = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xcc4444, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05,
          "flatShading": True,
      })
      accent = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x4a6fa5, "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1,
          "flatShading": True,
      })
      glow = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xffaa00, "emissive": 0xff7700,
          "emissiveIntensity": 2.0, "flatShading": True,
      })

      # Body — SDF-merged (PART 250)
      sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
          MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.4, 0], 0.3),
          MT_core.sdfCapsule([0, 0.0, 0], [0, 0.3, 0], 0.2),
      )
      body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, shell)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Head + eyes + antenna + arms + legs
      head = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.4, 0.35, 0.4), accent)
      head.position.y = 0.75
      head.castShadow = True
      head.name = "head"
      g.add(head)

      for i, x in enumerate((-0.08, 0.08)):
          eye = THREE.Mesh.new(
              THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.05, 8, 6),
              _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                  "color": 0xffffff,
                  "emissive": 0xaef5ff, "emissiveIntensity": 1.5,
              }),
          )
          eye.position.set(x, 0.78, 0.21)
          eye.name = f"eye_{i}"
          g.add(eye)

      antenna = THREE.Mesh.new(
          THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.01, 0.01, 0.15, 6), accent,
      )
      antenna.position.set(0, 0.97, 0)
      antenna.name = "antenna"
      g.add(antenna)
      tip = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.04, 8, 6), glow)
      tip.position.set(0, 1.07, 0)
      tip.name = "antenna_tip"
      g.add(tip)

      arm_profile = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.07},
                     {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.05},
                     {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.04}]
      arm_geo = MT_core.loft(arm_profile, {"radial": 8})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          arm = THREE.Mesh.new(arm_geo, shell)
          arm.position.set(side * 0.25, 0.35, 0)
          arm.castShadow = True
          arm.name = f"arm_{side}"
          g.add(arm)

      leg_profile = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.08},
                     {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.06},
                     {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05}]
      leg_geo = MT_core.loft(leg_profile, {"radial": 8})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          leg = THREE.Mesh.new(leg_geo, shell)
          leg.position.set(side * 0.12, -0.4, 0)
          leg.castShadow = True
          leg.name = f"leg_{side}"
          g.add(leg)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "torso", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "antenna_tip", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "character",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.8, "fill": 0.78},
      }

      # IoU check (image-driven only)
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 317 — CREATURE (BEETLE — STYLE B IMAGE-DRIVEN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  beetle_image_b.py — image-driven Style B factory.
  Analysis: chunky iridescent beetle with elytra, 6 legs.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_materials, MT_advanced, THREE, lblSpec
  import math

  def create_model(ref_img=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Iridescent Beetle (image-driven)"

      # Sample palette (or default to chitin colors)
      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=3) if ref_img
          else [
              {"name": "shell",  "color": "#261a0f",
               "iridescence": 1.0, "roughness": 0.35},
              {"name": "wing",   "color": "#0a2540",
               "iridescence": 1.0, "roughness": 0.2},
              {"name": "leg",    "color": "#3a2a1a", "roughness": 0.6},
          ]
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      # Chitin material (PART 286)
      chitin = _lbl_three_new("MeshPhysicalMaterial", {
          "color": 0x261a0f, "roughness": 0.35, "metalness": 0.2,
          "iridescence": 1.0, "iridescenceIOR": 1.3,
          "iridescenceThicknessRange": [200, 800],
          "clearcoat": 0.6, "clearcoatRoughness": 0.1,
      })

      # Body — SDF-merged
      sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
          MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.15, 0], 0.18),
          MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.0, 0.05], 0.15),
      )
      body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 96})
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, chitin)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Head
      head = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.10, 16, 12), chitin)
      head.position.set(0, 0.18, 0.16)
      head.castShadow = True
      head.name = "head"
      g.add(head)

      # Elytra
      for side in (-1, 1):
          elytron_geo = MT_core.makePrimitive("sphere", {
              "r": 0.16, "ws": 16, "hs": 8,
              "phiLength": math.pi / 2, "thetaLength": math.pi,
          })
          elytron = THREE.Mesh.new(elytron_geo, chitin)
          elytron.scale.set(0.9, 0.5, 0.4)
          elytron.position.set(side * 0.05, 0.18, -0.05)
          elytron.castShadow = True
          elytron.name = f"elytron_{side}"
          g.add(elytron)

      # Legs — 6 lofted
      leg_profile = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.025},
                     {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.018},
                     {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.012}]
      leg_geo = MT_core.loft(leg_profile, {"radial": 6})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          for i in range(3):
              leg = THREE.Mesh.new(leg_geo, chitin)
              x = side * (0.10 + i * 0.05)
              z = 0.10 - i * 0.05
              leg.position.set(x, 0.02, z)
              leg.rotation.z = side * (0.3 + i * 0.2)
              leg.castShadow = True
              leg.name = f"leg_{side}_{i}"
              g.add(leg)

      # Instanced eyes
      eye_geo = THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.025, 8, 6)
      eyes = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(eye_geo, 2, {
          "transforms": [
              {"position": [-0.03, 0.20, 0.25], "rotation": [0, 0, 0],
               "scale": [1, 1, 1]},
              {"position": [0.03, 0.20, 0.25], "rotation": [0, 0, 0],
               "scale": [1, 1, 1]},
          ],
      })
      g.add(eyes)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {"body": body, "head": head,
                     "elytra": eyes, "legs": g.children[3:9]},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "thorax", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "elytra", "region": "wings", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "legs", "region": "limbs", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "creature",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 1.2, "fill": 0.6},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 318 — VEHICLE (SEDAN — STYLE B IMAGE-DRIVEN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  sedan_image_b.py — image-driven Style B factory.
  Analysis: 4-door sedan with curved fairings, glass windows,
  4 lathed wheels, glowing headlights.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_hardsurface, THREE, lblSpec
  import math

  def create_model(ref_img=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Sedan (image-driven)"

      paint = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xcc4444, "roughness": 0.4, "metalness": 0.3,
          "clearcoat": 0.7, "clearcoatRoughness": 0.1,
      })
      tire = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x1a1a1e, "roughness": 0.95, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      glass = _lbl_three_new("MeshPhysicalMaterial", {
          "color": 0x90c0e0, "transmission": 0.85, "thickness": 0.05,
          "roughness": 0.05, "transparent": True, "opacity": 0.5,
      })

      # Body — extruded 2D shape with rounded top
      body_shape = THREE.Shape.new()
      body_shape.moveTo(-1.0, 0)
      body_shape.lineTo(1.0, 0)
      body_shape.bezierCurveTo(1.05, 0.2, 1.0, 0.45, 0.7, 0.5)
      body_shape.lineTo(-0.7, 0.5)
      body_shape.bezierCurveTo(-1.0, 0.5, -1.05, 0.2, -1.0, 0)
      body_shape.closePath()
      body_geo = _lbl_three_new("ExtrudeGeometry", body_shape, {
          "depth": 0.8, "bevelEnabled": True,
          "bevelSize": 0.04, "bevelThickness": 0.04, "bevelSegments": 4,
      })
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, paint)
      body.position.set(0, 0.3, 0)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.receiveShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Cabin + windows + wheels + headlights (from PART 319)
      # [same as the prompt-driven example — abbreviated here]

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "cabin", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "windows", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "wheel_0", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "wheel_1", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "vehicle",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.5, "fill": 0.7,
                          "wheel_radius_over_H": 0.15},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 319 — ARCHITECTURE (HOUSE — STYLE B IMAGE-DRIVEN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  house_image_b.py — image-driven Style B factory.
  Analysis: 4-wall house with door, windows, gable roof.
  """
  from js import MT_architectural, MT_lookdev, THREE, lblSpec
  import math

  def create_model(ref_img=None):
      house = THREE.Group.new()
      house.name = "Chunky House (image-driven)"

      palette = MT_architectural.materialPalette()
      wall_face = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", palette["wall_face"])
      roof_tile = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", palette["roof_tile"])
      door = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", palette["door"])
      window_glass = _lbl_three_new("MeshPhysicalMaterial", palette["window_glass"])

      # 4 walls with image-derived apertures
      for i, side in enumerate(("N", "E", "S", "W")):
          apertures = (
              [{"type": "door", "x": 0.5, "width": 0.9, "height": 2.1}]
              if side == "S" else
              [{"type": "window", "x": 1.5, "width": 0.8, "height": 0.8},
               {"type": "window", "x": 2.7, "width": 0.8, "height": 0.8}]
              if side == "N" else []
          )
          wall_geo = MT_architectural.createWallWithApertures({
              "width": 4.0, "height": 2.8, "thickness": 0.15,
              "apertures": apertures,
          })
          wall = THREE.Mesh.new(wall_geo, wall_face)
          wall.rotation.y = i * (math.pi / 2)
          wall.castShadow = True
          wall.receiveShadow = True
          wall.name = f"wall_{side}"
          house.add(wall)

      # Gable roof
      roof_geo = MT_architectural.createPitchedGableRoof({
          "width": 4.3, "length": 4.3, "pitch": 30, "tileRows": 8,
      })
      roof = THREE.Mesh.new(roof_geo, roof_tile)
      roof.position.y = 2.8
      roof.castShadow = True
      roof.receiveShadow = True
      roof.name = "roof"
      house.add(roof)

      # Door + windows (PART 320)
      door_geo = MT_architectural.createHingedDoorUnit({
          "width": 0.9, "height": 2.1, "thickness": 0.05,
          "hinge": "right",
      })
      door_mesh = THREE.Mesh.new(door_geo, door)
      door_mesh.position.set(0, 0, 2.0)
      door_mesh.name = "door"
      house.add(door_mesh)

      for i, x in enumerate((1.5, 2.7)):
          win_geo = MT_architectural.createGlazedWindowUnit({
              "width": 0.8, "height": 0.8, "frameDepth": 0.05,
          })
          win = THREE.Mesh.new(win_geo, window_glass)
          win.position.set(x, 1.2, -2.0)
          win.name = f"window_{i}"
          house.add(win)

      house.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in house.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in house.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "wall_N", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "wall_S", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "wall_E", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "wall_W", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "roof", "region": "roof", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "door", "region": "entrance", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.4},
          ],
      }
      house.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "architecture",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 1.4, "fill": 0.85},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(house, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              house.scale.set(1.05, 1.05, 1.05)
      return house

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 320 — WEAPON (SWORD — STYLE B IMAGE-DRIVEN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  sword_image_b.py — image-driven Style B factory.
  Analysis: medieval sword with curved blade, cross-guard, leather grip.
  """
  from js import MT_materials, MT_advanced, MT_core, THREE, lblSpec

  def create_model(ref_img=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Sword (image-driven)"

      # Materials — sampled from image (or default medieval colors)
      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=3) if ref_img
          else [
              {"name": "blade",   "color": "#cccccc", "roughness": 0.2,
               "metalness": 0.95},
              {"name": "guard",   "color": "#b5965a", "roughness": 0.4,
               "metalness": 0.85},
              {"name": "grip",    "color": "#3a2a1a", "roughness": 0.8,
               "metalness": 0.0},
          ]
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      blade_mat = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xcccccc, "roughness": 0.2, "metalness": 0.95,
      })
      guard_mat = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xb5965a, "roughness": 0.4, "metalness": 0.85,
      })
      grip_mat = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x3a2a1a, "roughness": 0.8, "metalness": 0.0,
      })

      # Blade — extruded 2D shape
      blade_shape = THREE.Shape.new()
      blade_shape.moveTo(-0.05, 0)
      blade_shape.lineTo(0.05, 0)
      blade_shape.lineTo(0.03, 0.8)
      blade_shape.lineTo(0, 0.85)  # tip
      blade_shape.lineTo(-0.03, 0.8)
      blade_shape.closePath()
      blade_geo = _lbl_three_new("ExtrudeGeometry", blade_shape, {
          "depth": 0.015, "bevelEnabled": True,
          "bevelSize": 0.005, "bevelThickness": 0.005, "bevelSegments": 2,
      })
      blade = THREE.Mesh.new(blade_geo, blade_mat)
      blade.position.set(0, 0, -0.0075)
      blade.castShadow = True
      blade.name = "blade"
      g.add(blade)

      # Cross-guard — extruded shape
      guard_shape = THREE.Shape.new()
      guard_shape.moveTo(-0.15, 0)
      guard_shape.lineTo(0.15, 0)
      guard_shape.lineTo(0.15, 0.05)
      guard_shape.lineTo(-0.15, 0.05)
      guard_shape.closePath()
      guard_geo = _lbl_three_new("ExtrudeGeometry", guard_shape, {
          "depth": 0.04, "bevelEnabled": True,
          "bevelSize": 0.005, "bevelThickness": 0.005, "bevelSegments": 2,
      })
      guard = THREE.Mesh.new(guard_geo, guard_mat)
      guard.position.set(0, -0.025, -0.02)
      guard.castShadow = True
      guard.name = "guard"
      g.add(guard)

      # Grip — lofted
      grip_profile = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.025},
                      {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.022},
                      {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.025}]
      grip_geo = MT_core.loft(grip_profile, {"radial": 8, "length": 8})
      grip = THREE.Mesh.new(grip_geo, grip_mat)
      grip.position.set(0, -0.25, 0)
      grip.castShadow = True
      grip.name = "grip"
      g.add(grip)

      # Pommel — sphere
      pommel = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.04, 12, 8),
                              guard_mat)
      pommel.position.set(0, -0.42, 0)
      pommel.castShadow = True
      pommel.name = "pommel"
      g.add(pommel)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "blade", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.35,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "guard", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.10,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "grip", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.20,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "pommel", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "triShare": 0.08,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "weapon",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.15, "fill": 0.4},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 321 — PLANT (TREE — STYLE B IMAGE-DRIVEN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  tree_image_b.py — image-driven Style B factory.
  Analysis: low-poly tree with bark trunk + foliage.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_advanced, THREE, lblSpec

  def create_model(ref_img=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Tree (image-driven)"

      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=2) if ref_img
          else [
              {"name": "bark",   "color": "#5a3a1a", "roughness": 0.95},
              {"name": "leaf",   "color": "#3a8a3a", "roughness": 0.7},
          ]
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      bark = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x5a3a1a, "roughness": 0.95, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      leaf = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x3a8a3a, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.0,
      })

      # Trunk — lofted
      trunk_profile = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.10},
                       {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.07},
                       {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05}]
      trunk_geo = MT_core.loft(trunk_profile, {"radial": 8})
      trunk = THREE.Mesh.new(trunk_geo, bark)
      trunk.position.y = 0.5
      trunk.castShadow = True
      trunk.name = "trunk"
      g.add(trunk)

      # Foliage — instanced icosahedra
      leaf_geo = MT_core.makePrimitive("icosahedron", {"r": 0.25, "detail": 0})
      foliage = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(leaf_geo, 5, {
          "transforms": [
              {"position": [0, 1.2, 0], "rotation": [0, 0, 0],
               "scale": [1, 1, 1]},
              {"position": [0.2, 1.4, 0.1], "rotation": [0.5, 0, 0],
               "scale": [0.8, 0.8, 0.8]},
              {"position": [-0.15, 1.35, -0.1], "rotation": [0, 0.5, 0],
               "scale": [0.7, 0.7, 0.7]},
              {"position": [0.1, 1.6, -0.15], "rotation": [0.3, 0.3, 0],
               "scale": [0.6, 0.6, 0.6]},
              {"position": [-0.05, 1.5, 0.15], "rotation": [0, 0, 0.3],
               "scale": [0.65, 0.65, 0.65]},
          ],
      })
      g.add(foliage)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {"trunk": trunk, "foliage": foliage},
          "meshes": {"trunk": trunk, "foliage": foliage},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "trunk", "region": "stem", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "foliage", "region": "canopy", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "plant",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.5, "fill": 0.4,
                          "trunk_share_of_height": 0.4},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 322 — FURNITURE + PROP (TABLE + MUG — STYLE B IMAGE-DRIVEN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  table_image_b.py + mug_image_b.py — image-driven Style B factories.
  Two more archetypes for completeness.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_materials, MT_advanced, THREE, lblSpec

  # Table (furniture)
  def create_table(ref_img=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Table (image-driven)"

      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=2) if ref_img
          else [
              {"name": "wood_top", "color": "#8a6a4a", "roughness": 0.8},
              {"name": "wood_leg", "color": "#5a3a1a", "roughness": 0.9},
          ]
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      top_mat = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x8a6a4a, "roughness": 0.8, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      leg_mat = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x5a3a1a, "roughness": 0.9, "metalness": 0.0,
      })

      # Top — box
      top = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.BoxGeometry.new(1.2, 0.05, 0.8), top_mat)
      top.position.y = 0.75
      top.castShadow = True
      top.receiveShadow = True
      top.name = "top"
      g.add(top)

      # 4 legs
      for i, (x, z) in enumerate([
          (-0.55, -0.35), (0.55, -0.35),
          (-0.55, 0.35), (0.55, 0.35),
      ]):
          leg = THREE.Mesh.new(
              THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.08, 0.75, 0.08), leg_mat,
          )
          leg.position.set(x, 0.375, z)
          leg.castShadow = True
          leg.name = f"leg_{i}"
          g.add(leg)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "top", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.4,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "leg_0", "region": "support", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.15,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "leg_1", "region": "support", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.15,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "leg_2", "region": "support", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.15,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "leg_3", "region": "support", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.15,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "furniture",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 1.4, "fill": 0.6},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

  # Mug (prop)
  def create_mug(ref_img=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Mug (image-driven)"

      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=2) if ref_img
          else [
              {"name": "ceramic", "color": "#f4f0e6", "roughness": 0.65},
              {"name": "rim",     "color": "#5a3a00", "roughness": 0.4},
          ]
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      ceramic = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xf4f0e6, "roughness": 0.65, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      rim = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x5a3a00, "roughness": 0.4, "metalness": 0.0,
      })

      # Body — cylinder
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(
          THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.5, 0.5, 1.4, 24), ceramic,
      )
      body.position.y = 0.7
      body.castShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Rim
      rim_mesh = THREE.Mesh.new(
          THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.52, 0.52, 0.06, 24), rim,
      )
      rim_mesh.position.y = 1.37
      rim_mesh.castShadow = True
      rim_mesh.name = "rim"
      g.add(rim_mesh)

      # Handle — 3 lofted segments
      handle_pts = [
          [0.5, 1.0, 0], [0.75, 0.7, 0], [0.5, 0.4, 0],
      ]
      handle_geo = MT_core.loft(
          [{"t": i/2.0, "radius": 0.06} for i in range(3)],
          {"radial": 8},
      )
      handle = THREE.Mesh.new(handle_geo, ceramic)
      handle.castShadow = True
      handle.name = "handle"
      g.add(handle)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.65,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "rim", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "triShare": 0.10,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "handle", "region": "primary", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.25,
               "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "prop",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.7, "fill": 0.6},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

  # The actual create_model for this archetype — image-driven decides
  # at runtime whether to build the table or the mug based on the
  # detected subject class:
  def create_model(ref_img=None):
      from js import lblSpec
      subject = lblSpec.helpers.detectSubject(ref_img) if ref_img else "mug"
      if subject == "table":
          return create_table(ref_img)
      else:
          return create_mug(ref_img)

  # Apply for whichever archetype.

PART 323-330 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PRODUCTION DISCIPLINE
================================================================================

The image-driven production discipline mirrors PART 293-325 in
Prompt_To_Py.txt, with image-driven specific notes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 323 — 60/25/15 POLYGON DISTRIBUTION (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Same allocation pattern as PART 323 in Prompt_To_Py.txt, but
  the priority mix is informed by the reference image's complexity:

       from js import lblSpec
       def allocate_tri_budget_image_drift(ref_img, total_tris=5000):
           # Use the image's complexity to inform the budget
           complexity = lblSpec.helpers.imageComplexity(ref_img)
           # complexity is a 0..1 score; high = more detail needed
           if complexity > 0.7:
               identity_share = 0.55
           elif complexity > 0.4:
               identity_share = 0.60
           else:
               identity_share = 0.65
           return {
               "IDENTITY": int(total_tris * identity_share),
               "SECONDARY": int(total_tris * (1 - identity_share) * 5/7),
               "TERTIARY": int(total_tris * (1 - identity_share) * 2/7),
           }

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 324 — 5-STAGE PIPELINE (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 5-stage pipeline from PART 324 in Prompt_To_Py.txt, with
  image-driven emphasis on the staged build passes (PART 346-352
  below).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 325 — 11-STEP RECIPE (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 11-step recipe from PART 325 in Prompt_To_Py.txt, with
  image-driven emphasis on the IoU check (step 10) which is
  mandatory for image-driven mode.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 326 — 3-VARIANT RANKING (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Same as PART 326 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven factories
  generate 3 variants from 3 seeds; the IoU is the primary
  ranking metric.

PART 327 — 7 PRODUCTION TECHNIQUES (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Same as PART 327 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Image-driven emphasis:
    1. Detail inventory tracking — match against the image
    2. Layered armor — match against the image's visible layers
    3. Multi-layer cloth — match against the image's clothing
    4. Micro-chips + decals — derive from the image's surface texture
    5. Silhouette pass — compare against the image's silhouette
    6. Material variants — derive from the image's color regions
    7. Production-completeness audit — check against the image

================================================================================
The image-driven Python factory has its own set of common mistakes
and worked examples, distinct from prompt-driven Python (PART
309-322 in Prompt_To_Py.txt). PART 309-322 here covers the
image-driven specific gotchas.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 309 — IMAGE-DRIVEN TYPOGRAPHY MISTAKES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  309.1 — IGNORING refImg WHEN AVAILABLE
        ❌ def create_model():
               # No ref_img argument, model drifts from reference
        ✅ def create_model(ref_img=None):
               if ref_img:
                   iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                   if iou < 0.80:
                       g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
        # The renderer passes ref_img as the factory's first arg
        # when in image-driven mode.

  309.2 — FIXED SEED FOR IMAGE-DRIVEN
        ❌ rng = mulberry32(42)  # hard-coded
        ✅ rng = mulberry32(derive_seed_from_image(ref_img))
        # See PART 299.

  309.3 — PALETTE FROM TEXT, NOT IMAGE
        ❌ lbl.define_palette({"shell": "#ff0000"})  # wrong color
        ✅ palette = sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=6)
           lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 310 — IMAGE-DRIVEN SEMANTIC MISTAKES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  310.1 — SHAPE MISMATCH
        ❌ # Image shows a curved fairing, but factory builds a box
        # IoU drops below 0.80, retry loop kicks in but doesn't fix
        ✅ # Image-driven factories must PRESERVE THE FORM
           # Use ExtrudeGeometry / LatheGeometry / loft, not boxes
        # See PART 277 (Hierarchy of Fidelity rule for image-driven).

  310.2 — PROPORTION DRIFT
        ❌ # Image shows W:H ratio 1:2 but factory produces 1:1
        # The validator's PART 49.1 audit fires but you ignore it
        ✅ # Adjust the proportions to match the image
           bp["meta"]["proportions"]["W_over_H"] = 0.5

  310.3 — DETAIL INVENTORY INCOMPLETE
        ❌ # Factory builds 4 parts but image shows 8
        ✅ # Use the detail inventory pattern (PART 331-338) and
           # verify against the image's visible features

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 311-315 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MATERIAL/TEXTURE/ANIMATION/STAGE MISTAKES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven specific notes for material/texture/animation/
  stage mistakes (see PART 311-315 in Prompt_To_Ty.txt for the
  full prompt-driven forms):

  - Image-derived texture MUST match the image's color/roughness
    exactly. Don't apply a flat plastic material to an image that
    shows a metallic surface.
  - The IoU check (PART 254) is mandatory for image-driven
    factories. Skip it only if there's no reference image.
  - The renderer's auto-frame uses the image's silhouette; off-
    frame parts get clipped.

  312 — IMAGE-DRIVEN ANIMATION (extends PART 213 + Prompt_To_Py.txt PART 208.3)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  When the reference image shows an action pose (a character mid-stride,
  a vehicle with spinning wheels, a flag billowing), the AI factory
  MUST publish the animation so the in-app player can replay it:

    312.1 — PUBLISH, DON'T JUST TICK
      ❌ mixer = lbl.make_mixer(group)
         lbl.play_animation(mixer, walk_clip)
         # toggle button stays hidden — see PART 213.15
      ✅ lbl.install_runtime(group)
         mixer = lbl.make_mixer(group)
         walk_clip = lbl.build_walk_clip([...])
         lbl.publish_animation(group, mixer=mixer, clips=[walk_clip])
         # toggle button enabled + mixer auto-ticks every frame

    312.2 — glTF BAKED CLIPS — translate channel names (PART 369.5)
      If you baked a clip from a .glb asset (or a keyframe set
      extracted from a similar image), the glTF "rotation" channel
      is quaternion-based (4 floats/key) and targets `.quaternion`,
      NOT `.rotation[x/y/z]`. Use lbl.detect_animation_track_type()
      to classify correctly, then use lbl.quaternion_keyframe_track
      / lbl.vector_keyframe_track / lbl.make_clip (not raw
      THREE.*KeyframeTrack.new — see PART 213.14 + 369.5).

    312.3 — HEADLESS VERIFICATION (PART 369.6)
      "Constructs without NameError" is NOT evidence the clip plays.
      Run the headless playback harness from public/modeling/
      test_integration.py BEFORE shipping a baked-clip factory.
      If the AFTER position equals the BEFORE position, the clip is
      silently failing.

  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  IMAGE-DRIVEN FACTORIES MUST ALSO READ THESE IN Prompt_To_Py.txt:
    PART 213.12 — `globals()["shoulder_L"]` to look up a local
      variable (KeyError). Same pitfall applies whenever you build
      a name -> object dict inside `create_model` and try to reach
      into `globals()` instead.
    PART 213.13 — `pyodide.ffi.to_js({...})` to keep clips alive.
      The `to_js` call DESTROYS the borrowed proxies it unwraps;
      assigning the Python dict directly to `root.userData.foo`
      is the correct keepalive.
    PART 213.14 — `THREE.AnimationMixer.new(...)` /
      `THREE.AnimationClip.new(...)` / `THREE.*KeyframeTrack.new(...)`.
      Pyodide 0.26 / 0.26.1 / 0.26.2 destroys the borrowed proxy
      for these classes before the first property access. Use
      `window.eval("new THREE.X(...)")` instead. This is the same
      fix that Prompt_To_Py.txt PART 203.1 documents, with full
      helper code.
    PART 213.15 — `sculptRuntime.animation` (SINGULAR) hides the
      animation toggle button. The renderer reads the PLURAL key
      `sculptRuntime.animations = { clips, mixer, actions }`. The
      helper `lbl.publish_animation(g, mixer=m, clips=[...])` writes
      the modern shape in one call. See PART 207 + PART 213.15 in
      Prompt_To_Py.txt for the full contract.
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────


────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 316-322 — IMAGE-DRIVEN WORKED EXAMPLES (5 NEW)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories have different patterns from prompt-
  driven. PART 316-322 here adds 5 image-driven Python examples
  covering the 5 archetypes:

  316 — SCOOT CHARACTER (image-driven Style A)
        # See PART 226 in this file for the canonical example.
        # The scooter example is the Style A image-driven baseline.

  317 — CHUNKY ROBOT FROM IMAGE (image-driven Style B)
        """
        chunky_robot_from_image.py — image-driven chunky robot.
        Analysis (sample): chunky 5-piece robot with rounded edges,
        red body, blue head, glowing antenna tip, matte plastic.
        """
        from js import MT_core, MT_materials, lblSpec
        import math

        def create_model(ref_img=None):
            g = THREE.Group.new()
            g.name = "Chunky Robot (image-driven)"

            # Sample the image's palette
            if ref_img:
                palette = sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=4)
            else:
                palette = [
                    {"name": "shell",  "color": "#cc4444",
                     "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05},
                    {"name": "accent", "color": "#4a6fa5",
                     "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1},
                    {"name": "glow",   "color": "#ffaa00",
                     "emissive": "#ff7700", "emissiveIntensity": 2.0,
                     "roughness": 0.3},
                ]
            lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

            shell = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                "color": 0xcc4444, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05,
                "flatShading": True,
            })
            accent = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                "color": 0x4a6fa5, "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1,
                "flatShading": True,
            })
            glow = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                "color": 0xffaa00, "emissive": 0xff7700,
                "emissiveIntensity": 2.0, "flatShading": True,
            })

            # Body — SDF-merged (PART 250)
            sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
                MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.4, 0], 0.3),
                MT_core.sdfCapsule([0, 0.0, 0], [0, 0.3, 0], 0.2),
            )
            body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})
            body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, shell)
            body.castShadow = True
            body.name = "body"
            g.add(body)

            # Head
            head = THREE.Mesh.new(
                THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.4, 0.35, 0.4), accent,
            )
            head.position.y = 0.75
            head.castShadow = True
            head.name = "head"
            g.add(head)

            # Eyes
            for i, x in enumerate((-0.08, 0.08)):
                eye = THREE.Mesh.new(
                    THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.05, 8, 6),
                    _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                        "color": 0xffffff,
                        "emissive": 0xaef5ff, "emissiveIntensity": 1.5,
                    }),
                )
                eye.position.set(x, 0.78, 0.21)
                eye.name = f"eye_{i}"
                g.add(eye)

            # Antenna with glowing tip
            antenna = THREE.Mesh.new(
                THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.01, 0.01, 0.15, 6), accent,
            )
            antenna.position.set(0, 0.97, 0)
            antenna.name = "antenna"
            g.add(antenna)
            tip = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.04, 8, 6), glow)
            tip.position.set(0, 1.07, 0)
            tip.name = "antenna_tip"
            g.add(tip)

            # Arms (lofted)
            arm_profile = [
                {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.07},
                {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.05},
                {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.04},
            ]
            arm_geo = MT_core.loft(arm_profile, {"radial": 8})
            for side in (-1, 1):
                arm = THREE.Mesh.new(arm_geo, shell)
                arm.position.set(side * 0.25, 0.35, 0)
                arm.castShadow = True
                arm.name = f"arm_{side}"
                g.add(arm)

            # Legs (lofted)
            leg_profile = [
                {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.08},
                {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.06},
                {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05},
            ]
            leg_geo = MT_core.loft(leg_profile, {"radial": 8})
            for side in (-1, 1):
                leg = THREE.Mesh.new(leg_geo, shell)
                leg.position.set(side * 0.12, -0.4, 0)
                leg.castShadow = True
                leg.name = f"leg_{side}"
                g.add(leg)

            g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
                "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
                "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                           if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
                "detailInventory": [
                    {"id": "body", "region": "torso", "kind": "feature",
                     "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
                    {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
                     "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
                    {"id": "antenna_tip", "region": "head",
                     "kind": "feature", "priority": "medium",
                     "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
                ],
            }
            g.userData.meta = {
                "style": "chunky-mascot",
                "category": "character",
                "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.8, "fill": 0.78},
            }

            # IoU check (image-driven only)
            if ref_img:
                iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                if iou < 0.80:
                    g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
            return g

  317-322 — Other 4 image-driven examples follow the same
        pattern: take a reference image, sample the palette,
        build with SDF + loft + lathed parts, run IoU check.
        See PART 226 + PART 276 (Style B) for the canonical
        image-driven examples. The other 4 (creature, vehicle,
        building, plant) follow the same recipe with archetype-
        specific palettes and geometries.
