PART 353-365 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN WORKED EXAMPLES (ALL 8 ARCHETYPES, FULL FORM)
================================================================================

PART 316-322 above gave the canonical 8 image-driven worked examples
in compact form. PART 353-365 here gives the FULL FORM of each
example, with detailed line-by-line explanations of what's happening.
This is the complete image-driven Python cookbook — an AI factory
author can read these examples in order to learn the image-driven
Python recipe.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 353 — CHARACTER IMAGE-DRIVEN (CHUNKY ROBOT — FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Same as PART 316 but with every line explained.

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.1 — IMPORT BLOCK
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

    from js import MT_core, MT_materials, MT_advanced, THREE, lblSpec
    import math

  Explanation:
    - MT_core: the renderer's core helpers (sdfUnion, sdfMarch, loft)
    - MT_materials: the materials/UV helpers (proceduralTextureCanvas)
    - MT_advanced: the advanced helpers (makeInstanced)
    - THREE: the Three.js library (Mesh, Group, BoxGeometry, etc.)
    - lblSpec: the renderer's spec API (compareToReference, helpers)
    - math: Python's math module (sin, cos, pi)

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.2 — FACTORY SIGNATURE
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

    def create_model(ref_img=None):
        g = THREE.Group.new()
        g.name = "Chunky Robot"

  Explanation:
    - ref_img: optional reference image (when image-driven mode)
    - The factory returns a THREE.Group with children (Style B).
    - g.name sets the model's name in the inspector's tree view.

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.3 — PALETTE DERIVATION
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        palette = (
            sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=4) if ref_img
            else DEFAULT_PALETTE
        )
        lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

  Explanation:
    - If ref_img is provided, sample the image's top-4 colors.
    - Otherwise, use a hard-coded default palette.
    - lbl.define_palette() registers the palette for Style A; Style B
      reads it from g.userData.meta when the model is mounted.

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.4 — MATERIALS
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        shell = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
            "color": 0xcc4444, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05,
            "flatShading": True,  # PART 252 chunky-mascot rule
        })
        accent = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
            "color": 0x4a6fa5, "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1,
            "flatShading": True,
        })
        glow = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
            "color": 0xffaa00, "emissive": 0xff7700,
            "emissiveIntensity": 2.0,  # PART 305 glow material
            "flatShading": True,
        })

  Explanation:
    - shell: matte plastic for the body
    - accent: glossy plastic for the head
    - glow: emissive material for the antenna tip (PART 305.10)
    - flatShading: True is the PART 252 chunky-mascot rule

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.5 — BODY (SDF MERGED)
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
            MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.4, 0], 0.3),
            MT_core.sdfCapsule([0, 0.0, 0], [0, 0.3, 0], 0.2),
        )
        body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})
        body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, shell)
        body.castShadow = True
        body.name = "body"
        g.add(body)

  Explanation:
    - PART 250: SDF + Marching Cubes for contiguous parts.
    - Sphere at (0, 0.4, 0) radius 0.3 = head sphere
    - Capsule from (0, 0, 0) to (0, 0.3, 0) radius 0.2 = torso
    - sdfUnion merges them into a single watertight mesh.
    - sdfMarch generates the geometry.
    - The body is a single mesh, no gaps.

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.6 — HEAD, EYES, ANTENNA
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        head = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.4, 0.35, 0.4), accent)
        head.position.y = 0.75
        head.castShadow = True
        head.name = "head"
        g.add(head)

        for i, x in enumerate((-0.08, 0.08)):
            eye = THREE.Mesh.new(
                THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.05, 8, 6),
                _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                    "color": 0xffffff,
                    "emissive": 0xaef5ff, "emissiveIntensity": 1.5,
                }),
            )
            eye.position.set(x, 0.78, 0.21)
            eye.name = f"eye_{i}"
            g.add(eye)

        antenna = THREE.Mesh.new(
            THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.01, 0.01, 0.15, 6), accent,
        )
        antenna.position.set(0, 0.97, 0)
        antenna.name = "antenna"
        g.add(antenna)
        tip = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.04, 8, 6), glow)
        tip.position.set(0, 1.07, 0)
        tip.name = "antenna_tip"
        g.add(tip)

  Explanation:
    - Head: simple box above the body
    - 2 eyes: small spheres with emissive glow
    - Antenna: thin cylinder + glowing tip sphere

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.7 — ARMS (LOFTED)
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        arm_profile = [
            {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.07},  # shoulder
            {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.05},  # elbow
            {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.04},  # wrist
        ]
        arm_geo = MT_core.loft(arm_profile, {"radial": 8})
        for side in (-1, 1):
            arm = THREE.Mesh.new(arm_geo, shell)
            # PART 251: bbox-derived pivot
            body.geometry.computeBoundingBox()
            torso_w = (body.geometry.boundingBox.max.x -
                       body.geometry.boundingBox.min.x)
            arm.position.set(side * torso_w * 0.55, 0.35, 0)
            arm.castShadow = True
            arm.name = f"arm_{side}"
            g.add(arm)

  Explanation:
    - PART 253: lofted limb (NOT stacked cylinders).
    - Profile: 3 control points (shoulder/elbow/wrist).
    - Bbox-derived pivot (PART 251): arm position is derived from
      the parent mesh's bounding box, NOT a magic number.

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.8 — LEGS (LOFTED)
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        leg_profile = [
            {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.08},
            {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.06},
            {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05},
        ]
        leg_geo = MT_core.loft(leg_profile, {"radial": 8})
        for side in (-1, 1):
            leg = THREE.Mesh.new(leg_geo, shell)
            leg.position.set(side * 0.12, -0.4, 0)
            leg.castShadow = True
            leg.name = f"leg_{side}"
            g.add(leg)

  Explanation:
    - Same pattern as arms but with thicker profile.

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.9 — sculptRuntime
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
            "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
            "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                       if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
            "detailInventory": [
                {"id": "body", "region": "torso", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
                {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
                {"id": "antenna_tip", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
                {"id": "arm_-1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
                {"id": "arm_1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
                {"id": "leg_-1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
                {"id": "leg_1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
                {"id": "eye_0", "region": "face", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
                {"id": "eye_1", "region": "face", "kind": "feature",
                 "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
            ],
        }
        g.userData.meta = {
            "style": "chunky-mascot",
            "category": "character",
            "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.8, "fill": 0.78,
                            "head_share_of_length": 0.3},
        }

  Explanation:
    - sculptRuntime: 9-key contract (PART 207). At minimum, nodes +
      meshes + detailInventory.
    - Every high-priority detailInventory entry has a matching Mesh.
    - meta.style = "chunky-mascot" (PART 252 style lock).
    - meta.category = "character" (PART 344 subject routing).
    - meta.proportions includes PART 49.1 required fields.

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1.10 — IoU CHECK + RETURN
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

        if ref_img:
            iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
            if iou < 0.80:
                # PART 254 retry loop
                g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
                iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                if iou < 0.80:
                    print(f"Warning: IoU still {iou} after scale-up")
        return g

  Explanation:
    - PART 254: silhouette IoU check (mandatory for image-driven).
    - Retry loop: scale up by 10% if IoU < 0.80.
    - The factory returns the model even if the retry fails
      (warning is logged but the build doesn't crash).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 354-360 — OTHER 7 ARCHETYPES (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 317 (creature beetle), PART 318 (sedan), PART 319 (house),
  PART 320 (sword), PART 321 (tree), PART 322 (table/mug) all have
  full forms with the same line-by-line explanation pattern as
  PART 353 above. The structure of each is:

    1. Import block
    2. Factory signature with ref_img=None
    3. Palette derivation (sample from image or default)
    4. Materials (one per archetype)
    5. Geometry (primitive + SDF + loft + extrude as needed)
    6. Accessories (rivets, decals, glow elements)
    7. sculptRuntime (nodes + meshes + detailInventory)
    8. meta (style + category + proportions)
    9. IoU check (image-driven only)

  Each archetype differs in:
    - Palette: 2-4 entries (creature + shell + accent; vehicle +
      paint + tire + glass; etc.)
    - Geometry: dominant technique (SDF for organic, ExtrudeGeometry
      for panels, Loft for limbs)
    - Detail inventory: 5-10 entries specific to the archetype

  See PART 316-322 above for the compact form of each. The full
  line-by-line explanation for PART 353 (chunky robot) covers the
  pattern; the other 7 follow the same recipe.

PART 361-365 — IMAGE-DRIVEN ADVANCED TECHNIQUES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 5 advanced image-driven techniques that go beyond the basic
  archetype recipes:

  361 — HIRES-TO-CHUNKY ROUND-TRIP
        async def create_model(ref_img=None, source_mesh_url=None):
            if source_mesh_url:
                source = await lbl.import_external_mesh(
                    source_mesh_url, format="glb",
                )
                if source.attributes["position"]["count"] < 5000:
                    source = lbl.subdivide_for_highpoly(source, {"levels": 2})
                chunky = lbl.decimate_for_lowpoly(
                    source, {"ratio": 3000 / source.attributes["position"]["count"]},
                )
                body = THREE.Mesh.new(chunky, mat_shell)
                g.add(body)
            else:
                body_geo = MT_core.makePrimitive("capsule", {"r": 0.3, "length": 1.0})
                body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, mat_shell)
                g.add(body)
            return g

  362 — IMAGE COLOR SAMPLING (CIE LAB SPACE)
        from js import lblSpec
        palette = lblSpec.helpers.sampleImagePaletteLAB(ref_img, n_colors=6)
        # palette = [
        #   {"name": "shell", "lab": [55, 30, 25], "color": "#cc4444", ...},
        #   ...
        # ]

  363 — SILHOUETTE POLYGON DECOMPOSITION
        from js import lblSpec
        silhouette_polys = lblSpec.helpers.silhouetteToPolygons(ref_img)
        for poly in silhouette_polys:
            shape = THREE.Shape.new()
            for x, y in poly:
                shape.lineTo(x, y)
            shape.closePath()
            geo = _lbl_three_new("ExtrudeGeometry", shape, {"depth": 0.5})

  364 — REFERENCE FEATURE DETECTION (LANDMARKS)
        from js import lblSpec
        landmarks = lblSpec.helpers.detectFeatures(ref_img)
        for lm in landmarks:
            if lm["name"] == "eye":
                eye_world_pos = lblSpec.helpers.imageToWorld(
                    [lm["x"], lm["y"]], ref_img, [0, 0, 0], [0, 0, 1],
                )
                eye.position.set(*eye_world_pos[:3])

  365 — VARIANT GENERATION (3 SEEDS)
        async def create_model(ref_img=None, seed=1):
            if seed == 2:
                g.userData.meta["style"] = "smooth-low-poly"
            elif seed == 3:
                for child in g.children:
                    child.scale.multiplyScalar(1.05)
            return g

PART 366-380 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN EXTENDED TECHNIQUE COVERAGE
================================================================================

Additional image-driven techniques that go beyond the basic 8
archetypes. Each PART here covers a specific technique with
image-driven emphasis.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 366-378 — IMPORTS FROM IMAGE TEXTURES (13 PROCEDURAL PATTERNS)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven procedural textures are derived from the reference
  image. PART 366-378 covers all 13 patterns (the 7 from PART 296
  + 3 micro-roughness + 4 height-to-X, plus image-driven variants).

  366 — NOISE FROM IMAGE GRADIENT
        from js import lbl, lblSpec
        def noise_from_image_gradient(ref_img):
            grad = lblSpec.helpers.imageGradient(ref_img)
            return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
                {"type": "noise", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "octaves": 4, "warpDirection": grad["dominant"]},
                size=512,
            )

  367 — VORONOI FROM IMAGE FEATURES
        def voronoi_from_image_features(ref_img):
            features = lblSpec.helpers.detectFeatures(ref_img)
            seeds = [lblSpec.helpers.imageToWorld(
                [f["x"], f["y"]], ref_img, [0, 0, 0], [0, 0, 1],
            ) for f in features]
            return lbl.cavity_aware_pattern(
                {"type": "voronoi", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "seeds": seeds},
                geom, size=256,
            )

  368 — BRICK FROM IMAGE EDGES
        def brick_from_image_edges(ref_img):
            edges = lblSpec.helpers.imageEdges(ref_img)
            angle = edges["dominant_angle"]
            return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
                {"type": "brick", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "rowCount": 8, "colCount": 16, "rowAngle": angle},
                size=512,
            )

  369 — STRIPE FROM IMAGE GRADIENT DIRECTION
        def stripe_from_image_gradient(ref_img):
            grad = lblSpec.helpers.imageGradient(ref_img)
            return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
                {"type": "stripe", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "count": 8, "angle": grad["dominant_angle"]},
                size=512,
            )

  370-373 — TRUCHET / CAIRO / SPLATTER FROM IMAGE
        def truchet_from_image(ref_img):
            return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
                {"type": "truchet", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "count": 8}, size=512,
            )
        # Same pattern for cairo + splatter

  374 — PUNCTATE FROM IMAGE NOISE
        def punctate_from_image_noise(ref_img):
            noise = lblSpec.helpers.imageNoise(ref_img)
            return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
                {"type": "punctate", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "density": noise["level"] * 64},
                size=256,
            )

  375 — STRIATE FROM IMAGE STREAK DIRECTION
        def striate_from_image_streak(ref_img):
            streak = lblSpec.helpers.imageStreak(ref_img)
            return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
                {"type": "stripe", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "count": 200, "angle": streak["angle"]},
                size=512,
            )

  376 — MICRO-ROUGHNESS FROM IMAGE
        def micro_roughness_from_image(ref_img):
            return lbl.multi_scale_pattern(
                {"type": "noise", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
                 "octaves": 4}, size=256,
            )

  377-378 — HEIGHT-TO-X FROM IMAGE
        def height_to_normal_from_image(ref_img):
            height_canvas = lblSpec.helpers.imageToHeightCanvas(ref_img)
            normal_canvas = height_to_normal(height_canvas, strength=1.0)
            return THREE.CanvasTexture.new(normal_canvas)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 379 — IMAGE-DRIVEN IMAGE-TO-3D PIPELINE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The complete image-to-3D pipeline in Python form:

       STAGES = [
           "image_drop",           # User drops image on page
           "load_ref_img",        # Renderer loads the image
           "ai_analyze",          # AI analyzes the image (PART 221-225)
           "ai_emit_factory",     # AI emits a .py factory
           "pyodide_load",        # Pyodide loads (cached after first)
           "factory_call",        # Factory is called with ref_img
           "validate",            # PART 165 + IoU check
           "add_to_scene",        # addTsModelToScene
           "lookdev_apply",       # MT_lookdev.applyLookDev
           "tick_register",       # userData.tick registered
           "render",              # Renderer's main loop
       ]

PART 381-420 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON LAYERED FORMS

PART 200-380 above covered the high-level structure and the basic
techniques. PART 381-420 here covers the DEEP forms — the detailed

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 381 — IMAGE-DRIVEN READING REFERENCE IMAGE (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The image-driven factory's first step is to read the reference
  image and extract the key information. The full process:

       # Step 1: Wait for the image to load (if it's a URL)
       if ref_img is None:
           # No image — fall back to prompt-only mode
           return create_model_prompt_only()
       if hasattr(ref_img, "complete") and not ref_img.complete:
           await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
       # Step 2: Get image dimensions
       width = ref_img.width
       height = ref_img.height
       # Step 3: Detect subject class (PART 344)
       subject = lblSpec.helpers.detectSubject(ref_img)
       # subject = "character" / "creature" / "vehicle" / etc.
       # Step 4: Sample palette
       palette = sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=6)
       # Step 5: Detect features
       features = lblSpec.helpers.detectFeatures(ref_img)
       # features = [{"name": "eye", "x": 0.5, "y": 0.2, ...}, ...]
       # Step 6: Measure proportions (imageToWorld)
       world_dims = lblSpec.helpers.measureDimensions(ref_img)
       # world_dims = {"width": 2.5, "height": 2.0, "depth": 1.5, ...}
       # Step 7: Detect style hints (PART 230)
       style_field = lblSpec.helpers.imageToStyle(ref_img)
       # style_field = {"mood": "metallic", "brightness": 0.7, ...}
       # Step 8: Pick archetype recipe
       recipe = ARCHETYPE_RECIPES[subject]
       # recipe = a factory builder function

  Each helper is documented in PART 380's index.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 382-388 — 7 IMAGE-DRIVEN READING HELPERS (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Each image-reading helper has its own PART here:

  PART 382 — sampleImagePalette(ref_img, n_colors)
      Returns: [{"name": "c0", "color": "#rrggbb",
                 "rgb": [r, g, b], "lab": [l, a, b],
                 "weight": 0..1, ...}, ...]
      Top-1 = dominant color (largest weight)
      The palette is sorted by weight, descending.
      n_colors default = 5.

  PART 383 — detectFeatures(ref_img)
      Returns: [{"name": "eye", "x": 0..1, "y": 0..1,
                 "strength": 0..1, "size": 0..1, ...}, ...]
      "name" is one of: eye, mouth, door, window, handle,
      wheel, head, body, leg, arm, antenna, fin, etc.
      "x"/"y" are normalized (0..1) within the image.
      "strength" is the detection confidence.
      "size" is the bounding box size (normalized).

  PART 384 — measureDimensions(ref_img)
      Returns: {"width": world_units, "height": world_units,
                "depth": world_units, "scale_factor": 1.0,
                "subject_bbox": [x0, y0, x1, y1]}
      The dimensions are in world units (default: meters).
      The subject_bbox is the bounding box of the detected
      subject within the image.
      Use these dimensions for the model's footprint_size.

  PART 385 — detectSubject(ref_img)
      Returns: "character" / "creature" / "vehicle" /
               "architecture" / "weapon" / "plant" /
               "furniture" / "prop"
      Used for archetype selection (PART 344).

  PART 386 — imageToStyle(ref_img)
      Returns: {"mood": ..., "brightness": 0..1,
                "saturation": 0..1, "keyColors": [...],
                "matchedKeywords": [...]}
      Drives meta.style selection.

  PART 387 — imageGradient(ref_img)
      Returns: {"dominant": 0..2pi, "magnitude": 0..1,
                "secondDominant": 0..2pi}
      Drives warp direction for procedural textures.

  PART 388 — imageHash(ref_img)
      Returns: an integer (deterministic per image content).
      Use this for the procedural texture seed.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 389-396 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON FACTORY LIFECYCLE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 389 — IMAGE-DROP-TRIGGERED FACTORY LOAD
        # The renderer's image-drop handler:
        async function onImageDrop(ref_img) {
            const factory_url = await aiOutputFile(model);
            // Load the factory .py file
            const factory_src = await fetch(factory_url).then(r=>r.text());
            // Load Pyodide if not cached
            await loadPyodideIfNeeded();
            // Run the factory with the image
            const pyResult = await runPyAsync(factory_src + `
                create_model(${ref_img_to_py_repr(ref_img)})
            `);
            // pyResult is a PyProxy of the THREE.Group
            const group = pyResult.toJs();
            // Add to scene
            scene.add(group);
            // Apply lookdev
            MT_lookdev.applyLookDev(group);
            // Register tick
            if (group.userData.tick) {
                ticker.add(group.userData.tick);
            }
        }

  PART 390 — FACTORY REFLEARNING (RETRAIN)
        # When the AI regenerates the factory, the renderer:
        # 1. Fetches the new factory source
        # 2. Diffs against the cached version
        # 3. Re-runs only if the diff is non-trivial
        # 4. Validates the new model against the reference image

  PART 391 — CACHING POLICY
        # The renderer caches:
        # - Pyodide runtime (10 MB compressed)
        # - lbl helper module (loaded once per session)
        # - MT bundle (loaded once per session)
        # - Factory source code (instant for repeat loads)
        # - Model result (cached per factory URL)
        # - Procedural textures (cached per seed)

  PART 392 — IoU RETRY LOOP (FULL FORM)
        from js import lblSpec
        # PART 254 IoU retry loop
        async def create_model_with_iou_retry(ref_img):
            g = build_model(ref_img)
            if ref_img:
                for attempt in range(3):
                    iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                    if iou >= 0.80:
                        break
                    # Adjust and retry
                    if attempt == 0:
                        g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
                    elif attempt == 1:
                        g.scale.set(0.9, 0.9, 0.9)
                    # attempt == 2: just return (last attempt)
                print(f"Final IoU: {iou}")
            return g

  PART 393-396 — RESERVED
        Reserved for future image-driven features.

PART 397-410 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MT BUNDLE EXAMPLES (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Every MT bundle helper, with an image-driven example.

  PART 397 — MT.core.makePrimitive (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven factory for a low-poly mushroom:
        def create_mushroom(ref_img=None):
            palette = sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=2) if ref_img else []
            lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})
            cap = THREE.Mesh.new(
                MT_core.makePrimitive("sphere", {
                    "r": 0.4, "ws": 12, "hs": 6,
                    "phiLength": 2 * math.pi, "thetaLength": math.pi / 2,
                }),
                _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                    "color": 0xcc3333, "roughness": 0.6, "flatShading": True,
                }),
            )
            cap.position.y = 0.3
            cap.castShadow = True
            cap.name = "cap"
            g.add(cap)
            stem = THREE.Mesh.new(
                MT_core.makePrimitive("cylinder", {
                    "r1": 0.10, "r2": 0.13, "h": 0.4,
                    "radial": 8, "height": 1,
                }),
                _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                    "color": 0xf4f0e6, "roughness": 0.8, "flatShading": True,
                }),
            )
            stem.position.y = 0.0
            stem.castShadow = True
            stem.name = "stem"
            g.add(stem)
            return g

  PART 398 — MT.core.loft (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven tapered limb:
        def create_lofted_limb(ref_img, segments):
            radius_pattern = lblSpec.helpers.imageTaper(ref_img)
            # radius_pattern = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.1}, ...]
            return MT_core.loft(radius_pattern, {"radial": 8})

  PART 399 — MT.core.sdfUnion (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven blob merging:
        def create_image_blob(ref_img):
            features = lblSpec.helpers.detectFeatures(ref_img)
            # Each feature becomes an SDF sphere
            sdfs = []
            for f in features:
                world_pos = lblSpec.helpers.imageToWorld(
                    [f["x"], f["y"]], ref_img, [0, 0, 0], [0, 0, 1],
                )
                radius = f["size"] * 0.3
                sdfs.append(MT_core.sdfSphere(world_pos[:3], radius))
            # Merge all SDFs
            sdf = sdfs[0]
            for s in sdfs[1:]:
                sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(sdf, s)
            return MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})

  PART 400 — MT_core.sdfMarch (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Same as PART 399 but with detail:
        # Higher resolution for smoother surfaces:
        mesh = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {
            "resolution": 128,  # 2x default
            "padding": 0.1,     # bounding box padding
            "maxIterations": 100,  # convergence limit
        })

  PART 401-403 — MT_core.csgUnion / Extrude / Lathe (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # csgUnion: image-driven boolean
        # Extrude: image-driven panel (PART 244)
        # Lathe: image-driven rotational
        # See PART 244 for the patterns.

  PART 404 — MT_meshes.subdivide (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven low-to-high boost:
        mesh = MT_core.makePrimitive("sphere", {"r": 1.0, "ws": 12, "hs": 8})
        high_mesh = MT_meshes.subdivide(mesh, {"levels": 2})
        # Add noise to make the surface irregular:
        high_mesh = MT_meshes.addNoise(high_mesh, {"amplitude": 0.05,
                                                    "scale": 2.0})

  PART 405 — MT_meshes.decimate (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven high-to-low:
        mesh = ...  # 50000-tri source
        chunky = MT_meshes.decimate(mesh, {"ratio": 0.05, "method": "qem"})
        # 2500-tri chunky mesh

  PART 406 — MT_meshes.bend / taper / twist (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven procedural deformation:
        curved = MT_meshes.bend(mesh, {
            "axis": "z", "angle": 0.5,
            "pivotPoint": [0, 0, 0],
        })
        tapered = MT_meshes.taper(mesh, {
            "axis": "y", "factor": 0.7,
        })

  PART 407 — MT_meshes.sdfBoolean (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # CSG with image-driven operands:
        box_geo = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(1, 1, 1)
        sphere_geo = THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.7)
        combined = MT_meshes.sdfBoolean({
            "operations": [
                {"type": "union", "a": box_geo, "b": sphere_geo},
            ],
        })

  PART 408 — MT_advanced.makeInstanced (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven instancing for repeated details:
        rivet_geo = THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.02, 8, 6)
        rivets = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(rivet_geo, 12, {
            "transforms": [
                {"position": [x, y, z], "rotation": [0, 0, 0],
                 "scale": [1, 1, 1]}
                for x, y, z in compute_rivet_positions(ref_img)
            ],
        })

  PART 409 — MT_materials.proceduralTextureCanvas (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven procedural texture:
        canvas = MT_materials.proceduralTextureCanvas(
            {"type": "noise", "seed": lblSpec.helpers.imageHash(ref_img),
             "octaves": 4},
            size=512,
        )
        tex = THREE.CanvasTexture.new(canvas)
        mat = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
            "color": 0xffffff, "map": tex,
        })

  PART 410 — MT_organic.smplBuild (IMAGE-DRIVEN FORM)
        # Image-driven skeletal subject:
        # Build a SMPL body from a humanoid reference image:
        body = MT_organic.smplBuild({
            "height": 1.8,
            "weight": 70,
            "muscleMass": 0.5,
            "bodyFat": 0.2,
            "pose": "standing",  # or "walking" / "sitting" / etc.
        })
        # Bind a skeleton to the mesh:
        rig = MT_organic.autoRig(body, {"skeletonType": "humanoid"})

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 411-420 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON COMMON MISTAKES (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 411 — MISTAKE M31 (NO IMAGE-AWARE PALETTE)
        SYMPTOM: model uses default colors regardless of image
        CAUSE: sampleImagePalette is not called or is called with
               ref_img=None
        FIX:
            palette = sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=6)
            for p in palette:
                if not lbl.define_palette(p["name"]):
                    lbl.define_palette(p)

  PART 412 — MISTAKE M32 (NO DIMENSIONAL MATCH)
        SYMPTOM: model has the wrong proportions for the image
        CAUSE: world_dims not used; magic numbers used instead
        FIX:
            world_dims = lblSpec.helpers.measureDimensions(ref_img)
            footprint_size = (world_dims["width"], world_dims["depth"])

  PART 413 — MISTAKE M33 (NO IoU CHECK)
        SYMPTOM: model looks fine but doesn't match the image
        CAUSE: IoU check is missing from the factory
        FIX:
            if ref_img:
                iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                if iou < 0.80:
                    # Adjust and retry
                    g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)

  PART 414 — MISTAKE M34 (NO sculptRuntime)
        SYMPTOM: renderer can't animate or inspect the model
        CAUSE: g.userData.sculptRuntime not set
        FIX:
            g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
                "nodes": {...},
                "meshes": {...},
                "detailInventory": [...],
            }

  PART 415 — MISTAKE M35 (NO tick)
        SYMPTOM: animated parts don't move
        CAUSE: g.userData.tick not set
        FIX:
            g.userData.tick = lambda dt, elapsed: (
                g.children[i].rotation.y += dt * 0.5
                for i in range(len(g.children))
            )

  PART 416 — MISTAKE M36 (Pyodide NEW ())
  PART 420-450 (v2.0.3) -- VFX / POST-FX / SHADER / PARTICLE / TRAIL SURFACE. See Prompt_To_Py.txt PART 420-450 for canonical documentation. The 11 VFX methods (lbl.outline_pass, lbl.post_fx, lbl.ibl_helmet, lbl.decal, lbl.shader_material, lbl.particle_system, lbl.trail, lbl.instanced_grass, lbl.bvh_raycast, lbl.sprite_sheet, lbl.lens_flare) close the gap between PART 28's PART 28 PART 290-291 advanced-shader references and a real, callable facade. See CHANGES_v202.md.
        SYMPTOM: Pyodide error "Three.Group is not a function"
        CAUSE: Using THREE.Group() instead of THREE.Group.new()
        FIX:
            g = THREE.Group.new()  # Pyodide idiom (not THREE.Group())

  PART 417 — MISTAKE M37 (MISSING IMPORT)
        SYMPTOM: NameError: name 'MT_core' is not defined
        CAUSE: forgot to import MT_core
        FIX:
            from js import MT_core, MT_materials, MT_advanced, THREE, lblSpec

  PART 418 — MISTAKE M38 (BAD MATRIX)
        SYMPTOM: deformations don't apply
        CAUSE: forgetting to set decompose matrix
        FIX:
            # Always call .updateMatrix() after position/rotation/scale
            mesh.position.set(1, 2, 3)
            mesh.updateMatrix()
            mesh.updateMatrixWorld()

  PART 419 — MISTAKE M39 (NO CASTSHADOW)
        SYMPTOM: model doesn't cast shadows
        CAUSE: forgot to set castShadow = True
        FIX:
            mesh.castShadow = True
            mesh.receiveShadow = True  # for floor parts

  PART 420 — MISTAKE M40 (MISSING DETAIL INVENTORY)
        SYMPTOM: validation warns "missing detail inventory entries"
        CAUSE: detailInventory doesn't match the actual meshes
        FIX:
            # Every Mesh in g.children should have a corresponding
            # entry in detailInventory, with matching name + priority.
            for n in g.children:
                assert n.name in [e["id"] for e in
                                  g.userData.sculptRuntime["detailInventory"]], \
                       f"Missing detail entry for {n.name}"

PART 421-440 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON RUNTIME CONTRACTS
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 421 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON RUNTIME CONTRACT (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The complete runtime contract for an image-driven Python factory:

       class ImageDrivenFactory:
           # Required methods
           def create_model(ref_img=None, options=None) -> THREE.Group:
               """Build the 3D model from an optional reference image."""
               ...

           # Optional methods (the renderer falls back to defaults)
           def get_meta() -> dict:
               """Return the model's metadata."""
               return {
                   "style": "chunky-mascot",
                   "category": "character",
                   "proportions": {...},
               }

           def validate(g, ref_img=None) -> list[str]:
               """Run validation checks. Return a list of warnings."""
               warnings = []
               if ref_img:
                   iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                   if iou < 0.80:
                       warnings.append(f"Low IoU: {iou}")
               return warnings

           def get_palette(ref_img=None) -> list[dict]:
               """Return the model's color palette."""
               if ref_img:
                   return sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=6)
               return DEFAULT_PALETTE

  ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Implementation example:
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────

       from js import MT_core, MT_materials, THREE, lblSpec
       import math

       class ChunkyRobotFactory:
           def create_model(self, ref_img=None, options=None):
               # ... full implementation ...
               return g

           def get_meta(self):
               return {
                   "style": "chunky-mascot",
                   "category": "character",
                   "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.8, "fill": 0.78},
               }

           def validate(self, g, ref_img=None):
               warnings = []
               if ref_img:
                   iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                   if iou < 0.80:
                       warnings.append(f"Low IoU: {iou}")
               return warnings

           def get_palette(self, ref_img=None):
               if ref_img:
                   return sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=4)
               return [
                   {"name": "shell",  "color": "#cc4444",
                    "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05},
                   {"name": "accent", "color": "#4a6fa5",
                    "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1},
                   {"name": "glow",   "color": "#ffaa00",
                    "emissive": "#ff7700", "emissiveIntensity": 2.0,
                    "roughness": 0.3},
               ]

       def create_model(ref_img=None, options=None):
           return ChunkyRobotFactory().create_model(ref_img, options)

PART 422-430 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON RUNTIME FIELDS
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Each field of the runtime contract:

  PART 422 — g.userData.sculptRuntime (FULL FORM)
        g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
            "version": "1.36",
            "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
            "meshes": {...},
            "detailInventory": [...],
            "skeleton": {...},  # optional
            "blendShapes": {...},  # optional
            "animClips": {...},  # optional
            "physics": {...},  # optional
            "audio": {...},  # optional
        }

  PART 423 — g.userData.meta (FULL FORM)
        g.userData.meta = {
            "version": "1.36",
            "style": "chunky-mascot",
            "category": "character",
            "proportions": {
                "W_over_H": 0.8,
                "fill": 0.78,
                "head_share_of_length": 0.3,
                "limb_count": 4,
                "joint_count": 8,
            },
            "lod": {
                "lods": [5000, 1500, 500, 150],
                "currentLod": 0,
            },
            "physics": {
                "rigid": True,
                "softBody": False,
                "constraints": [...],
            },
        }

  PART 424 — g.userData.tick (FULL FORM)
        # Per-frame animation callback
        def tick(dt, elapsed):
            # dt: time delta in seconds
            # elapsed: total elapsed time in seconds
            body = g.children[0]  # body
            body.rotation.y = math.sin(elapsed * 0.5) * 0.05
            # Animate the antenna tip
            tip = g.children[3]  # antenna_tip
            tip.material.emissiveIntensity = 2.0 + math.sin(elapsed * 4) * 0.5
        g.userData.tick = tick

  PART 425-430 — RESERVED FOR FUTURE USE

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 431-440 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON ERROR REFERENCE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 431 — PYODIDE LOAD ERROR
        CAUSE: Pyodide failed to load (network issue, MIME type, etc.)
        FIX: Check that Pyodide CDN is reachable. Fall back to
             TS-only mode.

  PART 432 — FACTORY IMPORT ERROR
        CAUSE: Python syntax error in the factory file
        FIX: Validate the factory with `python -m py_compile`.

  PART 433 — FACTORY CALL ERROR
        CAUSE: Runtime error during create_model()
        FIX: Check the traceback. Most common: missing import,
             Pyodide idiom (Three.Group.new()), missing argument.

  PART 434 — IoU LOW ERROR
        CAUSE: Model doesn't match the reference image (IoU < 0.80)
        FIX: PART 254 retry loop (PART 392). Otherwise, regenerate
             the factory with a different seed.

  PART 435 — VALIDATION WARNING
        CAUSE: Missing detailInventory entries, low triShare, etc.
        FIX: Add detailInventory entries. Reduce triShare on lower-
             priority meshes.

  PART 436 — SHADER COMPILE ERROR
        CAUSE: Shader fails to compile (vertex/fragment mismatch)
        FIX: Use only standard materials (MeshStandardMaterial,
             MeshPhysicalMaterial). Avoid custom shaders.

  PART 437 — GEOMETRY INVALID ERROR
        CAUSE: NaN or Inf in geometry vertices (degenerate CSG)
        FIX: Reduce the CSG operands' complexity. Use lower
             marching cubes resolution.

  PART 438 — MATERIAL MISSING ERROR
        CAUSE: Material is undefined when assigned to a mesh
        FIX: Always define materials before assignment.

  PART 439 — TEXTURE LOAD ERROR
        CAUSE: External texture URL is unreachable
        FIX: Fall back to procedural textures. Or use base64-encoded
             texture data.

  PART 440 — ANIMATION LOOP ERROR
        CAUSE: tick() throws an exception (IndexError, ZeroDivision)
        FIX: Wrap tick() in try/except. Log errors to console.

PART 501-520 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON MATERIAL GRAPH
================================================================================

The complete MT_materials.pbrMaterialGraph API for image-driven work.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 501-510 — PBR MATERIAL GRAPH NODES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 501 — albedo node
        node_id = "albedo"
        input: color
        output: color

  PART 502 — roughness node
        node_id = "roughness"
        input: scalar 0..1
        output: scalar

  PART 503 — metalness node
        node_id = "metalness"
        input: scalar 0..1
        output: scalar

  PART 504 — normal node
        node_id = "normal"
        input: vec3
        output: vec3

  PART 505 — ao node
        node_id = "ao"
        input: scalar 0..1
        output: scalar

  PART 506 — emissive node
        node_id = "emissive"
        input: color
        output: color

  PART 507 — texture sample node
        node_id = "tex2D"
        input: sampler2D + uv + vec2
        output: color

  PART 508 — multiply node
        node_id = "multiply"
        input: color * color / scalar * scalar
        output: color / scalar

  PART 509 — mix node
        node_id = "mix"
        input: float (factor) + a + b
        output: same as a, b

  PART 510 — power node
        node_id = "power"
        input: float (base), float (exponent)
        output: float

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 511-520 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MATERIAL GRAPH EXAMPLES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  PART 511 — IMAGE-DRIVEN METAL PAINT GRAPH
        graph = MT_materials.pbrMaterialGraph({
            "nodes": [
                {"id": "albedo", "type": "input", "value": "color"},
                {"id": "roughness", "type": "input", "value": "roughness"},
                {"id": "metalness", "type": "input", "value": "metalness"},
                {"id": "normal", "type": "input", "value": "normal"},
                {"id": "ao", "type": "input", "value": "ao"},
                {"id": "emissive", "type": "input", "value": "emissive"},
                # Map albedo * 0.8 for darker metal
                {"id": "darken", "type": "multiply",
                 "inputs": ["albedo", 0.8],
                 "output": "albedo"},
                # Add subtle emissive glow
                {"id": "glow", "type": "multiply",
                 "inputs": ["emissive", 0.1],
                 "output": "emissive"},
            ],
            "outputs": ["albedo", "roughness", "metalness",
                        "normal", "ao", "emissive"],
        })

  PART 512 — IMAGE-DRIVEN GLASS GRAPH
        graph = MT_materials.pbrMaterialGraph({
            "nodes": [
                {"id": "transmission", "type": "input", "value": "transmission"},
                {"id": "thickness", "type": "input", "value": "thickness"},
                {"id": "ior", "type": "input", "value": "ior"},
                {"id": "albedo", "type": "input", "value": "albedo"},
                {"id": "fresnel", "type": "power",
                 "inputs": ["albedo", 5.0],
                 "output": "albedo"},
                {"id": "tint", "type": "multiply",
                 "inputs": ["albedo", 0.3],
                 "output": "albedo"},
            ],
            "outputs": ["albedo", "transmission", "thickness", "ior"],
        })

       from js import MT_materials

           "uvIslands": [...],  # list of Mesh.geometry
           "atlasSize": 2048,
           "padding": 4,
           "method": "xatlas",  # or "stochastic" / "greedy"
       })

──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Triangulate a polygon mesh:

       from js import MT_meshes

           "method": "earclip",  # or "fan" / "constrained"
       })

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Convert triangles to quads:

       from js import MT_meshes
       quadified = MT_meshes.quadify(mesh, {
           "angleThreshold": 30,  # max angle for merging
           "method": "greedy",
       })

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Make all triangles uniform size:

       from js import MT_meshes
       remeshed = MT_meshes.isotropicRemesh(mesh, {
           "targetEdgeLength": 0.05,
           "iterations": 5,
       })

  Smooth mesh surface:

       from js import MT_meshes
       smoothed = MT_meshes.laplacianSmooth(mesh, {
           "iterations": 3,
           "lambda": 0.5,
       })

  Add surface detail from a texture:

       from js import MT_meshes
       displaced = MT_meshes.displaceSurface(mesh, {
           "texture": bump_texture,
           "amplitude": 0.05,
           "method": "linear",  # or "cubic" / "smooth"
       })

PART 541-580 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON COMPLETE API REFERENCE

The complete image-driven Python API surface. This section is the
authoritative source of truth for the image-driven factory API.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 541 — lbl MODULE API
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Functions exposed by the `lbl` Python helper module:

    lbl.name(name: str) -> None
    lbl.define_palette(palette: dict | list[dict]) -> None
    lbl.attach_box(name: str, footprint_size: tuple,
                   length: float = None, position: tuple = None,
                   material: str = None) -> dict
    lbl.attach_sphere(name: str, radius: float,
                      position: tuple = None,
                      material: str = None) -> dict
    lbl.attach_cylinder(name: str, radius1: float, radius2: float,
                        height: float, position: tuple = None,
                        material: str = None) -> dict
    lbl.attach_cone(name: str, radius: float, height: float,
                    position: tuple = None,
                    material: str = None) -> dict
    lbl.create_anchor(name: str, world_pos: tuple,
                      metadata: dict = None) -> dict
    lbl.define_joint(name: str, a: str, b: str,
                      pivot: tuple, axis: tuple = None) -> dict
    lbl.attach_image_plane(name: str, ref_img,
                           position: tuple = None) -> dict
    lbl.attach_csg_union(name: str, parts: list[dict],
                         position: tuple = None) -> dict
    lbl.blueprint() -> dict
    lbl.style_field(styles: list[dict]) -> dict
    lbl.multi_scale_pattern(params: dict, size: int = 256) -> Any
    lbl.cavity_aware_pattern(params: dict, geom,
                             size: int = 256,
                             curvatureMode: str = "both",
                             curvatureStrength: float = 0.5) -> Any
    lbl.geodesic_field(geom, anchors: list[tuple]) -> Any
    lbl.progressive_uv(geom, cameraDir: list, method: str = "planar",
                       axis: str = "y", atlasSize: int = 1024,
                       customFaceOrder: list = None) -> Any
    lbl.import_external_mesh(url: str, format: str = "glb") -> Any
    lbl.make_atlas_uv(geom, atlasSize: int = 1024,
                      padding: int = 4) -> Any
    lbl.fast_curvature(geom) -> Any
    lbl.highpoly_pbr(color: int = 0x885533, roughness: float = 0.4,
                     metalness: float = 0.05,
                     clearcoat: float = 0.3,
                     envMapIntensity: float = 1.0) -> Material
    lbl.photogrammetry_pbr() -> Material
    lbl.subdivide_for_highpoly(mesh, params: dict = None) -> Mesh
    lbl.decimate_for_lowpoly(mesh, params: dict = None) -> Mesh
    lbl.make_image_texture(url: str) -> Texture

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 542 — MT.core API
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Functions exposed by `MT.core`:

    MT.core.makePrimitive(type: str, params: dict) -> Geometry
        type = "box" / "sphere" / "cylinder" / "cone" /
               "torus" / "capsule" / "icosahedron" / "tetrahedron" /
               "octahedron" / "dodecahedron" / "plane" / "ring" /
               "tube" / "lathe" / "extrude"

    MT.core.bmeshFromGeometry(geom) -> BMesh

    MT.core.csgUnion(a: Geometry, b: Geometry) -> Geometry
    MT.core.csgDifference(a: Geometry, b: Geometry) -> Geometry
    MT.core.csgIntersection(a: Geometry, b: Geometry) -> Geometry

    MT.core.sdfSphere(center: tuple, radius: float) -> SDF
    MT.core.sdfBox(center: tuple, halfExtents: tuple) -> SDF
    MT.core.sdfCapsule(a: tuple, b: tuple, radius: float) -> SDF
    MT.core.sdfCylinder(center: tuple, radius: float,
                       height: float) -> SDF
    MT.core.sdfTorus(center: tuple, majorRadius: float,
                     minorRadius: float) -> SDF

    MT.core.sdfUnion(*sdfs: SDF) -> SDF
    MT_core.sdfSubtraction(a: SDF, b: SDF) -> SDF
    MT_core.sdfIntersection(a: SDF, b: SDF) -> SDF
    MT_core.sdfSmoothUnion(a: SDF, b: SDF, k: float = 0.1) -> SDF
    MT_core.sdfScale(sdf: SDF, scale: float) -> SDF

    MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf: SDF, params: dict = None) -> Geometry
        params:
            resolution: int = 64
            padding: float = 0.1
            maxIterations: int = 100
            method: str = "cubes"  # or "tetrahedra"

    MT_core.loft(profile: list[dict], params: dict = None) -> Geometry
        profile[i] = {"t": 0..1, "radius": float}
        params:
            radial: int = 8
            length: int = 1
            closed: bool = False
            taper: bool = False
            twist: float = 0

    MT_core.makeAtlasUV(manifest: dict) -> Any
    MT_core.makeHighPolySurface(...) -> Any
    MT_core.makeInstanced(geom, count: int, params: dict) -> Mesh
    MT_core.buildClothWorld(params: dict) -> Any
    MT_core.generateLOD(geom, params: dict) -> list[Mesh]
    MT_core.geoNodesEvaluate(nodeGraph: dict, params: dict) -> Any
    MT_core.physicsStep(world: dict) -> dict

PART 553 — THREE BRIDGE API

  All standard Three.js classes and properties are accessible via
  the `from js import THREE` bridge. Use THREE.ClassName.new() to
  create instances (Pyodide idiom).

PART 555-580 — RESERVED FOR FUTURE API

================================================================================
PART 581-620 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON COMPLETE WORKED EXAMPLES
================================================================================

Six complete worked examples covering the most common image-driven
use cases. Each example includes the full factory source, the
expected behavior, and the validation pipeline.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 581 — EXAMPLE 1: IMAGE-DRIVEN LOW-POLY CHARACTER (FULL)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_character.py — image-driven Style B factory.
  Builds a chunky-mascot character from a reference image.

  Usage:
    - Save as public/models/chunky_character.py
    - Renderer auto-discovers and registers it
    - Drop a reference image on the page to use it
    - Or use without an image for default character
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_materials, MT_advanced, MT_lookdev, THREE, lblSpec, lbl
  import math

  def create_model(ref_img=None, options=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Character"

      # ── Palette derivation ──
      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=4) if ref_img
          else DEFAULT_PALETTE
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      # ── Materials ──
      shell = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xcc4444, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05,
          "flatShading": True,
      })
      accent = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x4a6fa5, "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1,
          "flatShading": True,
      })

      # ── Body (SDF merged) ──
      sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
          MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.4, 0], 0.3),
          MT_core.sdfCapsule([0, 0.0, 0], [0, 0.3, 0], 0.2),
      )
      body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, shell)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # ── Head, eyes, antenna ──
      head = THREE.Mesh.new(
          THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.4, 0.35, 0.4), accent,
      )
      head.position.y = 0.75
      head.castShadow = True
      head.name = "head"
      g.add(head)
      for i, x in enumerate((-0.08, 0.08)):
          eye = THREE.Mesh.new(
              THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.05, 8, 6),
              _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                  "color": 0xffffff,
                  "emissive": 0xaef5ff, "emissiveIntensity": 1.5,
              }),
          )
          eye.position.set(x, 0.78, 0.21)
          eye.name = f"eye_{i}"
          g.add(eye)

      # ── Arms (lofted) ──
      arm_profile = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.07},
                     {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.05},
                     {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.04}]
      arm_geo = MT_core.loft(arm_profile, {"radial": 8})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          arm = THREE.Mesh.new(arm_geo, shell)
          arm.position.set(side * 0.25, 0.35, 0)
          arm.castShadow = True
          arm.name = f"arm_{side}"
          g.add(arm)

      # ── Legs (lofted) ──
      leg_profile = [{"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.08},
                     {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.06},
                     {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05}]
      leg_geo = MT_core.loft(leg_profile, {"radial": 8})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          leg = THREE.Mesh.new(leg_geo, shell)
          leg.position.set(side * 0.12, -0.4, 0)
          leg.castShadow = True
          leg.name = f"leg_{side}"
          g.add(leg)

      # ── sculptRuntime ──
      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "torso", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "eye_0", "region": "face", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "eye_1", "region": "face", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "arm_-1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "arm_1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "leg_-1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "leg_1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "character",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.8, "fill": 0.78},
      }

      # ── IoU check (image-driven only) ──
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

  DEFAULT_PALETTE = [
      {"name": "shell", "color": "#cc4444",
       "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05},
      {"name": "accent", "color": "#4a6fa5",
       "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1},
      {"name": "glow", "color": "#ffaa00",
       "emissive": "#ff7700", "emissiveIntensity": 2.0,
       "roughness": 0.3},
  ]

  def sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=5):
      """Sample dominant colors from the reference image."""
      return lblSpec.helpers.sampleImagePalette(ref_img, n_colors=n_colors)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 582 — EXAMPLE 2: IMAGE-DRIVEN VEHICLE (FULL)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_sedan.py — image-driven Style B factory for vehicles.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_materials, MT_hardsurface, MT_lookdev, THREE, lblSpec, lbl
  import math

  def create_model(ref_img=None, options=None):
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Sedan"

      palette = (
          sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=4) if ref_img
          else DEFAULT_VEHICLE_PALETTE
      )
      lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

      paint = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0xcc4444, "roughness": 0.4, "metalness": 0.3,
          "clearcoat": 0.7, "clearcoatRoughness": 0.1,
      })
      tire = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
          "color": 0x1a1a1e, "roughness": 0.95, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      glass = _lbl_three_new("MeshPhysicalMaterial", {
          "color": 0x90c0e0, "transmission": 0.85, "thickness": 0.05,
          "roughness": 0.05, "transparent": True, "opacity": 0.5,
      })

      # Body
      body_shape_2d = THREE.Shape.new()
      body_shape_2d.moveTo(-1.0, 0)
      body_shape_2d.lineTo(1.0, 0)
      body_shape_2d.bezierCurveTo(1.05, 0.2, 1.0, 0.45, 0.7, 0.5)
      body_shape_2d.lineTo(-0.7, 0.5)
      body_shape_2d.bezierCurveTo(-1.0, 0.5, -1.05, 0.2, -1.0, 0)
      body_shape_2d.closePath()
      body_geo = _lbl_three_new("ExtrudeGeometry", body_shape_2d, {
          "depth": 0.8, "bevelEnabled": True,
          "bevelSize": 0.04, "bevelThickness": 0.04, "bevelSegments": 4,
      })
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, paint)
      body.position.set(0, 0.3, 0)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.receiveShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Cabin
      cabin_shape = THREE.Shape.new()
      cabin_shape.moveTo(-0.6, 0)
      cabin_shape.lineTo(0.5, 0)
      cabin_shape.lineTo(0.4, 0.3)
      cabin_shape.lineTo(-0.5, 0.3)
      cabin_shape.closePath()
      cabin_geo = _lbl_three_new("ExtrudeGeometry", cabin_shape, {
          "depth": 0.7, "bevelEnabled": True,
          "bevelSize": 0.02, "bevelThickness": 0.02, "bevelSegments": 2,
      })
      cabin = THREE.Mesh.new(cabin_geo, paint)
      cabin.position.set(-0.05, 0.78, -0.05)
      cabin.castShadow = True
      cabin.name = "cabin"
      g.add(cabin)

      # Glass
      glass_geo = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.85, 0.25, 0.65)
      glass_mesh = THREE.Mesh.new(glass_geo, glass)
      glass_mesh.position.set(-0.05, 0.85, 0)
      glass_mesh.name = "windows"
      g.add(glass_mesh)

      # Wheels (4 lathed)
      wheel_geo = THREE.LatheGeometry.new(
          [THREE.Vector2.new(0.15, 0),
           THREE.Vector2.new(0.18, 0),
           THREE.Vector2.new(0.18, 0.13),
           THREE.Vector2.new(0.15, 0.13),
           THREE.Vector2.new(0.15, 0)],
          16,
      )
      wheel_positions = [
          (-0.7, 0.18, 0.4),
          (-0.7, 0.18, -0.4),
          (0.7, 0.18, 0.4),
          (0.7, 0.18, -0.4),
      ]
      for i, (x, y, z) in enumerate(wheel_positions):
          wheel = THREE.Mesh.new(wheel_geo, tire)
          wheel.position.set(x, y, z)
          wheel.castShadow = True
          wheel.name = f"wheel_{i}"
          g.add(wheel)

      # Headlights
      for i, x in enumerate((0.95, 0.95)):
          head = THREE.Mesh.new(
              THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.05, 8, 6),
              _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
                  "color": 0xffffaa,
                  "emissive": 0xffffaa,
                  "emissiveIntensity": 2.0,
              }),
          )
          head.position.set(x, 0.4, (i * 2 - 1) * 0.3)
          head.name = f"headlight_{i}"
          g.add(head)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "cabin", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "windows", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "wheel_0", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "wheel_1", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "wheel_2", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "wheel_3", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "vehicle",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.5, "fill": 0.7,
                          "wheel_radius_over_H": 0.15},
      }
      if ref_img:
          iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
          if iou < 0.80:
              g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
      return g

  DEFAULT_VEHICLE_PALETTE = [
      {"name": "paint", "color": "#cc4444", "roughness": 0.4,
       "metalness": 0.3, "clearcoat": 0.7, "clearcoatRoughness": 0.1},
      {"name": "tire", "color": "#1a1a1e", "roughness": 0.95,
       "metalness": 0.0},
      {"name": "glass", "color": "#90c0e0", "transmission": 0.85,
       "thickness": 0.05, "roughness": 0.05, "transparent": True,
       "opacity": 0.5},
  ]

  def sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=5):
      return lblSpec.helpers.sampleImagePalette(ref_img, n_colors=n_colors)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 621-700 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON FULL SPEC BODY
================================================================================

This section contains the full Image_To_Py.txt spec body, in the
same format as PART 223-290 in Prompt_To_Py.txt. Each PART covers
a major technique with image-driven emphasis.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 621 — IMAGE READING PATTERN (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 5-step image reading pattern:

  1. LOOK — survey the image as a whole. Note the dominant
     subjects, the silhouette, the lighting, the overall mood.
  2. SHAPE — identify the major shapes. Are they simple
     primitives? Curved fairings? Sculpted organic?
  3. COLOR — sample the dominant colors. Note the saturation,
     the value range, the metallic cues.
  4. MEASURE — estimate the proportions. How tall is it? How
     wide? How deep? Where are the joints?
  5. STYLE — assess the style hint. Is it matte? Glossy?
     Iridescent? Wireframe? Photogrammetry?

  Output: a structured plan (in g.userData.meta and the
  sculptRuntime contract) that drives the build.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 622 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 6-PRIMITIVE VOCABULARY (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories reach for these 6 primitives most often:

  1. BOX — for image-aligned structural parts (walls, body, head)
  2. SPHERE — for image-aligned round shapes (eyes, lights, fruit)
  3. CYLINDER — for image-aligned cylindrical parts (limbs, towers)
  4. CONE — for image-aligned tapering parts (antennas, spires)
  5. CAPSULE — for image-aligned organic parts (body, torso)
  6. ICOSAHEDRON — for image-aligned faceted parts (rocks, gems)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 623 — IMAGE-DRIVEN SDF + MARCH (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Use SDF + Marching Cubes for:
    - Image-aligned organic merging (torso + head as one mesh)
    - Image-aligned boolean shapes (subtract eye sockets from head)
    - Image-aligned continuous surfaces (no visible seams)

  Trade-offs:
    - PRO: watertight, no gaps, perfect for chunky-mascot
    - CON: slower than MT-mesh primitives, higher tri count

  Pattern:
    sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(sdf_a, sdf_b)
    geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 624 — IMAGE-DRIVEN LOFT (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Use Loft for:
    - Image-aligned tapered limbs (arms, legs, antennae)
    - Image-aligned organic tapering (snake, vine, tentacle)
    - Image-aligned curved surfaces (water flow, hair)

  Pattern:
    profile = [
        {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.07},  # shoulder
        {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.05},  # elbow
        {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.04},  # wrist
    ]
    geo = MT_core.loft(profile, {"radial": 8})

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 625 — IMAGE-DRIVEN EXTRUDE (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Use ExtrudeGeometry for:
    - Image-aligned panels (vehicle body, wall, sword blade)
    - Image-aligned shapes with depth (2D silhouette → 3D)

  Pattern:
    shape = THREE.Shape.new()
    shape.moveTo(-1.0, 0)
    shape.lineTo(1.0, 0)
    shape.bezierCurveTo(1.05, 0.2, 1.0, 0.45, 0.7, 0.5)
    shape.lineTo(-0.7, 0.5)
    shape.closePath()
    geo = _lbl_three_new("ExtrudeGeometry", shape, {
        "depth": 0.8, "bevelEnabled": True,
        "bevelSize": 0.04, "bevelThickness": 0.04,
    })

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 626 — IMAGE-DRIVEN LATHE (FULL FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Use LatheGeometry for:
    - Image-aligned rotational shapes (wheel, vase, column)
    - Image-aligned bottles, urns, hourglasses

  Pattern:
    pts = [
        THREE.Vector2.new(0.15, 0),
        THREE.Vector2.new(0.18, 0),
        THREE.Vector2.new(0.18, 0.13),
        THREE.Vector2.new(0.15, 0.13),
        THREE.Vector2.new(0.15, 0),
    ]
    geo = THREE.LatheGeometry.new(pts, 16)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 627 — IMAGE-DRIVEN CASTSHADOW + RECEIVESHADOW
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories should always set:

    mesh.castShadow = True
    mesh.receiveShadow = True  # for floor parts

  Default: all meshes cast shadows except light sources, particle
  systems, and transparent meshes (which are configured to
  castShadow = False for performance).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 628 — IMAGE-DRIVEN MATERIAL DICTIONARY
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories should use these materials in order of
  preference:

    1. MeshStandardMaterial (most cases)
    2. MeshPhysicalMaterial (transmission, iridescence, clearcoat)
    3. MeshLambertMaterial (no PBR needed)
    4. MeshBasicMaterial (no lighting, debug)
    5. ShaderMaterial (custom VFX, rare)

  Avoid: MeshPhongMaterial, MeshToonMaterial (deprecated)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 629 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 8 COMMON SHAPES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 8 shapes that appear in most image-driven factories:

    1. Body (capsule + sdfUnion)
    2. Head (box)
    3. Eyes (2 spheres)
    4. Limbs (4 lofted limbs)
    5. Antenna (cylinder + sphere tip)
    6. Wheels (lathe geometry, 4 instances)
    7. Windows (transmission glass)
    8. Lights (emissive spheres)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 630 — IMAGE-DRIVEN 5 CATEGORIES OF DETAIL
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories should distribute detail across these
  5 categories:

    1. PRIMITIVE features (eyes, mouth, doors) — high priority
    2. STRUCTURAL features (walls, beams) — medium priority
    3. SECONDARY features (rivets, decals) — medium priority
    4. AMBIENT features (smoke, particles) — low priority
    5. ENVIRONMENTAL features (ground, sky) — low priority

  Apply 60/25/15 to PRIORITY/MEDIUM/LOW.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 631-640 — RESERVED FOR FUTURE TECHNIQUES

================================================================================
PART 641-700 — v1.36 / v8.27 IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON FINAL API REFERENCE
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 641 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON VALIDATION CHECKLIST
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Run this checklist before returning the model:

  [ ] g.userData.sculptRuntime is set (PART 207)
  [ ] g.userData.sculptRuntime["nodes"] has every named child
  [ ] g.userData.sculptRuntime["meshes"] has every Mesh child
  [ ] g.userData.sculptRuntime["detailInventory"] is non-empty
  [ ] g.userData.meta is set (PART 252)
  [ ] g.userData.meta.style is a valid value (PART 481-494)
  [ ] g.userData.meta.category is set (PART 344)
  [ ] g.userData.meta.proportions has at least W_over_H + fill
  [ ] Every Mesh has castShadow = True
  [ ] Every Mesh has a unique .name
  [ ] Every Mesh in detailInventory matches a real Mesh
  [ ] IoU check (if ref_img) returns >= 0.80
  [ ] No magic numbers (use bbox-derived values, PART 251)
  [ ] No bare primitives without context (PART 250)
  [ ] Bounding box aspect ratio matches image (if ref_img)
  [ ] No NaN or Inf in geometry vertices (PART 437)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 642 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON TEST CASES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The renderer runs these test cases on every factory:

    1. create_model(None) — should return a valid Group
    2. create_model(mock_ref_img) — should return a valid Group
    3. The result should pass validation (PART 641)
    4. The result should have IoU >= 0.80 against mock_ref_img
    5. The result should render without errors
    6. The result should not throw when tick is called
    7. The result should have at least 1 mesh with castShadow
    8. The result should have at least 1 entry in detailInventory
    9. The result should have valid meta.style
    10. The result should have valid meta.proportions

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 643 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON PERFORMANCE TARGETS
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories should hit these targets:

    - Pyodide load: 2-5s first time, <100ms cached
    - Factory load: <50ms (after Pyodide loaded)
    - Factory execution: <500ms (for typical 5000-tri model)
    - IoU check: <200ms
    - Total pipeline: <2s typical, <5s worst case

  If a factory exceeds these targets:
    - Reduce tri count (use Decimate)
    - Reduce shader complexity (use simpler materials)
    - Reduce draw calls (use Instancing)
    - Cache expensive operations (lbl.define_palette, etc.)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 644 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON COMPATIBILITY MATRIX
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Browser support for image-driven Python factories:

    Browser           Pyodide    Pyodide+WebGL   Notes
    ──────            ────────   ──────────────   ─────
    Chrome 90+        ✓          ✓                Best support
    Edge 90+           ✓          ✓                Chromium-based
    Firefox 90+       ✓          ✓                Slightly slower
    Safari 15+        ✓          ✓                WebGL2 required
    Mobile Safari     ✓          ✓                Limited memory
    Chrome Android    ✓          ✓                Recommended
    Samsung Internet  ✓          ✓                Chromium-based

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 645 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON CROSS-PLATFORM NOTES
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Image-driven factories run in:
    - Desktop browsers (Chrome, Edge, Firefox, Safari)
    - Mobile browsers (iOS Safari, Chrome Android)
    - WebViews (Electron, Cordova, Capacitor)
    - Embedded browsers (Qt WebEngine, Chromium Embedded)

  Common pitfalls:
    - WebGL2 not available (use WebGL1 fallback)
    - Pyodide SharedArrayBuffer requires CORS headers
    - Cross-Origin isolation may not be set (HTTP header required)
    - Mobile devices have less memory (limit tri count)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 646 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON FUTURE ROADMAP
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future features (post-v1.36):

    - WebGPU support (faster than WebGL2)
    - WASM compilation (faster than Pyodide)
    - Server-side rendering (offload to GPU server)
    - Real-time collaboration (multiple users editing one model)
    - AI-assisted refinement (the AI re-runs the factory)
    - Style transfer (apply the style of one image to another)
    - Mesh neural compression (use neural networks for LOD)
    - Procedural animation (AI generates motion)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 701 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON FACTORY TEMPLATE
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Copy-paste template for a new image-driven Python factory:

       """
       [FACTORY NAME] — image-driven [STYLE] factory.
       Archetype: [character / creature / vehicle / etc.]
       Style: [chunky-mascot / smooth-low-poly / voxel / etc.]
       """
       from js import (
           MT_core, MT_materials, MT_advanced, MT_lookdev,
           MT_hardsurface, MT_architectural,
           THREE, lblSpec, lbl,
       )
       import math

       def create_model(ref_img=None, options=None):
           g = THREE.Group.new()
           g.name = "[MODEL NAME]"

           # ── Palette ──
           palette = (
               sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=4) if ref_img
               else DEFAULT_PALETTE
           )
           lbl.define_palette({p["name"]: p for p in palette})

           # ── Materials ──
           shell = _lbl_three_new("MeshStandardMaterial", {
               "color": 0xffffff, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.05,
               "flatShading": True,
           })
           # ... more materials ...

           # ── Geometry ──
           # ... build the model ...

           # ── sculptRuntime + meta ──
           g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
               "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
               "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                          if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
               "detailInventory": [
                   # ... list of detail entries ...
               ],
           }
           g.userData.meta = {
               "style": "[STYLE]",
               "category": "[CATEGORY]",
               "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.8, "fill": 0.7},
           }

           # ── IoU check ──
           if ref_img:
               iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
               if iou < 0.80:
                   g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
           return g

       DEFAULT_PALETTE = [
           # ... default colors if no ref_img ...
       ]

       def sample_image_palette(ref_img, n_colors=5):
           return lblSpec.helpers.sampleImagePalette(ref_img, n_colors=n_colors)

PART 705 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON GLOSSARY
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Common terms:

    - Factory: a Python function that creates a 3D model
    - Reference image: an image used to drive the factory's design
    - Style A: Blueprint-return authoring style
    - Style B: THREE.Group authoring style
    - sculptRuntime: the runtime contract on g.userData
    - detailInventory: the list of feature entries
    - IoU: Intersection over Union — silhouette similarity score
    - MT bundle: the 44-helper modeling toolkit
    - Pyodide: the CPython-in-WebAssembly runtime
    - PART: a section number in the LBL spec

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 706 — IMAGE-DRIVEN PYTHON CONTACT + SUPPORT
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  For questions, issues, or contributions:

    - GitHub: https://github.com/rdjpublishers/low_poly_3d
    - Issues: https://github.com/rdjpublishers/low_poly_3d/issues
    - Discussions: https://github.com/rdjpublishers/low_poly_3d/discussions

  Style guide for new factories:

    - Use snake_case for factory file names
    - Use clear, descriptive names (chunky_robot.py, beetle.py)
    - Include the archetype + style in the file's docstring
    - Keep factory functions under 200 lines (split if longer)
    - Document every material + every Mesh in the docstring
