PART 385 — SUMMARY: WHAT CHANGED IN v2.0
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

For the AI prompt author, here is the absolute minimum you need to know:

1. Use Style B (THREE.Group return) -- NOT Style A.
2. Use the 10-slot palette system (lbl.build_materials + setPalette).
3. Use solid PBR materials -- NO CanvasTexture, NO document.createElement.
4. Use separate Mesh layers for patterns (stripes, decals).
5. Set userData.sculptRuntime via lbl.install_runtime.
6. Set userData.tick for any animation.
7. Set userData.setPalette + availablePalettes for hot-swap.
8. Use the new custom shape helpers (FilletedBox, LathedTire, etc.)
9. Use rigging helpers (skeleton, skinned_mesh) for characters.
10. Use animation helpers (build_walk_clip, damp_spring, mixer).
11. Run preflight_check before returning.
12. NEVER use color=0xffffff as a texture placeholder.

If you remember these 12 rules, you'll generate v2.0-compatible Python
factories. If you also use the worked examples in Model_10.py /
Model_11_Character.py / Model_12_House.py as patterns, you'll match the
TypeScript output quality.

================================================================================
END OF PART 360-385 (v2.0 upgrade documentation)
================================================================================

================================================================================
PART 360-385 — v2.0.0 PYTHON UPGRADE (DIAGNOSTIC FIX + TS PARITY)
================================================================================

This PART block is the v2.0 upgrade documentation, layered additively on
top of every PART above. It addresses the diagnostic issues found in
Deepseek diagnostic report (`deepseek_text_20261004_c51484.txt`) AND
brings the Python implementation to full parity with the TypeScript
implementation, per PART 200-302 of the original spec.

Companion files:
  • public/modeling/lbl_py.py — the upgraded Python helper (v2.0)
  • public/models/Model_10.py — worked supercar example
  • public/models/Model_11_Character.py — worked rigged character
  • public/models/Model_12_House.py — worked architectural example
  • CHANGES_v2_0.md — release notes
  • public/modeling/test_integration.py — standalone test suite
  • public/modeling/build_inlined.py — regenerates the inlined JS helper
  • public/modeling/patch_index.py — patches index.html

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 360 — DIAGNOSTIC FIX SUMMARY (MUST READ)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The Deepseek diagnostic identified two failure modes for the v1.36 Python
factory path. Both are FIXED in v2.0. New code MUST follow them.

PROBLEM 1 — WHITE MODELS (the lbl.paint_rectangle path)
  Cause: Used document.createElement("canvas") + THREE.CanvasTexture.
          Pyodide on mobile has getContext("2d") returning null.
          CanvasTexture silently fails → THREE falls back to material.color.
          Since materials had color=0xffffff placeholder, everything
          rendered as solid white.
  FIX:    Use lbl.make_texture(...) which builds THREE.DataTexture (no
          canvas). All materials are solid PBR (no color placeholders).

PROBLEM 2 — SCATTERED DOTS (the Style A lbl.attach_* path)
  Cause: Used lbl.attach_box / lbl.attach_cylinder (Style A).
          The lbl helper geometry attachment calls failed silently in this
          Pyodide build, producing degenerate flat geometry.
  FIX:    Use Style B (THREE.Group direct) — `from js import THREE`,
          `THREE.Mesh.new(...)`, `g.add(...)`. This is the default.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 361 — STYLE B IS THE DEFAULT (Critical rule)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

For all v2.0 Python factories, use Style B (THREE.Group return).

  ✓ DO use Style B:  return THREE.Group() with full sculptRuntime
  ✗ DO NOT use Style A: lbl.attach_box etc. (kept for backward compat)

Why: Style A had silent failures in Pyodide. Style B uses raw THREE.*
calls which always work. Style B also supports everything Style A does
plus animations, materials, textures, rigging, and runtime contracts.

The minimal Style B factory:

    from js import THREE
    def create_model():
        g = lbl.Group("MyModel")
        g.userData.meta = {"name": "My Model", "style": "smooth-low-poly",
                            "category": "prop", "subject": "..."}
        body = lbl.Mesh(lbl.Box(1, 1, 1), lbl.standard(color=0xff8000),
                        name="body", cast_shadow=True, receive_shadow=True)
        g.add(body)
        lbl.install_runtime(g, {"detailInventory": [
            {"id": "body", "region": "main", "kind": "feature",
             "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7}]})
        return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 362 — THE 10-SLOT PALETTE CONTRACT (Critical rule)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every model uses a 10-slot palette. The slots are:
  body       — main body paint
  stripe     — racing stripe / livery accent
  carbon     — aero parts (splitter, diffuser, wing)
  rim        — wheel rim metal
  tire       — tire rubber
  glass      — windows (transparent)
  headlight  — emissive headlight colour
  taillight  — emissive taillight colour
  chrome     — exhaust + trim
  accent     — brake calipers, badges

Each slot is a dict: {color, roughness, metalness, emissive, emissiveIntensity, opacity}

8 BUILT-IN PRESETS (always available):
  racing-red, midnight-blue, sunset-gold, neon-cyber,
  british-green, stealth-black, pearl-white, desert-sand

Usage:
  mats = lbl.build_materials("racing-red")
  body_mat = mats["body"]      # → MeshStandardMaterial with full PBR
  stripe_mat = mats["stripe"]
  # ... use these as the material for your body / stripe meshes

REGISTER A CUSTOM PALETTE:
  lbl.register_palette("candy-purple", {
      "body":   {"color": "#b020c0", "roughness": 0.25, "metalness": 0.45},
      "stripe": {"color": "#ff80f0"},
      "accent": {"color": "#ffff80", "emissive": "#ffff80",
                 "emissiveIntensity": 0.4},
      "rim":    {"color": "#d0a0ff"},
  })

DERIVE FROM A BASE HEX:
  lbl.derive_palette("my-blue", base="#1e3a8a")
  # Auto-generates body, stripe, accent, rim, glass from base hex.
  # headlight + taillight stay constant (white + red).

HOT-SWAP AT RUNTIME:
  car.userData.setPalette("neon-cyber")               # full swap
  car.userData.setPalette({"body": {"color": "#00ff80"}})  # partial swap
  car.userData.availablePalettes                       # list all 8+

PER-LOAD OPTIONS:
  def create_model(options=None):
      palette = (options or {}).get("palette", "racing-red")
      mats = lbl.build_materials(palette)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 363 — MATERIALS ARE SOLID PBR (NO color=0xffffff PLACEHOLDERS)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Always use solid PBR materials. NEVER use CanvasTexture or
document.createElement. If you need a pattern, layer it as a separate Mesh.

WRONG (legacy — DO NOT USE):
  canvas = document.createElement("canvas")
  ctx = canvas.getContext("2d")
  # ... draw pattern
  mat.map = THREE.CanvasTexture.new(canvas)

RIGHT (v2.0):
  stripe = lbl.Mesh(lbl.Box(2.0, 0.005, 0.10), stripe_mat, name="stripe_0")
  stripe.position.set(0.50, 0.495, 0.16)
  car.add(stripe)
  # OR for actual texture needs:
  tex = lbl.make_texture({"type": "noise", "seed": 1}, size=256)
  mat.map = tex  # DataTexture — works in Pyodide everywhere

PBR recipes:
  lbl.material("metal",   color="#cfd2d6", roughness=0.2, metalness=1.0)
  lbl.material("stone",   color="#888880", roughness=0.85)
  lbl.material("wood",    color="#6a4020", roughness=0.7, clearcoat=0.3)
  lbl.material("leather", color="#5a3010", roughness=0.6, clearcoat=0.4)
  lbl.material("cloth",   color="#a02020", roughness=0.85, sheen=0.4)
  lbl.material("skin_material", color="#e0ae87", clearcoat=0.1)
  lbl.material("velvet",  color="#600030", sheen=1.0, sheen_color="#ff80c0")
  lbl.material("chitin",  color="#3a5a40", iridescence=0.6, clearcoat=0.8)
  lbl.material("elytra",  color="#1a2810", iridescence=0.4, clearcoat=1.0)
  lbl.material("membrane", color="#f0d8d8", transmission=0.7, ior=1.4)
  lbl.material("emissive", base="#ffffff", emissive="#fffaec", intensity=2.0)

Raw factories:
  lbl.standard(color=0xff8000, roughness=0.5, metalness=0.0)
  lbl.physical(color=0xff8000, clearcoat=1.0, transmission=0.0)
  lbl.basic(color=0xffffff)              # unlit

CS2 PBR (PART 45 / 289):
  lbl.cs2_finish("#a04030", finish="case-hardened", wear="FT")
  # 8 finishes: anodized, anodized-multicolored, custom-paint, gunsmith,
  #             hydrographic, patina, solid-color, sprayed, damascus-steel
  # 5 wear tiers: FN / MW / FT / WW / BS

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 364 — GEOMETRY: USE THE CUSTOM SHAPE HELPERS
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Beyond the basic THREE primitives, v2.0 adds these custom shape helpers:

  lbl.FilletedBox(w, h, d, radius, smoothness)
      Rounded-box geometry (uses RoundedBoxGeometry from three/addons).
      Use for body, fenders, doors, panels — anything box-shaped that
      shouldn't have sharp edges.

  lbl.TaperedTube(points, start_radius, end_radius, segments)
      Tube with variable radius along its length.
      Use for: rocket nozzles, antennae, exhaust transitions.

  lbl.StarPattern(num_spokes, inner_radius, outer_radius, thickness)
      5-spoke alloy rim star (BoxGeometry-based).
      Use for: wheel rims, decorative star patterns.

  lbl.BeveledWasher(inner_radius, outer_radius, thickness, bevel)
      Chamfered washer (RingGeometry + ExtrudeGeometry).
      Use for: bolts, gaskets, decorative washers.

  lbl.LathedTire(outer_radius, inner_radius, width, shoulder_radius, segments)
      PART 179 — lathed toroidal tire. Fixes audit E41 ("flat cylinder tire").
      Use for: ALL tires (mandatory compliance).

  lbl.SweptTube(points, radius, radial_segments, tubular_segments)
      PART 179 — tube swept along a CatmullRomCurve3.
      Use for: exhaust headers, pipes, flexible tubes.

  lbl.ExhaustCanister(length, radius_top, radius_bottom, oval=True, ...)
      PART 179 — oval exhaust canister (lathed + non-uniform X scale).
      Use for: motorcycle/vehicle exhaust.

  lbl.Loft(profile_points, path_points, segments, closed_path=False)
      Cross-section lofted along a path (custom geometry).
      Use for: limbs, organic tapered surfaces, character body parts.

  lbl.Lathe(profile_points, segments)
      LatheGeometry — rotational surface from [x, y] profile.
      Use for: wheels, vases, table legs, anything rotationally symmetric.

  lbl.Extrude(shape, depth, bevel_enabled, ...)
      ExtrudeGeometry — 2D shape → 3D body.
      Use for: fairings, panels, shields, custom 2D profiles.

  lbl.CatmullRom(points, tension=0.5, closed=False)
      CatmullRomCurve3 — smooth spline through points.

  lbl.Bezier(p0, p1, p2, p3, segments=24)
      CubicBezierCurve3.

  lbl.Shape() / lbl.Path()
      THREE.Shape / THREE.Path for custom 2D profiles.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 365 — PROCEDURAL TEXTURES (DataTexture-only, no canvas)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

CRITICAL: Pyodide on mobile often has getContext("2d") returning null.
CanvasTexture silently fails → 3D models render as solid color fallbacks.
NEVER use document.createElement or THREE.CanvasTexture.

ALWAYS use lbl.make_texture(...) which builds THREE.DataTexture:

  tex = lbl.make_texture({"type": "noise", "seed": 1, "octaves": 4}, size=256)
  mat.map = tex

Available texture types:
  • "noise"        — value noise grayscale RGBA
  • "voronoi"      — Voronoi / Worley cells
  • "brick"        — running-bond brick
  • "splatter"     — random splat dots
  • "stripe"       — vertical/horizontal stripes
  • "truchet"      — Truchet curves
  • "voxel"        — chunky pixel art
  • "radial"       — radial gradient
  • "linear"       — linear gradient
  • "micro-rough"  — micro-roughness (for material.roughnessMap)

Convenience façade:
  lbl.make_noise_texture(size=256, seed=1)
  lbl.make_brick_texture(size=256, ...)
  lbl.make_splatter_texture(size=256, ...)
  lbl.make_micro_roughness_texture(size=256, seed=1)
  lbl.make_radial_gradient_texture(size=256, color1, color2)
  lbl.make_linear_gradient_texture(size=256, color1, color2, angle)
  lbl.make_voxel_texture(size=256, primary, secondary, accent, ...)
  lbl.make_voronoi_texture(size=256, cells=8, ...)
  lbl.make_truchet_texture(size=256, ...)
  lbl.make_stripe_texture(size=256, color1, color2, stripe_width, vertical)

For patterns that are too complex for textures (stripes, decals, badges),
add them as separate Mesh layers:

  stripe = lbl.Mesh(lbl.Box(0.70, 0.005, 0.10), stripe_mat, name="stripe_0")
  stripe.position.set(0.50, 0.495, 0.16)
  car.add(stripe)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 366 — RIGGING (SKELETON, BONES, SKINNING)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Build a skeleton + bind a mesh as a SkinnedMesh:

  from js import THREE

  def build_skeleton():
      pelvis = THREE.Bone.new(); pelvis.name = "pelvis"; pelvis.position.set(0, 0, 0)
      spine  = THREE.Bone.new(); spine.name  = "spine";  spine.position.set(0, 0.3, 0)
      chest  = THREE.Bone.new(); chest.name  = "chest";  chest.position.set(0, 0.6, 0)
      neck   = THREE.Bone.new(); neck.name   = "neck";   neck.position.set(0, 0.9, 0)
      head   = THREE.Bone.new(); head.name   = "head";   head.position.set(0, 1.1, 0)
      spine.add(chest)
      chest.add(neck)
      neck.add(head)
      pelvis.add(spine)
      skel = THREE.Skeleton.new([pelvis, spine, chest, neck, head])
      return {"root": pelvis, "bones": {b.name: b for b in [pelvis, spine, chest, neck, head]}, "skeleton": skel}

Or use the lbl helper:
  skel = lbl.skeleton([
      {"name": "pelvis", "restPosition": [0, 0, 0]},
      {"name": "spine",  "restPosition": [0, 0.3, 0], "parent": "pelvis"},
      {"name": "chest",  "restPosition": [0, 0.6, 0], "parent": "spine"},
      {"name": "neck",   "restPosition": [0, 0.9, 0], "parent": "chest"},
      {"name": "head",   "restPosition": [0, 1.1, 0], "parent": "neck"},
  ])

Bind a mesh as SkinnedMesh:
  skinned_mesh = lbl.skinned_mesh(geometry, material, skel)
  # Now you can rotate any bone to deform the mesh.

For simpler hierarchical rigs (no skinning), use Object3D nesting:
  head_pivot = lbl.Group("head_pivot")
  head_pivot.position.set(0, 1.1, 0)
  head_mesh = lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(0.24, 0.26, 0.24, 0.05, 3),
                       skin_mat, name="head")
  head_pivot.add(head_mesh)
  body.add(head_pivot)
  # Animate: head_pivot.rotation.y = sin(elapsed) * 0.3

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 367 — ANIMATION (CLIPS, MIXER, KEYFRAMES, DAMPED SPRINGS)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Build a procedural walk cycle:

  mixer = lbl.make_mixer(group)
  walk_clip = lbl.build_walk_clip(
      joint_paths=[
          ("arm_L_pivot", 0.0),        # phase offset 0
          ("arm_R_pivot", math.pi),    # phase offset π (opposite)
          ("leg_L_pivot", math.pi),
          ("leg_R_pivot", 0.0),
      ],
      duration=1.0, frequency=2.0, amplitude=0.4,
  )
  lbl.play_animation(mixer, walk_clip, fade_in=0.3)

For more control, build clips from keyframe tracks:

  times = [0, 0.5, 1.0]                        # 3 keyframes
  values = [0.0, 0.5, 0.0]                     # arm.rotation.x values
  track = lbl.number_keyframe_track("arm_L_pivot.rotation[x]", times, values)
  clip = lbl.make_clip("wave", 1.0, [track])
  action = lbl.play_animation(mixer, clip)

Damped spring for secondary motion (cape, tail, antenna):

  spring = lbl.damp_spring(target=0, k=80, c=8, mass=1.0)
  def tick(dt, elapsed):
      target = math.sin(elapsed * 1.5) * 0.3
      spring.target = target
      spring.step(dt)
      cape.rotation.x = spring.value

Sine wave / bounce helpers:

  val = lbl.sine_wave(t, amplitude=1.0, frequency=2.0, phase=0.0)
  val = lbl.bounce(t, amplitude=1.0, frequency=2.0)  # damped
  l, r = lbl.walk_phase(t, frequency=2.0, amplitude=0.4)  # L/R pair

Loop frame equality check (use before shipping):
  ok = lbl.loop_frame_equal([first_frame_values], [last_frame_values])

To also surface the in-app Animation Controls panel (play/pause/
next-clip button) — REQUIRED for any non-trivial image-driven model
that animates — publish the modern shape so the renderer's
lblCollectAnimations() picks the clips up:

  lbl.publish_animation(group, mixer=mixer, clips=[walk_clip, wave_clip])

This writes `group.userData.sculptRuntime.animations = { clips, mixer,
actions }` (PLURAL key) so the toggle button is enabled. If you also
called `lbl.install_runtime(group)` earlier, its default tick already
drives the mixer — you can skip writing your own `group.userData.tick`.

See Prompt_To_Py.txt PART 207.4-208.7 + PART 213.15 for the full
AnimationMixer contract (glTF channel-name translation, headless
playback verification, custom-runtime update(elapsed), the
singular-vs-plural trap, etc.).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 368 — SCULPT RUNTIME CONTRACT (mandatory for Style B)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every Style B Group MUST populate userData.sculptRuntime. The simplest
way is:

  lbl.install_runtime(group, {
      "subject": "supercar",                # what the model IS
      "style":   "stylized-lowpoly",        # which visual style
      "category": "vehicle",                # canonical category
      "detailInventory": [                  # named parts the inspector should highlight
          {"id": "lower_body", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
           "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
          {"id": "headlight_0", "region": "lights", "kind": "feature",
           "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
          # ... every named mesh should have a matching inventory entry
      ],
  })

install_runtime auto-populates:
  group.userData.sculptRuntime = {
      "subject", "style", "category",
      "nodes":   {name: Object3D, ...},
      "meshes":  {name: Mesh, ...},
      "materials": {name: Material, ...},
      "sockets": {},
      "colliders": {},
      "destructionGroups": {},
      "actions": {},
      "animations": {                              # PLURAL key —
                                                    # the renderer's
                                                    # lblCollectAnimations()
                                                    # reads this shape.
          "clips":   [],                           # populate via
          "mixer":   None,                         # lbl.publish_animation()
          "actions": {},
      },
      "animation": {},                             # singular, legacy
                                                    # diagnostic mirror
                                                    # — see PART 213.15
                                                    # in Prompt_To_Py.txt
      "vfx": {},
      "passes": {},
      "passesReviewed": {},
      "passesComplete": False,
      "fidelity": {"score": 0.0, "issues": []},
      "landmarks": [],
      "detailInventory": [...],
  }
  group.userData.tick           = placeholder tick (drives a published
                                                 mixer if one exists —
                                                 see PART 208 + PART
                                                 213.15)
  group.userData.setPalette     = lbl.palette.set (runtime hot-swap)
  group.userData.availablePalettes = list of registered palette names

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 368 SUB-SECTIONS (mirror of TS PART 6.3-6.7 + Prompt_To_Py.txt 207.2-207.4)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

These sub-sections document the OPTIONAL fields of the sculptRuntime
contract that the renderer's Inspector + Action Hooks panel + Gzmo + 
Fidelity Gate actively read. Mirror of TS PART 6.3-6.7 and Prompt_To_Py.txt
PART 207.2-207.4.

368.1 — SOCKETS (empty Object3Ds the host can reparent things to)
   (mirror of TS PART 6.3)

Sockets are placeholders — empty THREE.Object3D nodes with `.name`
set, a position to mark the attach point, and no children. The host
(and downstream code) re-parents other meshes into them. Add them to
the `sculptRuntime.sockets` map so the Inspector's "Parts" panel can
list them alongside meshes and nodes:

  group.userData.sculptRuntime["sockets"] = {
      "muzzle":         muzzle_socket,
      "top_rotor":      top_rotor_socket,
      "under_thruster": under_thruster_socket,
  }

A `THREE.Group` also works as a socket, but a bare `THREE.Object3D.new()`
is the right choice when the socket is a single empty placeholder (no
children of its own, no transform children).

368.2 — DESTRUCTION GROUPS + COLLIDERS (mirror of TS PART 6.4-6.5)

For breakable models (a building that shatters, a vehicle that breaks
apart on impact), group breakable pieces by subassembly:

  group.userData.sculptRuntime["destructionGroups"] = {
      "claws":  [forearm_shield_L, forearm_shield_R, claw_tip_L, claw_tip_R],
      "rotors": [top_rotor_hub, bottom_rotor_hub],
      "panels": [panel_1, panel_2, panel_3],
  }
  group.userData.sculptRuntime["colliders"] = {
      "body":     THREE.Box3.new().setFromObject(body_orb),
      "head":     THREE.Box3.new().setFromObject(head),
      "left_arm": THREE.Box3.new().setFromObject(left_arm),
  }

`colliders` may be any shape the host recognises — `Box3`, `Sphere`,
`Mesh.raycast`-compatible, etc. Populate at factory body the moment
meshes are positioned, NOT on demand.

368.3 — PER-MESH METADATA (mirror of TS PART 6.7)

For complex models with many named parts, the renderer reads four
OPTIONAL per-mesh metadata fields. None are required, but populating
them turns the Inspector from a passive tree into a live gizmo that
knows what each part IS and what you can do to it. Set them AFTER
positioning the node in the scene.

  # 368.3.1 — sculptComponent: identity + role + parent + dependent sockets
  body.userData.sculptComponent = {
      "id":       "body",
      "parentId": "root",
      "role":     "shell",     # see PART 6.7 in Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the full enum
      "sockets":  [],
  }

  # 368.3.2 — actionProfile: which 1-click actions are valid for this part
  wheel.userData.actionProfile = {
      "canSpin": True,  "canGlow": True,  "canFire": False,
      "canRecoil": False, "canEject": False, "canOpen": False,
      "canElectrify": False, "canJump": False, "canWiggle": False,
      "canSway": False, "canRecenter": True,
  }

  # 368.3.3 — animationRole: classify nodes for skeletal animation
  shoulder_L.userData.animationRole = "bone"
  ik_target_hand.userData.animationRole = "ik-target"

  # 368.3.4 — socketFor: trace parent ↔ child between a part and its socket
  muzzle_socket.userData.socketFor = "barrel"

Names must match the same casing convention as sculptRuntime
('camelCase' descriptive consistent — `'frontTire'` in the runtime
MUST be `'frontTire'` here).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 369 — TICK CONTRACT (mandatory for animated models)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every Style B Group that animates MUST set:

  def tick(dt, elapsed):
      # dt = seconds since last frame
      # elapsed = seconds since first frame
      # ... animate here

  group.userData.tick = tick

The renderer calls tick(dt, elapsed) every render frame.

Common patterns:
  • Wheel spin:    part.rotation.x += dt * speed
  • Walk cycle:    phase = elapsed * 2.0
                   arm_L.rotation.x = sin(phase) * 0.4
                   arm_R.rotation.x = sin(phase + math.pi) * 0.4
  • Engine idle:   car.position.y = sin(elapsed * 35) * 0.003
  • Steering:      steer_angle += (target - steer_angle) * dt * 4.0
  • Lean-in-turn:  lean_angle += (-steer * abs(speed) * 0.15 - lean) * dt * 2.0
                   car.rotation.z = lean_angle
  • Damped spring: spring.target = target_value
                   spring.step(dt)
                   part.rotation.x = spring.value

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 369 SUB-SECTIONS (mirror of TS PART 5 + Prompt_To_Py.txt PART 208)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

These sub-sections document the canonical AnimationMixer / tick
contract for image-driven Python factories. They mirror Prompt_To_Py.txt
PART 208.1-208.7 (which mirror TS PART 5.1-5.7) one-for-one. AI authors
generating .py files from a reference image MUST read these before any
non-trivial animation work.

369.1 — SIGNATURE (mirror of TS PART 5.1)
  def tick(dt, elapsed):
      # dt = seconds since the previous frame (use this for ALL time math)
      # elapsed = seconds since the renderer started (use for periodic effects)
      pass
  group.userData.tick = tick

Do NOT use `performance.now()` or `time.time()` for time math — the
renderer is in control of time, your factory is a passive consumer.
Use `dt` for decay, easing, and integration; use `elapsed` for sin/cos
periodic motion where the phase needs to be stable across pauses.

369.2 — STATE MACHINES (mirror of TS PART 5.2)
  state = {
      "recoil": 0.0, "flash": 0.0, "since_fire": 0.0,
  }
  FIRE_INTERVAL = 2.4

  def fire():
      state["recoil"] = 1.0
      state["flash"]  = 0.07

  def tick(dt, elapsed):
      state["since_fire"] += dt
      if state["since_fire"] >= FIRE_INTERVAL:
          fire()
      state["recoil"] = max(0.0, state["recoil"] - dt * 3.2)
      gun.position.z = -state["recoil"] * state["recoil"] * 0.1
  group.userData.tick = tick

369.3 — DISCRETE / SPAWNED ACTORS — pre-allocate the pool (TS PART 5.3)
NEVER call `THREE.Obj.new(...)` inside the tick. Pre-allocate a pool
and recycle slots:

  shells = []
  for _ in range(5):
      s = build_shell()
      s.visible = False
      group.add(s)
      shells.append({"group": s, "active": False, "vy": 0.0, "life": 0.0})

  def tick(dt, elapsed):
      for slot in shells:
          if not slot["active"]:
              continue
          slot["life"] -= dt
          if slot["life"] <= 0:
              slot["active"] = False
              slot["group"].visible = False
              continue
          slot["vy"] -= 9.8 * dt
          slot["group"].position.y += slot["vy"] * dt
  group.userData.tick = tick

369.4 — EASING (mirror of TS PART 5.4)
The `lbl` module ships:
  - lbl.damp_spring(target=..., k=80, c=8).step(dt)  — spring with ease
  - lbl.bounce(elapsed, dur)                         — bounce envelope
  - lbl.sine_wave(elapsed, amp, freq, phase, base)  — pure sinusoid

For idle motion (sway, breathing, blink, head-bob), use `elapsed`
directly with a slow sin/cos so the phase survives any pause:
  group.rotation.y = math.sin(elapsed * 0.3) * 0.28
  group.position.y = 0.55 + math.sin(elapsed * 0.9) * 0.01

369.5 — glTF CHANNEL-NAME TRANSLATION TABLE (CRITICAL — mirror of TS 5.6a)
When clips are built from keyframe data extracted out of a reference
.glb/.gltf file, the raw glTF JSON uses glTF's own channel target names:

    translation | rotation | scale

These are NOT the three.js Object3D property names. Using the glTF
path name verbatim as the track name does NOT throw, does NOT log a
warning, and the AnimationClip constructs and plays "successfully" —
the track simply never finds anything to write to, so the model sits
frozen. This is the single most common way a baked clip silently fails.

    glTF channel target      three.js KeyframeTrack target      Track type
    --------------------      -----------------------------      ----------
    translation           →   <node>.position                    VectorKeyframeTrack
    rotation               →   <node>.quaternion                  QuaternionKeyframeTrack
    scale                   →   <node>.scale                       VectorKeyframeTrack

  # ALWAYS pass gltf_path through an explicit lookup table first:
  GLTF_PATH_TO_THREE_PROPERTY = {
      "translation": "position",
      "rotation":    "quaternion",
      "scale":       "scale",
  }
  track_name = f"{node_name}.{GLTF_PATH_TO_THREE_PROPERTY[gltf_path]}"

A `rotation` channel MUST be constructed with `QuaternionKeyframeTrack`
(4 floats/key, XYZW) targeting `.quaternion` — never NumberKeyframeTrack
per-Euler-component targeting `.rotation[x/y/z]` when the source data
is already baked as quaternions. lbl.detect_animation_track_type()
(PART 389.1 in Prompt_To_Py.txt) classifies correctly when the track
name ends in `.quaternion`, so name the track correctly and the helper
picks the right class.

In Pyodide 0.26+ the borrowed proxy returned by `.new()` for
QuaternionKeyframeTrack / VectorKeyframeTrack / AnimationClip /
AnimationMixer is destroyed at the end of the next JS function call —
use `window.eval("new THREE.QuaternionKeyframeTrack(name, times, values)")`
instead (PART 213.14). lbl.quaternion_keyframe_track() / lbl.vector_keyframe_track()
/ lbl.make_clip() / lbl.make_mixer() already wrap the window.eval path,
so prefer the helpers over raw `THREE.X.new(...)`.

369.6 — HEADLESS PLAYBACK VERIFICATION BEFORE DELIVERY (mirror of TS 5.6b)
"The clip constructs without a NameError" is NOT evidence the animation
plays. Before treating any baked-animation .py file as done, run an
actual headless playback check (see public/modeling/test_integration.py
for the stub-harness pattern):

  1. Stub `document.createElement` and `from js import THREE` (the
     test_integration.py harness does this).
  2. Call create_model(), grab a node that a track targets via
     `group.getObjectByName('<NodeName>')`.
  3. Record that node's `.position` / `.quaternion` BEFORE ticking.
  4. Call `group.userData.tick(dt, elapsed)` (or drive the mixer
     directly via mixer.update(dt)) for ~30-90 simulated frames.
  5. Record the same node's `.position` / `.quaternion` AFTER, and
     assert it actually changed.

If the AFTER values equal the BEFORE values, the clip is NOT playing
— even though the clip, action, and mixer all "exist".

369.7 — Custom-runtime `update(elapsed)` (no tick, no mixer) — TS 5.7
Some models keep their animation driver on a custom runtime namespace
object (think: `group.userData.<myNamespace>`) and expose a
`.update(elapsed)` method on it — but DO NOT set `userData.tick`.
The renderer's auto-discovery path:

  # The renderer walks root.userData after mount, finds the first
  # object with both an `update(elapsed)` method AND a `_root`
  # back-reference to the model, and calls it every frame with the
  # renderer's elapsed time. No `root.userData.tick` needed.

If you go this route, set `_root` on the runtime so the DFS finds it:
  runtime = {
      "_root": group,
      "update": lambda elapsed: None,    # your animation logic here
  }
  group.userData.my_namespace = runtime

Pick ONE driver — the renderer skips the custom-runtime `update()`
when `userData.tick` exists.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
DOUBLE-DRIVE BUG WARNING (mirror of TS PART 6.1B NOTE)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

If you publish a mixer under `sculptRuntime.animations.mixer` AND also
call `mixer.update(dt)` from `userData.tick`, the clip plays at 2×
speed (two update paths touching one mixer). The factory's tick
should own the `mixer.update()` call; `sculptRuntime.animations.mixer`
is for the renderer's UI/inspector to read from, NOT a second place
to drive it from. `lbl.install_runtime(group)` installs a default
tick that already calls `mixer.update(dt)` when a mixer is published,
so if you used `lbl.publish_animation()` after `lbl.install_runtime()`,
do NOT also write `group.userData.tick = lambda dt, elapsed:
mixer.update(dt)` — pick ONE driver.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 370 — CROSS-PRIMITIVE VOCABULARY (PART 67)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The cross-primitive helpers are patterns of how to ARRANGE primitives:

  lbl.disc(radius, thickness, segments)        — thin cylinder treated as disc
  lbl.arc(radius, tube, arc_angle, segments)   — partial torus
  lbl.ribbon(points, width, thickness, segs)   — flat strip along curve
  lbl.helix(turns, height, radius, tube, segs) — spiral tube
  lbl.cross_planes(size, axis1, axis2, axis3)  — 3 intersecting planes
  lbl.place_chain(base_geom, count, transforms) — placed instances
  lbl.recolor_by_palette(group, name)          — hot-swap on existing group
  lbl.apply_material_animation(mat, prop, keyframes, fps) — animated material
  lbl.preflight_check(group, **opts)          — full audit before shipping

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 371 — STANDALONE PRIMITIVES (PART 68-71)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  lbl.star(points, outer_radius, inner_radius, depth, bevel)     # PART 68
  lbl.polygon(sides, radius, depth, bevel)                       # PART 68
  lbl.annulus(inner_radius, outer_radius, depth, bevel)          # PART 68
  lbl.heart(size, depth, bevel)                                  # PART 69
  lbl.spade(size, depth, bevel)                                  # PART 69
  lbl.burst(rays, inner_radius, outer_radius, depth, bevel)      # PART 70
  lbl.arch(width, height, depth, segments, bevel)                # PART 70
  lbl.edged_box(width, height, depth, edge_radius, edge_segments) # PART 70
  lbl.molding(length, profile_points, depth, bevel)              # PART 70
  lbl.pumpkin(size, segments, height)                            # PART 71
  lbl.leaf(size, depth, bevel)                                   # PART 71
  lbl.arched_slab(width, height, depth, segments, bevel)         # PART 71

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 372 — 44-TECHNIQUE MODELING BUNDLE BRIDGE
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The 44 helpers from MT_core / MT_mesh / MT_organic / MT_materials /
MT_advanced are reachable from Python via:

  lbl.mt.makePrimitive(kind, opts)         # 1: box / sphere / cylinder / ...
  lbl.mt.bmeshFromGeometry(geom)            # 2: BMesh
  lbl.mt.proceduralGenerate(axiom, rules, iters)  # 3: procedural
  lbl.mt.makeParametric(uFn, vFn, opts)     # 4: parametric surface
  lbl.mt.generativeGrow({...})              # 5: space colonization
  lbl.mt.sdfSphere([x,y,z], r)              # 6: SDF
  lbl.mt.sdfUnion(sdf_a, sdf_b)
  lbl.mt.sdfMarch(sdf, {resolution: 32})    # marching cubes
  lbl.mt.voxelCarve(geom)                   # 7: voxel
  lbl.mt.marchingCubesFromVoxels(vg)
  lbl.mt.csgUnion(mesh_a, mesh_b)           # 8: CSG (requires three-bvh-csg)
  lbl.mt.csgSubtract(mesh_a, mesh_b)
  lbl.mt.lSystem(axiom, rules, iters)       # 9: L-system
  lbl.mt.fractalMandelbulb({power: 8, ...})  # 10: fractal
  lbl.mt.fractalMenger(...)
  lbl.mt.makeNurbsCurve(ctrl, degree)       # 11: NURBS
  lbl.mt.makeCatmullRom(points, opts)
  lbl.mt.detectNonManifold(geom)            # 12: non-manifold
  lbl.mt.fixNonManifold(geom)
  lbl.mt.subdivideCatmullClark(geom, levels) # 13: subdivision
  lbl.mt.isotropicRemesh(geom, opts)        # 14: remeshing
  lbl.mt.qemDecimate(geom, opts)            # 16: decimation
  lbl.mt.laplacianSmooth(geom, opts)        # 19: smoothing
  lbl.mt.sculptBrush(geom, opts)            # 20: sculpting
  lbl.mt.displaceSurface(geom, opts)        # 21: displace + noise
  lbl.mt.smplSkeleton(...)                  # 22: SMPL
  lbl.mt.makeBlendshape(target, name, weight) # 23: blendshapes
  lbl.mt.computeLBS(skeleton, geom)         # 26: LBS
  lbl.mt.computeDQS(skeleton, geom)         # 27: DQS
  lbl.mt.deltaMush(geom, opts)              # 29: delta mush
  lbl.mt.autoRig(geom, opts)                # 34: auto-rigging
  lbl.mt.uvUnwrap(geom, opts)               # 37: UV unwrapping
  lbl.mt.bakeMap(geom, kind, opts)          # 38: baking
  lbl.mt.packAtlases(rects, opts)           # 40: atlas packing
  lbl.mt.generateLOD(geom, opts)            # 41: LOD chain
  lbl.mt.makeInstanced(geom, count, opts)   # 42: instancing
  lbl.mt.physicsStep(world, dt)             # 44: physics

Engine dispatcher:
  lbl.mt.run(technique, target, opts)
  lbl.mt.runStack({target, steps})
  lbl.mt.applyToScene(technique, scene, opts)
  lbl.mt.selfTest()  # 16-bundle sanity check
  lbl.mt.catalog    # list of all 44 techniques

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 373 — DEFORMERS (PART 244.8 / 67)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

All operate on THREE.BufferGeometry and return a new geometry:

  lbl.bend(geometry, axis="x", angle=0.5)
      Bend around an axis (in radians).
  lbl.twist(geometry, axis="y", angle=1.0)
      Twist around an axis (in radians).
  lbl.taper(geometry, axis="y", ratio=0.5)
      Taper along an axis (scales cross-section linearly).
  lbl.spherize(geometry, amount=1.0)
      Pull toward a sphere (useful for organic blob characters).
  lbl.noise(geometry, amplitude=0.05, frequency=4.0, seed=1, octaves=4)
      Displace vertices by value noise.
  lbl.modifier_stack(geometry, modifiers)
      Chain modifiers in order. modifiers is a list of dicts.
  lbl.fill_vertex_channel(geometry, channel, value)
      Set every vertex value of a given attribute.
  lbl.converge_faces(geometry, target, radius=0.1, strength=1.0)
      Pull vertices toward a target point.
  lbl.project_uv(geometry, method="planar", axis="z")
      Assign UVs by planar/spherical/cylindrical/box projection.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 374 — LOOK-DEV LIGHTING
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  lbl.lookdev_lights(mode="reference")  # 4-mode studio rig
      mode ∈ {"reference", "grazing", "neutral", "stage"}
      Returns: {group, shadow_catcher, lights}

  lbl.reference_lights()  # warm key + cool fill + rim (default)
  lbl.grazing_lights()    # strong side lighting for surface detail
  lbl.neutral_lights()    # soft even lighting (for QA photography)
  lbl.stage_lights()      # 4-light stage rig

  lbl.shadow_catcher()    # transparent ground plane (receives shadows)

  lbl.architectural_lights(mode="day")  # 4-mode architectural rig
      mode ∈ {"day", "night", "interior", "studio"}

  lbl.build_arch_palette()  # 9-key architectural palette
      # Returns: {stuccoWhite, woodTeak, woodOak, concrete, stonePavers,
      #          brickRed, roofTile, glass, door} as MeshStandardMaterials

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 375 — VALIDATION (PART 174 / 248)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Before returning your group, run:

  warnings = lbl.preflight_check(group, triangle_budget=10000)
  # Returns list of warnings (empty = all checks passed).
  if warnings:
      group.userData.preflightWarnings = warnings

The combined audit checks:
  - Triangle budget (default 8,500 for supercar, 5,000 for character)
  - Bounding box (min_y, max_abs_coord)
  - Shadow setup (every body mesh has cast + receive)
  - sculptRuntime contract conformance
  - userData.tick is set (warns if not)
  - Material color is set (warns if 0xffffff placeholder)

Individual checks:
  lbl.validate_triangle_budget(group, budget)
  lbl.validate_bbox(group, min_y=0.0, max_abs_coord=10.0)
  lbl.validate_shadows(group, require_cast=True, require_receive=True)
  lbl.validate_sculpt_runtime(group)  # PART 207 contract

PART 376 — ARCHITECTURAL HELPERS (PART 270-274)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  7 parametric constructors:
    lbl.arch_parametric(width, height, depth, segments)
    lbl.molding_parametric(length, profile, depth)
    lbl.step_block(width, height, depth, bevel)
    lbl.stair(width, height, depth, steps, step_depth)
    lbl.column(radius, height, base_height, capital_height, flutes, material)
    lbl.arched_window(width, height, depth, frame_thickness, material)
    lbl.roof_tile(width, length, depth, bevel, material)

  Anti-z-fighting (PART 272):
    dx, dy, dz = lbl.stack_level(key, axis="y")
    # Returns a tiny offset to prevent z-fighting when stacking layers.

  Look-dev lighting (PART 273):
    lbl.architectural_lights(mode="day"|"night"|"interior"|"studio")

  9-key material palette (PART 274):
    lbl.build_arch_palette()  # Returns materials dict

PART 377 — VEHICLE / HARD-SURFACE HELPERS (PART 167-178 / 255-259)

  Skeleton-first pipeline:
    lbl.vehicle_skeleton_first(body_width, body_length, body_height,
                                wheel_radius, wheelbase)

  5 fairing techniques:
    lbl.quadratic_fairing(width, height, depth, control_points)

    lbl.lathe_fairing(profile_points, segments)

    lbl.FilletedBox(...)          # body

    lbl.BeveledWasher(...)        # bolts, gaskets
    lbl.LathedTire(...)           # tires (PART 179)
    lbl.SweptTube(...)            # exhaust headers
    lbl.ExhaustCanister(...)      # mufflers

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 378 — v2.0 UPGRADED COMMON MISTAKES (DO NOT)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

DO NOT use CanvasTexture or document.createElement — they fail in Pyodide
on mobile. The renderer silently returns null textures → 3D models render
as solid color fallbacks (white if no color set).

DO NOT set color=0xffffff as a texture placeholder.

DO NOT use Style A (lbl.attach_box, lbl.attach_cylinder, lbl.paint_rectangle)
for new code — Style B is the default and supports everything Style A does
plus more (animations, materials, textures, rigging, runtime contracts).

DO NOT print() for debug output — the renderer captures stdout.

DO NOT import external pip packages (numpy, PIL, etc.) — they don't ship
with Pyodide by default.

DO NOT create detached THREE objects and forget to add them to the Group.
Always: g.add(mesh).

DO NOT use lbl.cast_shadow=False on body meshes — shadows are required.

DO NOT return None from create_model — return a Group or blueprint dict.

DO NOT use Math.PI in Python — it's math.pi.

DO NOT use new keyword with Three.js — it's THREE.Mesh.new() in Pyodide.

DO NOT forget to call lbl.install_runtime() — without it the inspector
won't be able to show the model tree.

DO NOT forget to call lbl.preflight_check() before returning.

DO NOT skip userData.setPalette + availablePalettes — users want to
hot-swap palettes at runtime.

DO NOT skip userData.tick if the model animates.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 379 — v2.0 UPGRADED RUNTIME ERROR REFERENCE
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

"ImportError: cannot import name 'X' from 'js'" — you're importing a class
that wasn't mirrored to window. Common ones: RoundedBoxGeometry,
TransformControls, GLTFExporter. The renderer auto-mirrors THREE.* and
RoundedBoxGeometry/TransformControls/GLTFExporter, but custom addons
need explicit `from js import window; window.MyClass = MyClass` first.

"AttributeError: 'JsProxy' object has no attribute 'X'" — you used
THREE.X.new() but X is a property, not a class. Check Three.js docs.

"factory returned None and no builder-API objects were registered" —
your create_model returned None without calling lbl.blueprint() first.
Either return a Group, return lbl.blueprint(), or call lbl.attach_box()
+ similar.

"sculptRuntime contract not satisfied" — you forgot lbl.install_runtime()
or your detailInventory has items that don't match any mesh name.

"Pyodide loader did not expose loadPyodide() — CDN URL may have changed."
— the user's network is offline or the CDN is blocked. They need to
transpile their .py to .json locally.

"Model renders white" — you used CanvasTexture which failed silently.
Switch to lbl.make_texture(...) (DataTexture) or layer the pattern as a
separate Mesh.

"Model renders as scattered flat dots" — you used Style A (lbl.attach_*)
which has silent geometry failures. Switch to Style B (THREE.Group direct).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 380 — v2.0 UPGRADED PRE-OUTPUT CHECKLIST
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Before you (the AI) output the .py file, verify:
  [ ] create_model (or build / make / main) function is defined
  [ ] Signature: def create_model(options=None):
  [ ] Style B: returns a THREE.Group (not a dict)
  [ ] All meshes have name= set
  [ ] All meshes have castShadow=True + receiveShadow=True
  [ ] body / stripe / carbon / rim / tire / glass / headlight / taillight
      / chrome / accent palette slots are populated (if applicable)
  [ ] Pattern meshes (stripes, decals) are separate Mesh layers
  [ ] No CanvasTexture / document.createElement calls
  [ ] userData.sculptRuntime set (use lbl.install_runtime)
  [ ] userData.tick set if any animation
  [ ] userData.setPalette + availablePalettes for hot-swap
  [ ] userData.meta has name + style + category
  [ ] Imports: `from js import THREE` (only if you need THREE.* directly)
  [ ] lbl.preflight_check() called before returning
  [ ] All lbl.* method names match PART 3 / 364-377 above
  [ ] No 0xffffff color placeholders

PART 381 — MINIMAL v2.0 WORKED EXAMPLE (Style B)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  # hello_block.py -- minimal Style B Python factory.
  #
  # Renders a single red box with full PBR, palette hot-swap, and animation.

  from js import THREE

  def create_model():
      g = lbl.Group("Hello Block")
      g.userData.meta = {"name": "Hello Block", "style": "smooth-low-poly",
                          "category": "prop", "subject": "box"}

      # Build a mutable materials dict from the racing-red palette
      mats = lbl.build_materials("racing-red")
      body_mat = mats["body"]

      # Solid red box (v2.0 PBR -- no canvas, no texture placeholders)
      body = lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 0.08, 4),
                      body_mat, name="body")
      body.position.y = 0.5
      g.add(body)

      # Idle bob animation (Style B -- userData.tick)
      def tick(dt, elapsed):
          body.position.y = 0.5 + math.sin(elapsed * 1.5) * 0.05
      g.userData.tick = tick

      # SculptRuntime contract (auto-populates 9-key + setPalette)
      lbl.install_runtime(g, {"subject": "box", "style": "smooth-low-poly",
                                "category": "prop",
                                "detailInventory": [
                                    {"id": "body", "region": "main",
                                     "kind": "feature", "priority": "high",
                                     "reviewThreshold": 0.7}
                                ]})

      # Preflight audit
      warnings = lbl.preflight_check(g, triangle_budget=2000)
      if warnings:
          g.userData.preflightWarnings = warnings

      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 382 — ADVANCED v2.0 WORKED EXAMPLE (supercar with palette hot-swap)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  # supercar.py -- Apex Horizon GT Style B factory with full v2.0 surface.
  #
  # Try at runtime in browser console:
  #   car.userData.setPalette("neon-cyber")
  #   car.userData.setDriveSpeed(0.5)
  #   car.userData.steer(0.3)
  #   car.userData.revEngine(1.0)

  from js import THREE

  def create_model(options=None):
      palette_name = (options or {}).get("palette", "racing-red")
      mats = lbl.build_materials(palette_name)
      body_mat    = mats["body"]
      stripe_mat  = mats["stripe"]
      carbon_mat  = mats["carbon"]
      rim_mat     = mats["rim"]
      tire_mat    = mats["tire"]
      glass_mat   = mats["glass"]
      headlight_mat = mats["headlight"]
      taillight_mat = mats["taillight"]
      chrome_mat  = mats["chrome"]
      accent_mat  = mats["accent"]

      car = lbl.Group("ApexHorizonGT")
      car.userData.meta = {"name": "Apex Horizon GT", "category": "vehicle",
                            "style": "stylized-lowpoly", "subject": "supercar"}

      # Lower body
      lower = lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(2.4, 0.30, 1.70, 0.06, 4),
                       body_mat, name="lower_body")
      lower.position.set(0, 0.62, 0)
      car.add(lower)

      # Hood
      hood = lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(1.2, 0.10, 1.40, 0.04, 3),
                      body_mat, name="hood")
      hood.position.set(0.85, 0.99, 0)
      car.add(hood)

      # Racing stripes (FIX 4 -- separate Mesh layers, not textures)
      for side in (-1, 1):
          s = lbl.Mesh(lbl.Box(2.0, 0.005, 0.06), stripe_mat,
                       name="stripe_{}".format(side))
          s.position.set(0, 1.05, side * 0.18)
          car.add(s)

      # Front splitter (carbon)
      splitter = lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(2.1, 0.04, 1.75, 0.015, 2),
                          carbon_mat, name="front_splitter")
      splitter.position.set(1.20, 0.42, 0)
      car.add(splitter)

      # Rear wing (carbon)
      wing = lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(0.30, 0.04, 1.10, 0.015, 2),
                      carbon_mat, name="rear_wing")
      wing.position.set(-1.10, 1.18, 0)
      car.add(wing)

      # Canopy (glass)
      canopy = lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(1.10, 0.32, 1.20, 0.04, 3),
                        glass_mat, name="canopy")
      canopy.position.set(-0.05, 1.08, 0)
      car.add(canopy)

      # Headlights (emissive)
      for side in (-1, 1):
          hl = lbl.Mesh(lbl.Box(0.18, 0.10, 0.30), headlight_mat,
                        name="headlight_{}".format(side))
          hl.position.set(1.30, 0.85, side * 0.55)
          car.add(hl)

      # Wheels using LathedTire + StarPattern (custom shape helpers)
      def build_wheel(x, side):
          wg = lbl.Group("wheel_{}".format(side))
          tire_geo = lbl.LathedTire(outer_radius=0.34, inner_radius=0.18,
                                      width=0.22, shoulder_radius=0.04)
          tire_geo.rotateY(math.pi/2)
          tire = lbl.Mesh(tire_geo, tire_mat, name="tire")
          wg.add(tire)
          spokes = lbl.StarPattern(5, 0.07, 0.28, 0.05)
          spokes.position.x = (0.11 / 2) * side
          wg.add(spokes)
          wg.position.set(x, 0.34, 0)
          return wg
      for x in (-1.25, 1.25):
          car.add(build_wheel(x, -1))
          car.add(build_wheel(x, 1))

      # Exhaust (oval canister + swept header)
      exhaust = lbl.Mesh(
          lbl.ExhaustCanister(length=0.45, radius_top=0.085, radius_bottom=0.095,
                              oval=True), chrome_mat, name="exhaust")
      exhaust.rotation.z = math.pi / 2
      exhaust.position.set(-1.20, 0.50, 0.85)
      car.add(exhaust)
      header = lbl.Mesh(
          lbl.SweptTube([[-1.20, 0.50, 0.65], [-1.00, 0.40, 0.55],
                          [-0.70, 0.35, 0.50], [-0.40, 0.40, 0.50]],
                         radius=0.025, radial_segments=8), chrome_mat,
          name="exhaust_header")
      car.add(header)

      # Tick (animation -- wheels spin, body leans, lights pulse)
      state = {"drive": 0.0, "steer": 0.0, "lean": 0.0, "rev": 0.0}
      target_steer = [0.0]

      def tick(dt, elapsed):
          state["steer"] += (target_steer[0] - state["steer"]) * min(1, dt * 4)
          state["lean"] += (-state["steer"] * abs(state["drive"]) * 0.15 -
                            state["lean"]) * min(1, dt * 2)
          car.rotation.z = state["lean"]
          for w in car.children:
              if w.name.startswith("wheel_"):
                  if w.name.endswith("-1") or w.name.endswith("1"):
                      w.rotation.y = state["steer"]
                  if w.children and w.children[0].name == "tire":
                      w.children[0].rotation.x += dt * state["drive"] * 4
          if headlight_mat:
              headlight_mat.emissiveIntensity = 2.0 + math.sin(elapsed * 1.5) * 0.1

      car.userData.tick = tick

      # Public API (Actions)
      def steer(a):
          target_steer[0] = max(-0.6, min(0.6, a))
      def set_drive(s):
          state["drive"] = max(-2.0, min(2.0, s))
      def rev_engine(power=1.0):
          state["rev"] += 0.3 * power
          state["rev"] = min(2.5, state["rev"])
      car.userData.steer = steer
      car.userData.setDriveSpeed = set_drive
      car.userData.revEngine = rev_engine

      # SculptRuntime contract (auto-populated)
      lbl.install_runtime(car, {"subject": "supercar", "style": "stylized-lowpoly",
                                 "category": "vehicle",
                                 "detailInventory": [
                                     {"id": "lower_body", "region": "chassis",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "high",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
                                     {"id": "hood", "region": "chassis",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "high",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
                                     {"id": "front_splitter", "region": "aero",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "medium",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
                                     {"id": "rear_wing", "region": "aero",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "medium",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
                                     {"id": "canopy", "region": "cockpit",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "high",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
                                     {"id": "headlight_-1", "region": "lights",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "high",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
                                     {"id": "headlight_1", "region": "lights",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "high",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
                                     {"id": "exhaust", "region": "exhaust",
                                      "kind": "feature", "priority": "medium",
                                      "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
                                 ]})

      # Preflight
      warnings = lbl.preflight_check(car, triangle_budget=10000)
      if warnings:
          car.userData.preflightWarnings = warnings

      return car

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 383 — CHARACTER RIGGING v2.0 EXAMPLE
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  # character.py -- rigged humanoid with skeleton + walk cycle.
  #
  # Try at runtime:
  #   char.userData.setSkinTheme("void-monarch")
  #   char.userData.playAnimation("walk", fade=0.3)

  from js import THREE

  SKIN_THEMES = {
      "cyber-paladin":    {"skin": "#e0ae87", "armor": "#1b202c", "accent": "#fbb034", "glow": "#00f5d4"},
      "void-monarch":     {"skin": "#c9987a", "armor": "#0d0817", "accent": "#fb923c", "glow": "#e879f9"},
      "celestial-knight": {"skin": "#e8be99", "armor": "#f8fafc", "accent": "#f59e0b", "glow": "#38bdf8"},
  }

  def create_model(options=None):
      theme = (options or {}).get("theme", "cyber-paladin")
      T = SKIN_THEMES[theme]
      skin_mat = lbl.skin_material(base_color=T["skin"])
      armor_mat = lbl.standard(color=T["armor"], roughness=0.30, metalness=0.70)
      glow_mat = lbl.emissive(T["glow"], T["glow"], 1.5)

      g = lbl.Group("rigged_character")
      g.userData.meta = {"name": "Rigged Character", "category": "character",
                          "style": "stylized-lowpoly", "subject": "humanoid"}

      # 5-bone skeleton (rigging)
      skel = lbl.skeleton([
          {"name": "pelvis", "restPosition": [0, 0, 0]},
          {"name": "spine",  "restPosition": [0, 0.3, 0], "parent": "pelvis"},
          {"name": "chest",  "restPosition": [0, 0.6, 0], "parent": "spine"},
          {"name": "neck",   "restPosition": [0, 0.9, 0], "parent": "chest"},
          {"name": "head",   "restPosition": [0, 1.1, 0], "parent": "neck"},
      ])
      g.add(skel["root"])

      # Body parts (hierarchical -- no skinning needed for chunky-mascot)
      g.add(lbl.Mesh(lbl.Box(0.36, 0.20, 0.26), armor_mat, name="pelvis"))
      g.add(lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(0.40, 0.50, 0.30, 0.04, 3),
                      armor_mat, name="torso"))
      g.add(lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(0.24, 0.26, 0.24, 0.05, 3),
                      skin_mat, name="head"))
      for side in (-1, 1):
          g.add(lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(0.14, 0.46, 0.14, 0.03, 2),
                          armor_mat, name="arm_{}".format(side)))
          g.add(lbl.Mesh(lbl.FilletedBox(0.16, 0.50, 0.16, 0.03, 2),
                          armor_mat, name="leg_{}".format(side)))

      # Walk cycle via AnimationMixer + procedural clip (animation)
      mixer = lbl.make_mixer(g)
      walk_clip = lbl.build_walk_clip(
          joint_paths=[
              ("arm_-1_pivot", 0.0), ("arm_1_pivot", math.pi),
              ("leg_-1_pivot", math.pi), ("leg_1_pivot", 0.0),
          ],
          duration=1.0, frequency=2.0, amplitude=0.4,
      )
      lbl.play_animation(mixer, walk_clip, fade_in=0.3)

      # Damped spring for cape (secondary motion)
      cape_spring = lbl.damp_spring(target=0, k=80, c=8)
      def tick(dt, elapsed):
          cape_spring.target = math.sin(elapsed * 1.5) * 0.3
          cape_spring.step(dt)

      g.userData.tick = tick
      g.userData.playAnimation = lambda n, f=0.3: lbl.play_animation(
          mixer, walk_clip if n == "walk" else None, f)
      g.userData.setSkinTheme = lambda t: None  # placeholder
      g.userData.availableThemes = list(SKIN_THEMES.keys())

      lbl.install_runtime(g, {"subject": "character", "style": "stylized-lowpoly",
                                "category": "character",
                                "detailInventory": [
                                    {"id": "head", "region": "head",
                                     "kind": "feature", "priority": "high",
                                     "reviewThreshold": 0.9},
                                    {"id": "torso", "region": "torso",
                                     "kind": "panel", "priority": "high",
                                     "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
                                ]})
      return g

PART 385 — SUMMARY: WHAT CHANGED IN v2.0
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

For the AI prompt author, here is the absolute minimum you need to know:

1. Use Style B (THREE.Group return) -- NOT Style A.
2. Use the 10-slot palette system (lbl.build_materials + setPalette).
3. Use solid PBR materials -- NO CanvasTexture, NO document.createElement.
4. Use separate Mesh layers for patterns (stripes, decals).
5. Set userData.sculptRuntime via lbl.install_runtime.
6. Set userData.tick for any animation.
7. Set userData.setPalette + availablePalettes for hot-swap.
8. Use the new custom shape helpers (FilletedBox, LathedTire, etc.)
9. Use rigging helpers (skeleton, skinned_mesh) for characters.
10. Use animation helpers (build_walk_clip, damp_spring, mixer).
11. Run preflight_check before returning.
12. NEVER use color=0xffffff as a texture placeholder.

If you remember these 12 rules, you'll generate v2.0-compatible Python
factories. If you also use the worked examples in Model_10.py /
Model_11_Character.py / Model_12_House.py as patterns, you'll match the
TypeScript output quality.

================================================================================
END OF PART 360-385 (v2.0 upgrade documentation)
================================================================================
