PART 386-410 — v2.0.2 PATCH (FULL PARITY + ATTACHMENT-DRIVEN FEATURES)
================================================================================

This PART block is layered additively on top of PART 360-385. It
brings v2.0.0 to **full parity with the LBL v1.35 / v8.26 spec**, as
documented in:

  - ANALYSIS_REPORT.txt (2026-09-14, comprehensive renderer audit)
  - SportMotorcycle_3D_Techniques_Specification.txt (10-section
    chunky-mascot vehicle reference)
  - CHANGES_v130.md / CHANGES_v131.md (PART 145-166: perfect-shape
    pipeline + image-driven factory defaults)
  - CHANGES_v132.md (PART 167-178: hard-surface / vehicle pipeline)
  - CHANGES_v133.md (PART 179: form-preservation patch)
  - CHANGES_v134.md (PART 180-189: chunky sport motorcycle reference)
  - CHANGES_v135.md (PART 190-199: architectural modeling pipeline)
  - CHANGES_SUMMARY.md (v1.25 / v1.26 / v1.27 / v1.29 / v1.30-1.35)

The lbl helper has been extended with new module-level functions
plus 30+ new `_LBLFacade` methods. Every PART below documents
ONE new feature surface and the corresponding lbl.* call.

AI authors generating .py files via this spec SHOULD read PART 386-410
after reading PART 360-385.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 386 — ACES (anyCreature Engine Surface) FULL SURFACE
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 90-94 in the original spec is the ACES (anyCreature Engine
Surface) integration. It ports 7 sub-modules from ariescar's
anyCreature 1.3.1 into the renderer:

  public/aces/aces-engine.js     PART 90 — orchestrator
  public/aces/aces-oklab.js      PART 91.1 — OKLab colour space
  public/aces/aces-normals.js    PART 91.2 — angle-weighted + crease
  public/aces/aces-ao.js         PART 91 — per-vertex hemisphere AO
  public/aces/aces-shade.js      PART 92 — L1-L8 OKLab stack
  public/aces/aces-checks.js     PART 93+98 — 25 mechanical + style checks
  public/aces/aces-bones.js      PART 94 — public bone-name + extras

v2.0.2 exposes the ACES surface to Python factories via 7 new lbl
methods:

  lbl.aces_run(scene_or_spec, opts=None)         # PART 386.1
  lbl.aces_runstack(spec_list, opts=None)        # PART 386.2
  lbl.aces_self_test()                           # PART 386.3
  lbl.aces_export_bone_names(spec, opts=None)    # PART 386.4
  lbl.aces_asset_extras(spec, opts=None)         # PART 386.5
  lbl.aces_oklab_to_linear(rgb)                  # PART 386.6
  lbl.aces_linear_to_oklab(rgb)                  # PART 386.7

In Pyodide, `lbl.aces_run()` delegates to `window.ACES.run()` which
runs the 6-stage pipeline (extract → crease split + angle-weighted
normals → AO bake → L1-L8 OKLab stack → L8 bone-field softening →
25 mechanical checks → write-back). In headless test environments
it returns a placeholder report so the lbl surface stays callable.

ACES.run() example:

  import lbl
  report = lbl.aces_run(car_root, {
      "smoothAngle": 30.0,        # degrees
      "ao": {"samples": 16, "strength": 0.59, "radius": 0.6},
      "checksOpts": {"strict": False},
  })
  print("blocks:", len(report.get("blocks", [])))
  print("warns:", len(report.get("warns", [])))

The 6 stages in detail:
  1. EXTRACT — walk the THREE.Object3D tree, pull out
     {V, F, C, N, skin, color, material, part, chain, partType,
      smoothAngle, faceted, soft} records per mesh.
  2. CREASE SPLIT — angle-weighted vertex normals; split vertices
     when more than one smoothing group is present.
  3. AO BAKE — Möller-Trumbore hemisphere rays from every vertex
     against the whole model. Uniform-grid acceleration.
  4. L1-L8 STACK — 8 layers of OKLab shading:
     L1 seam-safe flesh colour
     L2 deterministic value-noise pattern (flesh only)
     L3 top-to-bottom ramp in OKLab
     L4 boost (brighter + more saturated up top)
     L5 hardware bleed (sharp-edge preserved, darkened around)
     L6 hardware shadow (AO only, gamma=0.7)
     L7 flesh body shadow (horizontal light ring × AO)
     L8 bone-field NORMAL softening (flesh only)
  5. L8 BONE-FIELD NORMAL SOFTENING — apply softened normals to
     the live BufferGeometry's NORMAL attribute.
  6. 25 MECHANICAL + STYLE CHECKS — BLOCK (legality) + WARN (style).
     The BLOCK roster: mesh_integrity, root_containment, part_attachment,
     touch, balance, size, proportion, limb_clearance, anim_integrity,
     attack_reach, faceted_body, mirror_distortion, part_overlap,
     part_seat, soft_mass.
     The WARN roster: value_order, contrast_adjacent, share_hierarchy,
     focal_contrast, saturation_area, thinnest_px48, sq_fill, mirror_sym,
     straight_max, tri_budget, bright_floor.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 387 — SKELETON JSON TEMPLATE LOADERS (6 BUILT-INS)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 76.3 in the original spec mandates that the renderer auto-load
skeleton templates from `public/rig/skeletons/*.json` and scale them
to the model's bounding box. The 6 templates are:

  humanoid   — 13 joints, 1.0 m A-pose (head, spine, 2 arms, 2 legs)
  quadruped  — 23 joints, 1.115 m long (4 legs + spine + chest + head + tail)
  bird       — 15 joints, 0.30 m
  fish       — 9 joints, 0.50 m
  insect     — 17 joints, 0.05 m
  object     — 5 joints, 1.0 m (1 root + 4 anchors) [PART 388 fix]

v2.0.2 inlines these templates into the lbl helper as a Python dict
so Pyodide factories don't need to fetch() them:

  lbl.load_skeleton_template("quadruped")
  lbl.list_skeleton_classes()    # returns ["humanoid", "quadruped", ...]

The 'object' template is now single-rooted (1 Bone_Center + 4
anchor children) per ANALYSIS_REPORT [B] #1. The original JSON file
in the repo had 4 root joints (Bone_Top, Bone_Bottom, Bone_Left,
Bone_Right), which made `validateRigGraph()` and `buildSkeleton()`
throw "rig must have one root joint".

The 'quadruped' template has its `height` set to 1.115 m (matching
the actual silhouette 0.035 m → 1.150 m = 1.115 m) per
ANALYSIS_REPORT [B] #2. The original spec declared 1.45 m which was
23% off.

Example:

  tmpl = lbl.load_skeleton_template("quadruped")
  skeleton = lbl.skeleton(tmpl["joints"])  # build a Three.js skeleton
  skinned = lbl.bind_skin(geometry, skeleton, weights, indices)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 388 — MT HELPER FALLBACKS (pure-Python equivalents)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The renderer's `window.MT_*` modules expose ~75 helpers across 6
namespaces (core / mesh / organic / materials / advanced / textures
+ hardsurface + architectural + highpoly). v2.0.0 had thin
pass-throughs for ~70 of these. v2.0.2 adds 17 more that were
missing from the bridge, with pure-Python fallbacks when the
module isn't loaded:

  lbl.bmesh_to_geometry(bmesh)              # PART 388.1
  lbl.sdf_box(center, half_extents)         # PART 388.2
  lbl.sdf_torus(center, major, minor)       # PART 388.3
  lbl.sdf_plane(normal, offset)             # PART 388.4
  lbl.sdf_intersect(a, b)                   # PART 388.5
  lbl.sdf_subtract(a, b)                    # PART 388.6
  lbl.lsystem_interpret(rules, axiom, iter)  # PART 388.7
  lbl.smpl_apply_shape(geom, shape, weight)  # PART 388.8
  lbl.lbs_skin(geom, skel, w, idx, infl)    # PART 388.9
  lbl.dqs_skin(geom, skel, w, idx, infl)    # PART 388.10
  lbl.auto_retopologize(geom, target)       # PART 388.11
  lbl.subdivide_loop(geom, levels)          # PART 388.12
  lbl.isotropic_remesh(geom, edge, iter)    # PART 388.13
  lbl.triangulate(geom)                     # PART 388.14
  lbl.quadify(geom, angle_threshold)        # PART 388.15
  lbl.taubin_smooth(geom, iter, pass_band)   # PART 388.16
  lbl.sculpt_brush(geom, pos, dir, r, s)    # PART 388.17
  lbl.displace_surface(geom, hmap, amount)  # PART 388.18

In the browser, these call `window.MT_core.sdfBox(...)` etc. when
the module is loaded. In headless tests, they fall back to pure-
Python implementations so the lbl surface stays callable.

Example:

  # Build a 2 m × 1 m × 1 m box SDF
  box = lbl.sdf_box(center=(0, 0.5, 0), half_extents=(1, 0.5, 0.5))
  # Sample at the origin (should be negative = inside)
  print(box((0, 0.5, 0)))    # negative
  print(box((5, 5, 5)))      # positive (far outside)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 389 — ANIMATION TRACK-TYPE DETECTION (ANALYSIS_REPORT #18 FIX)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

When a TS / .py factory publishes `runtime.animations` as a plain
`[{name, duration, tracks:[{name, times, values}]}]` array (the
"legacy hint" path), the renderer needs to detect whether each
track is `NumberKeyframeTrack`, `VectorKeyframeTrack`, or
`QuaternionKeyframeTrack` by the values-array length. v2.0.2 adds
3 new helpers:

  lbl.detect_animation_track_type(name, values)   # PART 389.1
  lbl.build_animation_track(name, times, values)  # PART 389.2
  lbl.collect_animation_clips(rt, root)           # PART 389.3

Detection rule (lbl.detect_animation_track_type):
  - name ends with '.quaternion' OR name ends with '.rotation'
    AND values length divisible by 4 → 'quaternion'
  - values length divisible by 3 → 'vector'
  - otherwise → 'number'

Example:

  # Quat track (rotation)
  print(lbl.detect_animation_track_type("head.quaternion",
                                         [0, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0.707, 0.707]))
  # → 'quaternion'

  # Vec track (position)
  print(lbl.detect_animation_track_type("head.position",
                                         [0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0]))
  # → 'vector'

  # Scalar track (emissive intensity)
  print(lbl.detect_animation_track_type("headlight.emissiveIntensity",
                                         [0, 1, 0.5, 0.7]))
  # → 'number'

`lbl.collect_animation_clips()` also fixes ANALYSIS_REPORT [L] #23:
the legacy hint path is short-circuited when the real .clips / .mixer
path already produced results.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 390 — SCHEMA NAMESPACING LINTER (architectural correctness)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The renderer's `MT_architectural.schemaNamespacingLinter` walks a
THREE.Object3D scene graph and verifies:
  1. Every required `detailInventory` entry has at least one matching
     mesh (by `userData.featureId` OR `mesh.name` regex).
  2. No two meshes have coplanar faces within 5 mm of each other
     (anti-Z-fighting sanity check).

v2.0.2 exposes this to Python:

  report = lbl.schema_linter(group, {
      "gridKeyMm": 5.0,   # coplanar threshold
  })
  print("blocks:", report["blocks"])
  print("warns:", report["warns"])
  print("required_seen:", report["required_seen"])
  print("required_total:", report["required_total"])

The linter emits a warn line for every required detail that has no
matching mesh, plus a warn line for every pair of meshes whose
bounding boxes share a 5mm world-space grid key.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 391 — HARD-SURFACE FACTORY SHELL (8-SECTION SCAFFOLD)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 175 in the original spec mandates an 8-section code architecture
for hard-surface factories:

  Section 1 — Module Header & Coordinate Contract
  Section 2 — Type Definitions & Config Options
  Section 3 — Palette & Physical Constants
  Section 4 — Reusable Geometry Helpers
  Section 5 — Centralized Material Registry
  Section 6 — Isolated Subassembly Builders
  Section 7 — Primary Factory Function
  Section 8 — Dedicated Look-Dev Lights Factory

`lbl.hard_surface_factory_shell(spec)` returns a dict with the
8-section scaffold (palette stub, materials stub, lookDevLights stub,
audits, dimension contract, clearances) the caller populates:

  shell = lbl.hard_surface_factory_shell({
      "name": "Apex Horizon GT",
      "bounding_box": [4.2, 1.5, 1.8],   # PART 168.1 car dimensions
  })
  print(shell["audits"])
  # → {"validate_triangle_budget": 8500, "validate_draw_call_count": 30, ...}
  print(shell["dimension_contract"]["clearances"])
  # → {"tire_mudguard": 0.015, "rotor_caliper": 0.010, ...}

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 392 — createWallWithApertures (piecewise sub-wall)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 192 in the original spec codifies the Aperture Framing Pattern.
The wall is built as a slab grid (X × Y) with cavities removed where
apertures overlap. No CSG runtime cost, sharp UVs, no face overlaps,
fully hollow cavities.

  lbl.create_wall_with_apertures(
      width=4.0, height=3.0, thickness=0.2,
      apertures=[
          {"x": 1.0, "y": 0.0, "w": 0.9, "h": 2.1, "kind": "door"},
          {"x": 0.5, "y": 1.5, "w": 1.0, "h": 1.0, "kind": "window"},
          {"x": 2.5, "y": 1.5, "w": 1.0, "h": 1.0, "kind": "window"},
      ],
      material="stuccoWhite",
  )

In the browser this returns a THREE.Group with the wall built as
slab grid. In headless tests it returns a spec dict the renderer
materialises.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 393 — SOCKET ATTACHMENT HIERARCHY (PART 179.9 ENFORCEMENT)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Sockets declared in `userData.sculptRuntime.sockets` MUST NOT be
loose groups parented to root. They MUST be parented to their
operational anchor:

  Socket_Headlight        → NoseCowl or SteeringHeadPivot
  Socket_ExhaustTip       → ExhaustMufflerCanister
  Socket_RiderHips        → RiderSeatGroup
  Socket_FuelCap          → FuelTank (in Body_Fairings & FuelTank envelope)
  Socket_MirrorLeft/Right → ClipOnHandlebars (under SteeringHeadPivot)

`lbl.validate_socket_attachments(group, spec=None)` walks the
scene graph and reports any mismatches:

  report = lbl.validate_socket_attachments(car_root, {
      "sockets": {
          "Socket_Headlight":  ["NoseCowl", "SteeringHeadPivot"],
          "Socket_ExhaustTip": ["ExhaustMufflerCanister"],
      },
  })
  if not report["ok"]:
      for m in report["mismatches"]:
          print(f"  {m['socket']} parented to '{m['parent']}' "
                f"but expected one of {m['expected_parents']}")

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 394 — FORM-PRESERVATION DIRECTIVES (Hierarchy of Fidelity)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 167 (top of CHANGES_v133) defines the **5-tier Hierarchy of
Fidelity** that governs the entire PART 167-179 vehicle / hard-
surface section:

  1. VISUAL SILHOUETTE & CONTINUOUS SURFACE FLOW (absolute priority)
  2. COMPOUND CONTOUR PROCEDURAL TECHNIQUE (lathe / bezier)
  3. PBR TACTILE MATERIAL SHADING (micro-roughness)
  4. MECHANICAL SKELETON & PIVOT HIERARCHY
  5. CODE MINIFICATION & PARAMETRIC HELPERS

When two rules conflict, the higher tier wins. The AI factory MUST
apply this hierarchy in order. Skipping tier 1 (visual silhouette)
to save a few lines of code is the #1 anti-pattern.

The 4 hard rules that fall out of this hierarchy:

  RULE 167.0.1 — Structural pivots MUST be invisible structural
    parents. They MUST NEVER dictate or chop up the continuous
    aesthetic lines of exterior bodywork.

  RULE 167.0.2 — FORBIDDEN FAIRING GEOMETRY. BoxGeometry,
    CylinderGeometry, and FilletedBoxGeometry are STRICTLY
    FORBIDDEN for primary aerodynamic cowls, fuel tanks,
    fairings, visors, and tires.

  RULE 167.1.1 — THE FORM PRESERVATION MANDATE. Under no
    circumstances may the skeletal pivot tree disrupt the
    continuous, sculpted lines of vehicle bodywork. A model
    that has correct pivot names but looks like assembled
    boxes is REJECTED.

  RULE 169.1.2 — FILLETED BOX USAGE CONSTRAINT.
    `createFilletedBoxGeometry` is strictly classified as an
    INTERNAL STRUCTURAL primitive. It is PROHIBITED for fuel
    tanks, aerodynamic fairings, streamlined cowls,
    aerodynamic visors, and tires.

  RULE 169.3.2 — MANDATORY WHEEL PROFILE REVOLUTION. A tire
    is NEVER a plain CylinderGeometry. Every wheel MUST
    feature a lathed tire with (1) toroidal crown, (2)
    inward tapered sidewalls, and (3) stepped bead.

In v2.0 Python, the `lbl.FilletedBox(...)` helper (which delegates
to `MT_hardsurface.createFilletedBoxGeometry`) is fine for engine
blocks, calipers, brackets — but MUST NOT be used for fuel tanks,
fairings, visors, or tires. The `lbl.LathedTire(...)` helper
(delegate to `MT_hardsurface.createLathedTireGeometry`) is the
canonical way to author any tire.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 395 — BEZIER-EXTRUDE AERODYNAMIC COWLS (PART 170.2 / 179.3)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

For fuel tanks, side cowls, nose cones, tail cowls, and aerodynamic
fairings, the canonical approach is bezier profile + extrude:

  cp = [
      (0.0, 0.0),    # start
      (0.3, 0.4),    # c1
      (0.7, 0.4),    # c2
      (1.0, 0.0),    # end of first segment
      (1.3, -0.2),   # c1 of second segment
      (1.7, -0.1),   # c2
      (2.0, 0.0),    # end
  ]
  spec = lbl.bezier_fairing(cp, depth=0.04, bevel=0.02, twist=0.0)
  # The renderer materialises the spec via THREE.ExtrudeGeometry.

For the most aggressive aerodynamics, add a subtle twist:

  spec = lbl.bezier_fairing(cp, depth=0.04, bevel=0.02, twist=0.08)

The `twist` parameter adds `THREE.ExtrudeGeometry`'s `.twist` option
so the extrusion rotates as it travels along the depth axis. Useful
for saddle-bag style cowls that twist with the bodywork.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 396 — LATHED TIRE PROFILE (PART 169.3 / 179.2)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every wheel MUST feature a lathed tire with:
  1. Toroidal crown (parabolic or sinusoidal curve)
  2. Inward tapered sidewalls (pinch toward the rim)
  3. Stepped bead (inner lip where tire meets wheel rim)

  tire = lbl.LathedTire(outerR=0.35, width=0.20, crown=0.05,
                          taper=0.04, bead=0.02, segments=24)
  # OR explicit lathe
  tire = lbl.Lathe([
      [0.18, 0.10],   # bead
      [0.30, 0.10],   # sidewall start
      [0.32, 0.06],   # taper inward
      [0.35, 0.00],   # crown
      [0.32, -0.06],  # taper outward
      [0.30, -0.10],  # sidewall end
      [0.18, -0.10],  # bead
  ], segments=24)

Plain `lbl.Cylinder(...)` wheels FAIL automated audit E41 (PART
174.3 "loft trap-warnings" / "distinct-Z test") and are explicitly
rejected by the form-preservation mandate (PART 394 / 169.3.2).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 397 — EGG VISOR FIX (PART 170.6 / 179.6)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The "egg visor" phenomenon is a common failure mode: a windscreen
authored as a CapsuleGeometry or SphereGeometry looks like a soap
bar on a vehicle's nose. The fix is a 5-component composition:

  1. Bezier profile front arch (PART 395)
  2. Extrude thickness 0.03 - 0.05 m (NOT 0.005 m)
  3. Rake backward 40° - 55° (v1.33 raised minimum from v1.32's 37°)
  4. MeshPhysicalMaterial with transmission 0.45, roughness 0.20,
     thickness 0.08
  5. Thin dark rubber perimeter gasket (TorusGeometry scaled
     non-uniformly to (1.0, 0.85, 1.0))

In Python:

  visor_profile = [(0.0, 0.0), (0.3, 0.4), (0.7, 0.4), (1.0, 0.0)]
  visor = lbl.bezier_fairing(visor_profile, depth=0.04, bevel=0.005)
  visor_material = lbl.physical(transmission=0.45, roughness=0.20,
                                 thickness=0.08, ior=1.5)
  # Rake backward 45°:
  visor_root = lbl.attach_at(group, visor, x=0, y=1.1, z=0,
                              rx=math.radians(45), ry=0, rz=0)
  # Gasket:
  gasket = lbl.Torus(0.20, 0.01)
  gasket.scale.set(1.0, 0.85, 1.0)
  gasket.material = lbl.rubber_dark()
  visor_root.add(gasket)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 398 — TS1117 DUPLICATE-KEY FIX (PART 179.8)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

When the generator merges bevel options with extrude parameters,
accidental duplicate keys (e.g. `steps: 1` appearing twice) cause
fatal compiler breaks under TypeScript strict mode (TS1117).

Three fix patterns:
  - `Object.freeze(...)` the canonical `ExtrudeGeometryOptions`
    constant.
  - Use a typed helper interface.
  - Funnel every PART 179.3 / PART 179.5 extrusion through ONE
    factory function (e.g. `lbl.bezier_fairing()`) that internally
    owns the single canonical options object.

v2.0.2 makes `lbl.bezier_fairing()` the canonical funnel for all
aerodynamic fairing extrusions. The `_bezier_fairing_spec()`
function in lbl_py.py builds a single canonical options dict and
returns it as a spec; the renderer materialises it via
`THREE.ExtrudeGeometry` with no duplicate keys.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 399 — SPORT MOTORCYCLE REFERENCE SPECIFICATION (PART 180-189)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The Chunky 3D Sport Motorcycle (Superbike) is the canonical worked
example for the LBL v1.34 / v8.25 reference specification. The
10-section layout:

  PART 180 — Spec layout (10 sections)
  PART 181 — Core technologies stack (3+1+1+1)
  PART 182 — Authoritative geometry catalog
  PART 183 — Reference materials & color palette
  PART 184 — Modular component architecture (8 builders)
  PART 185 — Coordinate system & proportions
  PART 186 — sculptRuntime runtime contract (9 keys + 5 actions)
  PART 187 — Look-dev lighting system (3 modes)
  PART 188 — Architectural & performance targets
  PART 189 — Style metadata + LBL version stamp

The 8 modular builder functions (PART 184):

  createChunkySportWheel()      # Torus tire + deep-dish rim + 5-spoke
  buildFrontForkAssembly()      # USD inverted forks + triple clamps
  buildFairingAndCockpit()      # Nose cowl + headlight + windscreen
  buildFuelTank()               # Sculpted tank + knee recesses
  buildSeatAndTailSection()     # Contoured saddle + ducktail cowl
  buildChassisAndEngine()       # Twin-spar frame + engine + radiator
  buildExhaustSystem()          # Curved header + upswept oval muffler
  buildRearSwingarm()           # Twin arms + sprocket + chain

The sculptRuntime contract (PART 186) requires 9 keys on
`root.userData.sculptRuntime`:

  nodes            : named Object3D hierarchy
  meshes           : named Mesh registry
  sockets          : attachment points (headlight, exhaustTip, riderSeat)
  colliders        : Box3 bounding volumes
  destructionGroups : fairing / wheel / exhaust groupings
  materials        : material map
  actions          : public API methods
  animation        : animation state
  vfx              : VFX sockets

Plus 5 public Actions API methods (PART 186.2):

  revEngine()
  steer(angle: number)
  setDriveSpeed(speed: number)
  toggleHeadlight(force?: boolean)
  resetPose()

The 3-mode look-dev lighting rig (PART 187):

  'reference'  warm-key + cool-fill + rim
  'grazing'    low-angle grazing for edge contour inspection
  'neutral'    balanced reference for unbiased material eval

In v2.0 Python, the lighting is exposed via `lbl.sport_motorcycle_lights(mode)`.
The 8 builders are userland code (each < 200 lines in `Model_10.py`).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 400 — ARCHITECTURAL REFERENCE SPECIFICATION (PART 190-199)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The Architectural Modeling Pipeline (PART 190-199) is the canonical
worked example for buildings. The 8 parametric helpers (PART 190):

  createWallWithApertures()   # PART 192, piecewise sub-wall
  createHingedDoorUnit()      # PART 193, dual-pivot kinematic
  createGlazedWindowUnit()    # PART 194, multi-pane casement
  createPitchedGableRoof()    # PART 195, gable + tile rows
  createLouveredAtticVent()   # PART 196, 4-frame + N-angled-slat
  createPottedPlant()         # PART 197, truncated cone + foliage
  createSteppingStonePath()   # PART 198, quadratic-Bezier + jitter
  materialPalette()           # PART 199.4, 9-key canonical palette

The 11-key STACK_INDEX anti-Z-fighting ladder (PART 191):

  FOUNDATION  (y = 0.000)
  GRAVEL      (y = 0.005)
  PLAINTH     (y = 0.050)
  PORCH_DECK  (y = 0.075)
  DOOR_SILL   (y = 0.110)
  PAVERS      (y = 0.370)
  WALL_FACE   (y = 0.000 to wall_height, x=0)
  WALL_FACING (y = 0.000 to wall_height, x=+0.005)
  WALL_TRIM   (y = 0.000 to wall_height, x=+0.010)
  HARDWARE    (y = local, x=+0.015)
  GLASS       (y = local, z=+0.020)

The 4-mode look-dev lighting (PART 199.3):

  'day'       bright balanced outdoor
  'dusk'      warm orange + cool fill
  'night'     low ambient + blue point lights
  'warmglow'  amber interior glow

The 9-key material palette (PART 199.4):

  stuccoWhite, darkCharcoal, teakWood, roofTile, windowGlass,
  stonePavers, darkMetalTrim, warmTeak, foliageGreen

In v2.0 Python, `lbl.architectural_lights(mode)` returns the lighting
group, and `lbl.build_arch_palette()` returns the 9-key palette.
The other 6 helpers (Wall / Door / Window / Roof / Vent / Plant /
Path) are userland code in `Model_12_House.py`.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 401 — IMAGE-DRIVEN FACTORY DEFAULTS (PART 153-166)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 153-166 codifies what the AI factory SHOULD do when authoring
a model from an image reference (vs from a text prompt):

  PART 153 — Image-driven factory technique defaults
  PART 154 — Auto-trigger ACES (after construction)
  PART 155 — PBR preset table (look up MAT_DB by semantic name)
  PART 156 — Style lock → flat-shading / outline triggers
  PART 157 — imageToWorld helper (pixel → pivot position)
  PART 158 — Silhouette IoU check
  PART 159 — SDF + Marching Cubes trigger (>3 touching parts)
  PART 160 — CSG recipes for common patterns
  PART 161 — Use MT.core.loft (NOT hand-written profileLoft)
  PART 162 — Instanced rivets / repeated details
  PART 163 — Auto-enable PMREM/HDRI for metallic materials
  PART 164 — Detail inventory before building
  PART 165 — 11-step canonical recipe (updated from PART 152)
  PART 166 — Generate 3 variants, pick best (seeds 1, 2, 3)

In v2.0 Python, the most important hooks are:

  lbl.aces_run(car_root)                  # PART 154
  scene.environment = lbl.HDRI(pmrem, "room")  # PART 163
  lbl.install_runtime(root, detail_inventory)   # PART 164
  lbl.preflight_check(root)                     # PART 165

For a 3-variant generation pipeline (PART 166):

  best_iou = 0
  best_root = None
  for seed in (1, 2, 3):
      root = create_apex_horizon_gt(seed=seed, palette="racing-red")
      report = lbl.aces_run(root)
      iou = report.get("iou", 0)
      if iou > best_iou:
          best_iou, best_root = iou, root
  return best_root

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 402 — PERFECT-SHAPE PIPELINE (PART 145-152)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The 8-step perfect-shape pipeline (PART 145):

  STEP 1 — ANALYZE the reference (proportions / palette / parts)
  STEP 2 — SKELETON-FIRST (place joints before any geometry)
  STEP 3 — CROSS-SECTION PROFILES at each joint
  STEP 4 — LOFT between profiles (one mesh per limb)
  STEP 5 — POSITION ACCESSORIES procedurally
  STEP 6 — UNIFORM BEVEL pass (0.02 m chamfer on every solid mesh)
  STEP 7 — RIG + VALIDATE (userData.partName + isPickable)
  STEP 8 — ANIMATE (4 named clips minimum)

The 8 named shape-creation techniques (PART 146):
  146.1 PROFILE LOFTING (lbl.Loft)
  146.2 LATHE GEOMETRY (lbl.Lathe)
  146.3 EXTRUDE GEOMETRY (lbl.Extrude)
  146.4 CSG (lbl.mt.csgSubtract)
  146.5 SDF + MARCHING CUBES (lbl.sdf_box + lbl.sdf_union + sdfMarch)
  146.6 SUBDIVISION SURFACES (lbl.mt.subdivideCatmullClark)
  146.7 NURBS / SPLINES (lbl.mt.makeNurbsCurve + lbl.CatmullRom)
  146.8 PROCEDURAL DEFORMERS (lbl.bend / twist / taper / spherize)

The 10 pitfalls (PART 151):
  1. Visible intersections → use SDF union or shrink 0.01 m
  2. Pivot offset doesn't match mesh length → pivot = bottom of mesh above
  3. getObjectByName returns wrong object → use _mesh / _visual suffix
  4. Track name uses glTF form → 'rotation' → 'quaternion'
  5. Mix tick and mixer.update without coordinating → pick ONE
  6. Single BIG mesh instead of multiple small ones → PART 30.7 contract
  7. Smooth-shaded normals on a low-poly mesh → flatShading: true
  8. Hand-placed dark boxes for seams → striateMap
  9. Glossy plastic look → microRoughnessMap
  10. Animation amplitudes tuned for human scale → chunkier = smaller

The 10-step cheat-sheet (PART 152):
  1. ANATOMY TABLE in code comment
  2. SKELETON FIRST
  3. ONE MESH PER LIMB (profile loft)
  4. CSG FOR RECESSES
  5. UNIFORM BEVEL
  6. MATERIALS (microRoughnessMap + striate + aoMap)
  7. RIG (PART 30 + 30.7 + userData.partName)
  8. ANIMATIONS (4 clips minimum)
  9. VALIDATE (4-angle silhouette + triangle budget)
  10. SHIP (single .py file, 6:3:1 hierarchy, deterministic)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 403 — RENDERER BUG FIXES (ANALYSIS_REPORT [B] [H] [M])
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

ANALYSIS_REPORT.txt (2026-09-14) catalogued 30 issues. v2.0.2
addresses the 6 most important:

  [B] #1 — object.json multi-root
    FIXED: The 'object' skeleton template now has ONE root
    (Bone_Center) with 4 anchor children. lbl.load_skeleton_template
    returns the fixed version.

  [B] #2 — quadruped_light.json height off by 0.335 m
    FIXED: The 'quadruped' skeleton template has `height: 1.115`
    (matching the actual silhouette 0.035 → 1.150 = 1.115 m).

  [B] #7 — ACES extractSkeleton() uses root.THREE which is undefined
    FIXED in renderer: `window.THREE = THREE` is now set after
    the THREE import. v2.0 lbl.aces_run() works in the browser.

  [H] #4 — LBL version stated 4 different ways
    FIXED: All v2.0 docs (lbl helper, Prompt_To_Py.txt, Image_To_Py.txt)
    use the canonical pair "v2.0 / v2.0" (Python path) or
    "v1.36 / v8.27" (TS/JSON reference). The repository's 4-way
    version drift is documented in the spec files for traceability.

  [M] #5 — Models 7, 8, 9 missing `default` export
    FIXED: lbl.add_default_export(model_func) exposes a `create*Model`
    function as both named AND default-style export. Use it at the
    bottom of your factory file.

  [M] #6 — ACES module loading is fragile
    FIXED in renderer: explicit `if (!oklab) { throw new Error(...) }`
    guards. v2.0 lbl.aces_self_test() reports whether the 7 sub-modules
    are wired up correctly.

  [M] #18 — lblCollectAnimations() legacy hint path loses track type
    FIXED: lbl.detect_animation_track_type() detects track type
    from name + values shape. lbl.build_animation_track() builds the
    right track spec. lbl.collect_animation_clips() uses the detection
    for the legacy hint path.

  [L] #23 — lblCollectAnimations() runs the legacy hint path even when
           the real .clips / .mixer path already produced results
    FIXED: lbl.collect_animation_clips() short-circuits the legacy
    hint path when clips already produced results.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 404 — 3D MODELING STYLE DOCTRINE (PART 96-100)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PART 96-100 inlines the anyCreature 3D modeling style guide into
the LBL spec. The 5 PARTs:

  PART 96 — 3D Modeling Style Doctrine
  PART 97 — 3D Spec Language (JSON shape)
  PART 98 — 10 Style Claims
  PART 99 — 4 Join Verbs
  PART 100 — 5-Stage Pipeline

The 5 iron laws (from PART 96):
  1. Form beats obedience, everywhere.
  2. The 6:3:1 hierarchy (60% primary, 30% secondary, 10% tertiary).
  3. Mass thirds (rear / mid / front of the body).
  4. Focal contrast (two focal parts' shares must differ by ≥2×).
  5. The 9-slot brief (mandatory pre-build analysis).

The 9-slot brief (PART 96 / 100):
  1. SILHOUETTE (one clear shape from 4 angles)
  2. PALETTE (3-5 hex codes)
  3. PROPORTION (W:H:D ratios)
  4. PARTS (the 60:30:10 split)
  5. JOIN (how parts meet the body)
  6. MOTION (4 named clips)
  7. MATERIAL (PBR profile)
  8. SCALE (24px reads FEEL, 48px reads IDENTITY, 200px+ reads SURFACE)
  9. CONTEXT (the supporting silhouette)

The 4 join verbs (PART 99):
  - insert (sinks in)
  - extrude (grows out)
  - snap (lies on)
  - place (deliberately detached)

The 5-stage pipeline (PART 100):
  Stage 0 START  — 5 iron laws + 3 discarded ideas + ONE exaggeration
  Stage 1 LOW    — 9-slot brief + 6:3:1 hierarchy + Gate 1 RECOGNISED
  Stage 2 MID    — Edit vocabulary + hands and feet + stage-end gate
  Stage 3 HIGH   — 5 colour norms + 3 canonical anims + materials format
  Stage 4 SHIP   — Red lines + gate stamp + ONE question + closing ledger

The 10 style claims (PART 98, advisory only):
  - value_order
  - contrast_adjacent
  - share_hierarchy
  - focal_contrast
  - saturation_area
  - thinnest_px48
  - sq_fill
  - mirror_sym
  - straight_max
  - tri_budget
  - bright_floor

These are advisory — they never refuse a build. The 15 legality
checks (PART 93) BLOCK the build when geometry is broken.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 405 — 44-TECHNIQUE MODELING BUNDLE (PART 100-143)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

The renderer's `window.MT` bundle is the 44-technique vocabulary.
v2.0.2's `lbl.mt` bridge exposes ~75 helpers (44 in the original
bundle + 6 hardsurface + 8 architectural + 5 textures + 7 highpoly
+ 4 engine dispatcher functions).

The 5 namespaces:

  MT_core           12 techniques  (primitives, bmesh, SDF, CSG, lSystem,
                                     fractals, NURBS, manifold detect)
  MT_mesh            9 techniques  (subdivision, retopo, decimation,
                                     smoothing, sculpt, displacement)
  MT_organic        13 techniques  (SMPL, blendshapes, LBS, DQS,
                                     implicit skin, cage, muscle,
                                     softbody, hair, autoRig)
  MT_materials       6 techniques  (PBR graph, procedural texture, UV
                                     unwrap, bake, paint, atlas pack)
  MT_advanced        4 techniques  (LOD, instanced, geo nodes, physics)
  MT_hardsurface     9+ helpers    (FilletedBox, TaperedTube, StarPattern,
                                     BeveledWasher, LathedTire, SweptTube,
                                     ExhaustCanister, LookDevLights,
                                     FactoryShell, 4 validators)
  MT_architectural   8+ helpers    (Wall, Door, Window, Roof, Vent, Plant,
                                     Path, Palette, LookDevLights 4-mode,
                                     schemaLinter)
  MT_textures        5 helpers     (StyleField, MultiScalePattern,
                                     CavityAwarePattern, GeodesicField,
                                     ProgressiveUV)
  MT_highpoly        8 helpers     (importExternalMesh, AtlasUV,
                                     FastCurvature, highPolyPBR,
                                     photogrammetryPBR, subdivideForHighPoly,
                                     decimateForLowPoly, makeImageTexture)

The engine dispatcher:

  MT.run(name_or_number, target, opts)        # run a single technique
  MT.runStack(techniques, target)             # run a stack in sequence
  MT.applyToScene(target, opts)               # run a pattern across the scene
  MT.selfTest()                               # confirm the bundle is wired

In v2.0 Python:

  lbl.mt.makePrimitive("sphere", {"size": 1, "detail": 2})
  lbl.mt.subdivideCatmullClark(geom, {"levels": 2})
  lbl.mt.autoRig(geom, {"resolution": 16})
  lbl.mt.run("subdivideCatmullClark", geom, {"levels": 2})
  lbl.mt.runStack(["subdivideCatmullClark", "taubinSmooth"], geom)
  lbl.mt.selfTest()  # returns {"ok": True, "ran": 44, "errors": []}

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 408 — UPDATED COMMON MISTAKES (v2.0.2)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

In addition to the v2.0 common mistakes in PART 378, v2.0.2
adds:

  DO NOT use lbl.Cylinder() for vehicle wheels. Use lbl.LathedTire()
  (PART 396) or lbl.Lathe() with a 7-point profile.

  DO NOT use lbl.FilletedBox() for fuel tanks, fairings, or visors.
  Use lbl.bezier_fairing() (PART 395) for organic exterior panels.

  DO NOT use plain lbl.Sphere() for windscreens. Use the
  egg-visor fix (PART 397) with transmission material + rake +
  perimeter gasket.

  DO NOT chain lbl.aces_run() before the ACES sub-modules have
  loaded. Call lbl.aces_self_test() first to confirm.

  DO NOT pass a raw list of joint dicts to lbl.skeleton() without
  a 'parent' key. Use lbl.load_skeleton_template() to get a
  known-good template, then modify the joints.

  DO NOT use the legacy-hint animation path (plain
  [{name, duration, tracks:[…]}]) when you have real
  AnimationClips. The renderer short-circuits the hint path
  in that case (ANALYSIS_REPORT #23).

  DO NOT parent sockets (Socket_Headlight, Socket_ExhaustTip, etc.)
  to root. Use lbl.validate_socket_attachments() to check (PART 393).

  DO NOT use the default-export-only pattern. Always provide BOTH
  the named export (`createXxxModel`) AND the default export
  (use lbl.add_default_export()) for portability with the TS path.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 409 — UPDATED RUNTIME ERROR REFERENCE (v2.0.2)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

In addition to the v2.0 errors in PART 379, v2.0.2 adds:

  "skeleton has 4 root joints" → The 'object' template fixed in
    v2.0.2 has 1 root. If you built a custom skeleton, ensure
    EXACTLY ONE joint has parent=None. Use lbl.list_skeleton_classes()
    to get a known-good starting point.

  "rig must have one root joint" → Same as above.

  "Character rig must have exactly one root, got 4" → Same as above.

  "Cannot destructure property 'lin2oklab' of 'oklab' as it is
   null" → The ACES module loading order is wrong. Call
    lbl.aces_self_test() to confirm the 7 sub-modules are wired.
    If not, the renderer needs to re-load aces-oklab.js first.

  "setFromMatrixPosition is not a function" → The ACES
    extractSkeleton() needs `window.THREE` to be set. The renderer
    sets it after the THREE import; if you're using lbl.aces_run()
    outside the renderer context, this is expected.

  "track name uses glTF form" → Use lbl.detect_animation_track_type()
    to detect whether your track is 'number' / 'vector' / 'quaternion'
    based on name + values length. Then build the right track class.

  "coplanar/overlap candidate" → lbl.schema_linter() reports this
    when two meshes share a 5mm world-space grid key. Stagger them
    by 0.005 m or move them apart.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 410 — v2.0.2 SUMMARY (20 RULES)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

For the AI prompt author, here is the absolute minimum you need to
know about v2.0.2 (combined with v2.0 from PART 385):

  1. Use Style B (THREE.Group return) -- NOT Style A.
  2. Use the 10-slot palette system (lbl.build_materials + setPalette).
  3. Use solid PBR materials -- NO CanvasTexture, NO document.createElement.
  4. Use separate Mesh layers for patterns (stripes, decals).
  5. Set userData.sculptRuntime via lbl.install_runtime.
  6. Set userData.tick for any animation.
  7. Set userData.setPalette + availablePalettes for hot-swap.
  8. Use the new custom shape helpers (FilletedBox, LathedTire, etc.)
  9. Use rigging helpers (skeleton, skinned_mesh) for characters.
  10. Use animation helpers (build_walk_clip, damp_spring, mixer).
  11. Run preflight_check before returning.
  12. NEVER use color=0xffffff as a texture placeholder.
  13. Use lbl.load_skeleton_template() to get a known-good skeleton
      before customising (PART 387).
  14. Call lbl.aces_run() AFTER the model is built, BEFORE shipping
      (PART 386). Check the report and fix BLOCK warnings.
  15. For vehicles, follow the Hierarchy of Fidelity (PART 394):
      silhouette > compound contour > PBR > skeleton > minification.
  16. For tires, use lbl.LathedTire() — NEVER plain Cylinder
      (PART 396).
  17. For fuel tanks / fairings / visors, use lbl.bezier_fairing()
      — NEVER plain BoxGeometry or FilletedBox (PART 395).
  18. For windscreens, follow the egg-visor fix (PART 397) with
      transmission material + 40-55° rake + perimeter gasket.
  19. Parent sockets to their operational anchor (PART 393).
      Use lbl.validate_socket_attachments() to check.
  20. Use lbl.add_default_export() to expose BOTH named AND default
      exports (PART 403).

If you remember these 20 rules, you'll generate v2.0.2-compatible
Python factories. If you also use the worked examples in
Model_10.py / Model_11_Character.py / Model_12_House.py as patterns,
you'll match the TypeScript output quality.

Companion files (v2.0.2):
  • public/modeling/lbl_py.py — 5,700+ lines of v2.0.2 surface
  • public/models/Model_10.py — Apex Horizon GT supercar
  • public/models/Model_11_Character.py — rigged character
  • public/models/Model_12_House.py — procedural cottage

The build + patch tools (only when re-building the inlined source):
  • public/modeling/build_inlined.py — regenerates the inlined JS helper
  • public/modeling/patch_index.py — patches index.html with new source

The standalone test suite:
  • public/modeling/test_integration.py — 8 integration tests

The release notes:
  • CHANGES_v2_0.md — full v2.0.0 changelog
  • (this file) PART 386-410 — v2.0.2 patch changelog

END OF PART 386-410 (v2.0.2 patch documentation)

PART 416 — v2.0.2 PATCH NOTES (IMAGE-TO-PYTHON)
================================================================================

The v2.0.2 release (paired with Prompt_To_Py.txt PART 411-416) brings
the image-driven Python factory pipeline up to parity with the new
lbl_py.py SECTION 22 surface. Image-driven factories MUST now use:

  • lbl.aces_run(group)              — after the model is built
  • lbl.system_capabilities()        — discover what's available
  • lbl.sport_motorcycle_lights(mode) — for sport-motorcycle images
  • lbl.architectural_lights(mode)   — for building images
  • lbl.schema_linter(group)         — verify detailInventory coverage
  • lbl.make_sculpt_runtime(spec)    — build the 9-key runtime contract
  • lbl.install_set_palette(group, [...]) — palette hot-swap

For image-driven factories specifically, v2.0.2 adds:

  PART 416.1 — imageToWorld mapping now uses lbl.compute_silhouette_iou
               as a 3-variant ranking helper (PART 166 + PART 254).
  PART 416.2 — IoU grading is now exposed as lbl.compute_silhouette_iou
               (returns 0.0 in headless; browser returns real number).
  PART 416.3 — color sampling is unchanged but the 6-band palette can
               be derived with lbl.make_style_field_palette(prompt) when
               the user supplies free-text cues alongside the image.
  PART 416.4 — proportion declaration is unchanged (PART 49.1 contract).

The standalone test suite grew from 8 to 17 tests. Run it with
`python3 test_integration.py` after upgrading lbl_py.py.

END OF PART 416 (v2.0.2 patch notes)

IMPORTANT — THINGS IN THE SYSTEM NOT IN PRIOR SPEC FILES
================================================================================

This section catalogs 25 capabilities the repo has but the spec docs
don't (yet) document. Added in the v2.0.2 / v1.37 / v8.28 sync pass.

These are real renderer capabilities (verified against index.html,
public/aces/, public/modeling/, public/rig/, public/models/) that the
AI factory author should know about but couldn't find in the spec.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1.  Mockup Generator sibling (mockup-generator.html, 84 KB) — the
    renderer has a sibling HTML file that pre-loads the model store.
    Python factories don't depend on it but the renderer does.
    Spec gap: NONE of the 8 spec files document it.

2.  ACES load order is strict — aces-oklab → aces-normals → aces-ao →
    aces-shade → aces-checks → aces-bones → aces-engine. Reordering
    crashes the app because of top-level destructure. Spec gap: only
    PART 386 names the modules; load order is in a source comment only.

3.  Eager MT/ACES import (430 KB) — index.html eagerly imports all
    7 ACES + 7 MT modules at startup. The modules are only used when
    the user clicks ACES/MT toolbar buttons. Lazy-import would shave
    ~430 KB off first-page (ANALYSIS_REPORT #24). Spec gap: PART 386 /
    405 don't mention the trade-off.

4.  Three.js addon classes — the renderer mirrors addon classes
    (RoundedBoxGeometry, TransformControls, GLTFExporter, etc.) into
    the `window` namespace so user code can do
    `from js import RoundedBoxGeometry` in Pyodide. Spec gap: PART
    203 mentions `from js import THREE` but doesn't enumerate the
    addon classes.

6.  _stripEmptyBufferViewsFromGLB is a 170-line scrubber fixing a
    GLTFExporter bug (empty bufferViews). Spec gap: never mentioned
    in the 8 spec files.

7.  Animation track-type loss (ANALYSIS_REPORT #18) — the legacy
    hint path loses the original VectorKeyframeTrack /
    QuaternionKeyframeTrack type. v2.0.2 lbl.detect_animation_track_type
    / build_animation_track / collect_animation_clips fix it on the
    Python side; the renderer itself still doesn't call them yet.
    Spec gap: PART 389 documents detection but the renderer wiring
    is still TODO.

8.  Skeleton JSON files still buggy — public/rig/skeletons/object.json
    still has 4 root joints and public/rig/specs/quadruped_light.json
    still declares height 1.45 m (actual 1.115 m). The in-Python
    lbl.load_skeleton_template returns FIXED templates; the
    renderer's disk-loaded templates are still buggy. Spec gap:
    PART 387 documents the fix in the inlined helper but doesn't
    say "the JSON files in public/rig/skeletons/ are still buggy;
    use lbl.load_skeleton_template instead".

9.  Pyodide version pinning (Pyodide 0.26.2 is pinned in index.html
    line 13842). Newer Pyodide versions may break the `from js
    import THREE` bridge or the import map. Spec gap: not mentioned.

10. Undo/redo (60-step via buildScene wrapper). Spec gap: not
    mentioned in any of the 8 files.

11. 36 anti-pattern validators (E1..E36) + 9 fidelity-gate checks
    (STYLE / CATEGORY / COLOR-SILHOUETTE / DETAIL COUNT / METALLIC-
    PRESENCE / EMISSIVE-PRESENCE / STYLE-COHERENT-MATERIALS).
    Spec gap: only PART 174 (Python form) names a few — the full
    list is never documented.

12. 4 lighting presets (Studio / Outdoor / Night / Flat) — toolbar
    cycle button. Spec gap: not mentioned.

13. 2 outline modes ('backface' default + 'screenspace' depth+
    normal edge detect, ~50 lines of GLSL). Spec gap: not mentioned.

14. Per-style tone-mapping (NoToneMapping for voxel/toon/flat,
    ACESFilmic for realistic, Cineon for stylized/chibi, Linear for
    painterly). Spec gap: not mentioned.

15. Modular socket kitbash (PART 33.1[7]). Spec gap: not mentioned.

16. FSM (animation state machine) inspector. Spec gap: not mentioned.

17. 18-render multi-view validation grid (PART 44.7). Spec gap: not
    mentioned.

18. LOD chain generation (5-LOD chain, displayed in inspector).
    Spec gap: not mentioned.

19. T-pose, mesh cleanup, eye-tracking helpers. Spec gap: not
    mentioned.

20. 3 export formats (GLB, OBJ, STL). Spec gap: not mentioned.

21. GLB-with-rigged-skeleton path (exportRiggedModelToGlb in
    Model_7). Spec gap: not mentioned.

22. Group / ungroup / combine operations (group-pivot wrap). Spec
    gap: not mentioned.

23. Face-texture editor (per-face UV/transform). Spec gap: not
    mentioned.

24. Texture-positioning tool (drag/scale on viewport). Spec gap: not
    mentioned.

25. Runtime hook contract — userData.sculptRuntime (PART 207) +
    userData.tick (per-frame lambda) + userData.isPickable = true +
    userData.partName on every mesh. Spec gap: PART 207 documents
    sculptRuntime + tick but doesn't enumerate isPickable / partName.

26. TypeScript transpilation via ts.transpileModule() in-browser —
    no external TS server. Spec gap: not mentioned in
    Prompt_To_Ts.txt.

27. MODELING_TECHNIQUES_v143.md is the canonical 44-technique annex
    for PART 100-143. Spec gap: PART 405 mentions it but never
    publishes its 44 helper names.

28. EXAMPLES_v8_17.md (filename stale — see #29) has worked
    examples. Spec gap: filename out of sync with the spec version.

29. EXAMPLES_v8_17.md is named after v8.17 but the spec is now at
    v8.28 — the file should be renamed EXAMPLES_v8_28.md. Spec gap:
    filename drift.

30. ANALYSIS_REPORT.txt (71 KB, 2026-09-14) is the canonical
    renderer+ACES+MT audit. Lists 30 issues with severity, solution,
    and recommended fix. Spec gap: PART 200-302 references the
    [B] / [H] / [M] / [L] tags but never publishes the full list.

31. SYSTEM_UPGRADE_SPECIFICATION.txt — referenced from PART 179 but
    not in the repo (was the diagnostic that drove the v1.33 fix).

32. CHANGES_v130.md / v132 / v133 / v134 / v135 — the version
    changelogs. Spec gap: only referenced from the version header.

33. SPEC target advertised 4 different version numbers across the
    repo (ANALYSIS_REPORT #4). The v2.0.2 / v1.37 / v8.28 sync
    pass brings all 8 spec files to ONE consistent canonical version
    per pair (v8.28 / v1.37 for JS+TS+JSON; v2.0.2 for Python).
